*The music ends. The cassette clicks to a stop.*
*I’ve listened to it so many times I’ve even memorized the placement of the static from the songs’ original broadcasts.*
*I’m worried the deck might eventually eat the tape, but it never happened to him no matter how many times he played the damn thing, so I’m not too scared.*
*It used to drive me crazy.*
*Every day he played it over and over again.*
*Sure, he would throw on something different after a few loops, but soon enough he'd switch back and the cycle would repeat itself.*
*Sometimes, I think he would do it just to mess with me.*
*I would be in the back, in bed, smothering a pillow over my head to block out the music. He would just turn it up louder. Even during all-nighters.*
*That was so like him.*
*Even near the end, when we had to finally settle down, he was still pulling that kind of crap.*
*I’d come home from school sometimes and the police would be at the door because the entire block had called in a noise complaint. He said it was because he was hard of hearing, but I know he did it just to get a rise out of the old grouches who lived nearby.*
*I think that was his worst fear—ending up just another bitter sack of bones. So he did everything in his power to be the exact opposite, right up until the end.*
*Of course, that meant I would always deal with the fallout.*
*I can’t imagine what the neighbors thought, seeing a little girl go door to door, apologizing because some old man decided it would be funny to put plastic flamingos all over their award winning lawns.*
*I can’t help but smile, just a little. Even if it does hurt.*
*I take a sip of my coffee. Outside, a heat haze shimmers over the never-ending oceans of sand.*
*So much desert.*
*Even Roswell was just rock, sand, and a few shacks. All alien-themed, of course.*
*Didn’t see any UFOs, though.*
*I’m not used to how dry everything is. Even with the AC on high enough to rip off my eyebrows, I’m still all sweaty.*
*Guess that’s what happens when you laze around in snowy Colorado for a decade.*
*In that regard, the desert is a nice change of scenery. I don’t exactly hate it.*
*New sights are nice. Especially since his health didn’t allow him to do a lot of traveling when I was a little girl.*
*I could see myself getting used to this.*
*Well, maybe in another fifteen years when I have everything paid off…*
*Agh, dammit, Amber. Snap out of it. You can worry about that later. This isn’t your trip, so stop being so selfish.*
*I push the eject button.*
*I take the tape out and look it over. Probably for the twentieth time today.*
*Years' worth of brown stains and greasy fingerprints are smudged all over, making it feel grimy to the touch. It still works like a charm, though.*
*There are more cassettes in the glove compartment. A mixture of mine and his. Mostly his.*
*I should just play one of those and get it over with.*
*My hand gravitates towards the latch.*
*It would be okay… Right?*
*No.*
*I snatch my hand away from the latch and flip the tape over.*
*Once again, I find myself staring.*
*With a sigh, I stick it back into the deck. I reach towards the play button, taking another look to see if the road is still clear.*
*Like always, it—*
*Isn’t?*
*I slam on the brakes, their shriek piercing my skull. A glance at my rearview mirror shows a girl cringing next to an ancient looking car, covering her ears as I screech down the road.*
*Making a U-turn of dubious legality, I pull up in front of her car, killing the motorhome’s engine.*
*I hop out and shield my eyes from the blazing New Mexico sun. Only now do I realize how precious the AC really is.*
*It doesn’t take more than several steps before the girl comes bouncing up to me with a look of relief.*
Girl: "Oh my god, thank you thank you thank you thank you! I was starting to think no one would ever stop!"
*With each word she gets a little closer, until we’re finally within touching distance of each other, her smiling face now filling my entire field of vision.*
*Her bright auburn hair is done into a girlish sidetail, glistening beneath specks of sweat. Standing out in the heat has made her round, pale cheeks a sun kissed red. But she still looks put together, like the desert would sooner melt her than do any real damage to her looks.*
*I almost feel embarrassed to be in front of someone who dresses so cutely, her frilly green top and skirt putting my red t-shirt and khakis to shame.*
*It’s all brought together by one thing, though. Her eyes. They’re blue—vividly blue, deep and rich, complementing the traces of her misty perfume as she gets closer and closer.*
*Too close too close too close.*
*I scramble back and cough, trying to hide any awkwardness.*
*What’s she doing out here alone? Driving through? Hell, is she even alone?*
*I peer off into the plains on the side of the road, half expecting a guy to pop up from his hiding place now that some idiot has fallen for their trap.*
*That idiot being me, of course.*
*But, much to my surprise, no one does. Instead, the girl continues to stare at me, smiling as she patiently waits for my response.*
*I glance behind her, focusing on her car.*
Amber: "So, what’s the issue? Your car break down?"
Girl: "I think so, yeah."
Amber: "Stopped?"
Girl: "Yeah…"
*She lowers her head in shame, eyes fixated on her feet, kicking the dust around her.*
Amber: "I'm not much of a car buff, but I can take a look at it."
*Her head pops back up and life returns to her eyes.*
Girl: "Oh wow, could you?"
Amber: "Yeah. Can’t promise that I can do anything, but I can at least check it out."
Girl: "That’d be great!"
Amber: "Alright, first things first, then."
*This thing looks even older than my motorhome. It was probably a nice car once upon a time, but with a dented grill, multiple scratches, and faded murky green color, it’s hard to believe such a cute girl was driving it.*
*I slide into the fabric seat and start the ignition. I don’t know too much about how this stuff works, just enough to keep the motorhome from giving out, but I can’t imagine it’s too significant of an issue. Probably just a dead battery or—*
*No gas.*
*A single car passes by, cooling us with a refreshing gust of wind, sprinkled with dirt and sand.*
Girl: "I-Is something wrong?"
*Is this a joke?*
Girl: "Oh no! Did I break it?"
Amber: "Uhh, no, it’s just out of gas."
Girl: "What, no way!"
Girl: "Dad said I should've filled up before I left…"
*Is she for real? Why is this girl in the middle of nowhere by herself?*
*As if trying to force all the ideas into the center of her brain, the girl grabs her head and whines under her breath.*
Girl: "Ah man, and I didn’t charge my phone, either. What am I gonna do?"
*I’d offer her mine, but I’ve never had a cellphone in my life.*
*Seems like you'd have to be pretty absentminded to forget that, though.*
*She whines once more and turns to me.*
Girl: "Hey, this might seem a little weird…"
*Oh no.*
Girl: "But could you take me somewhere nearby so I can get some gas?"
*And what do you know? She actually did it!*
Amber: "How long did you say you’ve been stuck out here?"
Girl: "Hmm. I don’t know… A few hours maybe?"
Girl: "I’ve been trying to wave down everyone who drives by, but you were the first to stop."
Amber: "Hold on. Everyone?"
Girl: "Yup!"
Amber: "That’s probably not a good idea, you know."
*She cocks her head to the side and gives me a look of genuine curiosity. Like it wasn’t obvious or something.*
Girl: "Huh, why?"
Amber: "Well, you know…"
Amber: "A cute, young girl alone in the middle of the desert... It’s just asking for trouble."
Girl: "But you’re a cute, young girl alone in the middle of the desert."
Amber: "Wh-What?"
*Her comeback takes me off guard and I turn away a little, avoiding eye contact.*
Amber: "Yeah, but that’s different…"
*A smile returns to her face, the same bright one that she showed me when I first pulled over.*
*I’m still avoiding eye contact, but she leans over to the direction I’m looking and continues to smile.*
Girl: "So do you think you can give me a ride?"
Amber: "I don’t know…"
Girl: "Pleeeease. It shouldn’t take very long."
*This girl, it’s like she didn’t even listen to what I said.*
*Still, better me than some psycho.*
*Besides, it’s not like a small detour will make me late or anything.*
Amber: "I guess it’s fine."
Amber: "I’m not in too big of a rush, anyway."
Girl: "Yay!"
*Before I’m even finished, she’s springing off the ground, bouncing to the motorhome.*
Amber: "Hey, wait!"
*I heave a heavy sigh, rubbing my temple as I climb back up into the driver’s seat.*
*The key is still dangling in the ignition. I wrench it around and the motorhome roars to life, forcing a surprised squeak out of the girl.*
Girl: "By the way, my name is Marina. Thank you sooooo much for helping me out!"
Amber: "Amber. Don’t mention it."
*Careful not to hit her car, I back the RV up and turn it around, sending us in my original direction.*
*I reach for the play button on the cassette player, finally giving in to temptation, but Marina starts up again.*
Marina: "Why aren’t we going the other way? Wasn’t there a station a little ways back?"
Amber: "It was more than a ‘little ways’ back."
Amber: "I saw a sign a few miles ago that said there was a station not too far from here, so we’re going there."
Marina: "Oh wow, I didn’t even see that. You’re really observant!"
*Yeah, or you’re just really oblivious.*
Amber: "Not really."
*Silence falls between us, the roaring of the engine and swishing of the wind from outside being the only sounds.*
*I take that as my cue and reach for the cassette player once more, finally giv—*
Marina: "How do you even drive this thing? It’s huuuuge!"
*Marina spreads her arms out like an eagle unfurling its wings, almost bopping me on the head in the process.*
*I clear my throat and grab her arm out from the air, gently placing it on her lap.*
Amber: "It’s not that bad. You get used to it."
*She tilts her head to the side and gives a light grunt as if saying "that so" and we return to the silence of before.*
*Again, I reach for the cassette play—*
Marina: "So what are you doing out here? Are you going camping? Seems like what you’re doing is fun."
*I take a deep breath and hold it on as long as possible before letting it out.*
Amber: "Just traveling."
Amber: "What about you? Why are you out here?"
Marina: "Oh. Well... It’s kind of silly."
*She scratches her head and lets out an embarrassed laugh before staring at her feet. She quiets down, and surprisingly, stays quiet. *
*Well, looks like that worked.*
*I reach for the cassette player one last time. I stop for a moment to look at the glove compartment and then Marina, who looks lost in thought.*
*Finally, I press play.*
*A pleasant little chime greets me as I enter the station, Marina trailing closely behind.*
*The drive was quiet after I turned the music on. Turning the tables on her and asking what she was doing out there seemed to throw her off.*
*I didn’t mean to come off as cold as I did.*
*It’s just been ages since I’ve had a conversation beyond accepting some half-hearted condolences. Though to be fair, I haven’t really wanted to hold one.*
*The guy behind the counter is busy ringing someone up, so I set out in search of a gas can.*
*The store is one of those big mini-mart places, made specifically for travelers. It’s surprisingly clean for a place surrounded by sand. Every corner has an unnatural, sanitary shine accompanied by the scent of citrus cleaning product.*
Marina: "Hey, they have the cola flavor here!"
*I turn and find Marina clutching the slushie machine, her eyes widening like a cat’s when tempted with yarn.*
Amber: "Don’t most gas stations have it?"
*She shakes her head, but doesn't take her eyes off of the machine.*
Marina: "Uh-uh."
Marina: "All the places back home only had cherry and raspberry. When we got sick of them, we just had to mix them together."
*Marina finally turns to me, wearing a face of pure, concentrated sadness.*
*It’s the saddest thing I’ve ever seen. Just looking at her, I can feel my heart brea—wait a second.*
Amber: "How were you expecting to pay for the gas?"
Marina: "I-I don’t know. I just remembered."
*How did she last five minutes out in the desert?*
Amber: "Fine, I guess I’ll pay for it."
Marina: "Really?"
Amber: "Yeah, yeah. And grab yourself one of those slushies, too."
Amber: "But make it a small one."
*Her pure, concentrated sadness melts into pure, concentrated happiness.*
Marina: "You’re the best, Amber!"
*She grabs a cup and holds down the button until the slushie overflows and drips down the side.*
*I was going to pay for the gas anyway, but I’m hoping the drink makes up for the drive over.*
*I spot the gas cans, and with Marina happily sipping her drink, grab one and head for the counter.*
*The guy at the register is still ringing up the same girl. Lines of flimsy plastic bags line the counter, filled with a mixture of off-brand junk food and gardening tools.*
*He scans the items as the girl digs through her wallet, her bandana-wrapped head pressing a phone against her shoulder.*
Bandana Girl: "Aren't you supposed to be the badass mechanic? What good are you if you're not?"
*She swipes out a card and hands it to the cashier, more interested in staring at her nails than looking at him.*
Bandana Girl: "How the hell would I know? Have the squirt check underneath it if you're so curious."
*On the other end of the line, I can hear the faintest of squabbling. I can't make out anything they're saying, but the tone sounds worlds more tolerable than the girl's.*
Bandana Girl: "If it's that old then I say do it. Cash is cash. It's not like anyone's gonna care."
Bandana Girl: "You better not take as long as last time though! I’ll be waiting for you slackers outside."
*The girl slips her phone back into the crevices of her dangerously unzipped jumpsuit. What little clothing she has on underneath looks like it's going to burst from the slightest movement.*
*Not my type, especially with that personality, but it's hard for me to do anything other than stare.*
*The attendant reaches out to help with the bags, but the girl snaps them away before he can. She shoots him with a heated glare that burns straight through the man and into the wall behind him.*
*Turning around, the girl looks at us for a second before rolling her eyes, then marches out the door, the bags weighing her down as they nearly scrape the ground.*
*Yeah, definitely not my type.*
Attendant: "Good luck!"
*Marina and I set the can and nearly empty slushie on the counter.*
Attendant: "Will that be all?"
Amber: "Yeah. Sorry if we're more boring than that other girl."
*The attendant hacks out a dry cackle and scans the gas can. He flicks his oily ponytail behind his shoulder, and then rings up Marina's drink.*
Attendant: "Been a lot of folks passin' through and stockin' up the past few days."
Attendant: "It's been a good change of pace."
Attendant: "I’m guessin’ that’s why you girls are here too?"
*Marina eases in towards the counter, nudging herself tightly between me and a rack, her hands creeping over to the slushie.*
Amber: "...Treasure?"
*The clerk's chubby face morphs into a grin.*
Attendant: "Ya telling me that you girls haven’t heard of the treasure and the journal?"
Marina: "I have!"
*I frown. Embarrassed, Marina looks at the floor again, her cheeks flushing red.*
*I guess she's always just this forward.*
Marina: "Well, I heard about it on the news."
Marina: "It's kind of why I drove down here..."
Amber: "You drove out into the middle of the desert for... treasure?"
Amber: "Sorry, but you’ve lost me."
Attendant: "Not just any treasure, missy—the Miner’s Treasure!"
*He says it like that should clarify what he meant.*
*I stare at him.*
*He stares back.*
*Marina sips the last dregs of her slushie.*
Attendant: "Ya honestly don’t know?"
Amber: "Not a clue. Are you gonna change that?"
Attendant: "Lil' feisty, ain't ya?"
Amber: "Not really. Just have stuff to do."
Attendant: "Fair 'nuff."
Attendant: "A little while back, some folks were going through all the trash in their garage."
Attendant: "As fate would have it, they came across an old journal."
Attendant: "Turns out, the thing belonged to their great uncle—or maybe it was their great grandfather—I don’t know. Some dead old ma-"
*I cut him off with a cough, a polite hint for him to either get on with it or quit his yammering.*
Attendant: "Sorry, I’ll try keepin’ it short."
Attendant: "Anyway, after flippin’ through it, they found out that he'd struck it rich during the gold rush."
Amber: "If he "struck it rich" then wouldn’t the family know about it? They’d have to be pretty well off, right?"
Marina: "Oh, I can answer that!"
Marina: "He went back home to Missouri without any gold and told all his friends he hid it!"
*By now, the slushie is all gone. Marina tries for one last sip, but all that's sucked through the straw is air. She peers down into the cup and blows out a small sigh.*
Marina: "No one believed him, though..."
*I raise an eyebrow. If even this girl knows about it then I must be more out of the loop than I thought.*
Marina: "Mhm!"
*The attendant leans into the counter and smirks.*
Attendant: "Story goes, he wanted to eventually move his family out West and dig his gold back up along the way, but kicked it before he could."
*He slides his thumb along his neck and makes a cheesy sound effect to go with it. Marina giggles, but I just keep staring.*
Attendant: "The folks that found the journal ended up selling it to some hotshot publishin’ house. Now everyone wants to retrace his steps and find the treasure."
Attendant: "Shipments have been sellin' like hotcakes. Just got ours last week."
*He points to a rack of identical books sitting by the door. A sign hanging above it reads Treasure of the Desert: A Miner’s Journey - Take Part in the New Gold Rush!*
*Yeah, that’ll stick in people’s heads.*
Amber: "And people are really buying into this?"
Attendant: "Sure are. I’ve worked here for over twenty years, and never have I seen this many people passing through."
Attendant: "Even the banshee before you was here for some supplies and a journal."
*Sure, okay. This is a hoax if I’ve ever seen one.*
Attendant: "No one knows."
Attendant: "It’s all scattered around the desert. They say the journal has hints to its whereabouts."
Amber: "Uh-huuuuh."
Amber: "Marina, you said this is why you drove out here?"
Marina: "Yep! That’s right!"
Amber: "But you don’t really believe the treasure exists, do you?"
Marina: "Sure I do!"
*She pauses to think.*
Marina: "At least, I want to."
Marina: "But wouldn't it be super cool if there was treasure?"
Amber: "Sure it would be cool…"
Marina: "No... Not really. I never got that far. I don't even have a copy of the journal."
*She lowers her head, like she knows there's a good chance what I'm saying is true.*
Marina: "But I have to try."
*Her voice is soft and quiet, but there's a faint glow of determination behind it. It makes me feel ashamed for scolding, but the idea that she dropped everything for treasure is insane.*
*To her credit, she did "a little research," but that's cancelled out by driving into the desert without a full tank of gas. She wasn't prepared at all.*
*The people who care about her must be worried sick. I barely even know her and I'm worried.*
Attendant: "Aww, don’t be too hard on her, missy. After all, who can resist a good treasure hunt?"
*He looks to Marina and gives a big gummy grin, courtesy of his missing teeth.*
Attendant: "Ain’t that right, sister?"
*Marina springs back with a smile of her own.*
Marina: "That’s right!"
*Rolling my eyes, I reach into my pocket and pass some crumpled bills to the clerk.*
Amber: "Yeah, well, I’ll believe when I see it."
*He shrugs and stuffs the cash in the register, though now I’m just paying for the tank and an empty cup. I pocket the change.*
*I give a casual wave from the exit—that should be a good enough thank you.*
*Marina follows, though a bit more animated, waving her arm like a fan as she says goodbye.*
Attendant: "Oh, and sister!"
Marina: "Yeah?"
Attendant: "You go ahead and take one of them journals."
Attendant: "Free of charge."
Marina: "Really?"
*Marina lights up like a firecracker and bounces up and down like she's a kid again.*
Attendant: "Yup!"
Marina: "Oh wow, thanks, mister!"
*In one swift motion, she swipes one of the remaining books off the rack so fast that I swear my eyes are skipping frames.*
Marina: "Hey Amber, look. He gave me one for free!"
Amber: "Yeah, yeah, I see it…"
*I tune out her chattering and nod as we walk out to the parking lot so we can finally get the fuel.*
*The attendant shouts one last thing, his voice fading behind the glass of the closing door.*
Attendant: "Good luck on your trip!"
*...*
*Desert people are weird.*
Amber: "No way."
Marina: "Maybe... it’s further up the road?"
Amber: "I can’t believe it. It’s gone."
Marina: "We could have just passed it."
Amber: "We weren’t even away that long! How can it be gone?"
*We stare at the empty space that once housed a certain ugly green car.*
*I look in every conceivable direction, but there’s nothing, nothing but an ocean of sand and a never ending stretch of road.*
*Was it towed?*
*No. Not out here, not this quick.*
Marina: "Yeah, that is kind of weird, isn’t it?"
*Marina’s hand rests against her cheek as she looks on with only mild confusion, like she’s trying to solve a small mystery.*
Marina: "Well, it’s actually my big brother’s car. He just lent it to me when he left for college."
Amber: "Oh, well that makes everything better, then."
Marina: "Really?"
Amber: "No!" with hpunch
*I let out a heavy sigh and press my fingers to my temple, attempting to massage away the stress and irritation.*
Amber: "It was probably stolen. We’ll need to call the police."
Amber: "You have a phone, right?"
Marina: "I do, but…"
*Her voice trails off a cliff. What she said earlier echoes in my head.*
Amber: "That’s right, you said it was dead."
Amber: "And I’m guessing you left it in your car?"
Marina: "Yeah..."
Amber: "Well that’s just great."
Marina: "Don’t you have one?"
Amber: "Nope. Never needed one."
Marina: "Afraid no one would call you?"
Amber: "Not quite."
*I shield my eyes and look up at the dry sky. Its color has shifted to a glowing orange as sunset begins to settle over the distant valley peaks.*
*It’s going to get dark pretty soon.*
Marina: "Don’t worry, Amber. I would call you!"
*I can’t just leave her.*
*Even if she did have her phone, it would take forever before anyone could get out here. She’d go from burning up in the heat to freezing out in the cold within a few hours.*
*Plus, if there are people around who would steal cars, then there’s no telling who else is out here.*
*I can already see how all this would go down. She would be huddled on the side of the road, trying to keep warm. A pack of coyotes would be howling all around her, closing in. Then, from down the road, a starved creep would pull up in a trashy old pickup truck, open his door for her, and…*
Amber: "Hey, where’re you from, if you don’t mind me asking?"
Marina: "Oh, I’m from Carlsbad."
*She pauses for a moment.*
Marina: "Or at least kinda close to it. It’s a little out of the way."
Amber: "Carlsbad? You’re from California?"
Marina: "No, silly. Carlsbad, New Mexico."
Amber: "Oh, uh, right."
*Had me going there for a second.*
Amber: "How far’s that?"
Amber: "Wow, you didn’t get very far at all, did you?"
*Marina lowers her head in shame.*
Marina: "I never really seem to."
Amber: "Hey, listen… You don’t live too far away. If you want, I can probably give you a lift back home."
*I prep myself for an explosion of thankful energy and excitement.*
*But there isn't one.*
*Instead she continues to stare at the ground, her hands now balled up in a fist as she fidgets ever so slightly.*
Amber: "Something wrong?"
*She shifts her gaze from the ground and looks me straight in the eyes, drawing a deep, long breath.*
Marina: "Remember the treasure we were talking about at the store?"
Amber: "Yeah."
Marina: "I know it’s a lot to ask, and you’ve already helped me out so much already."
Marina: "But do you think you could take me the first place the treasure is?"
Amber: "Huh?"
*She wants to keep going? Even after this entire disaster?*
Amber: "I heard ya the first time!"
Amber: "It’s just…"
Amber: "You don’t even know if this thing is real, but you’ve dropped everything to go look for it."
Amber: "And now you’re asking me, someone you’ve just met out in the middle of the desert, to take you to it."
Amber: "Did I catch all that?"
Marina: "Uh-huh."
*Shameless.*
*I’m at a loss for words.*
*How do I even respond to that?*
*I try to recollect my thoughts, but only one word comes to mind:*
Amber: "Why?"
*Marina shrugs.*
*Nothing more than that. She just shrugs.*
*I take a look at her—a good look. Her wavy hair has been dried out by the hot desert air. Her face is red and drenched in sweat, probably close to melting off her makeup. Even her nice clothes are a little ruffled.*
*But despite all that, this cute, peppy girl wants to keep pressing on.*
*Even when most people would have just called it quits after the car broke down.*
*Even out here all by herself.*
*For all I know, she could be some weirdo out to mug me and this is all some elaborate scheme.*
*Probably not.*
*But she’s still asking. Why should I say no? I don’t have to be in California for another few weeks, and isn’t that why I’m taking this trip? To see the sights, for him.*
*She’s a little excitable, and even more naive, but maybe I need a little bit of that. It’s a lot better than just being out here all by myself with nothing but the ghosts of my memories to keep me company. Even if it’s just for a little while.*
Amber: "Eh, why the hell not? Hop in."
Marina: "For real?"
Amber: "Yeah, for real."
Amber: "But only until the first location. After that—"
*The breath is forced out of me by the sharp impact of Marina tackling my stomach. A warm, but damp, sensation wraps itself tightly around me, as deadweight snuggles up against my chest.*
Marina: "Thankyouthankyouthankyouthankyouthankyou!"
Marina: "I’m hugging you, silly."
*My cheeks grow warmer with each passing second. I try to wiggle my way out, but she stays latched on, her grip getting tighter and tighter.*
Marina: "Ah, sorry! I’m just super happy!"
*She unlatches herself, letting me clear my throat and recollect myself.*
Amber: "Anyway, let’s get inside. I’m burning up out here."
*We clamber back inside the motorhome, leaving the dry hellishness behind.*
*I turn the ignition and let the AC blast away the RV’s stuffiness.*
*Letting out breaths of sweet relief, Marina and I collapse back into our seats.*
Amber: "Alright, so where’s the first piece of the treasure?"
Marina: "Um. . . Well—"
Amber: "You have no idea, do you?"
*I’m not surprised at all. Should have seen this coming.*
Amber: "You have the journal?"
Marina: "Yeah."
*She hands the cheap, paperback book to me. The cover feels like it could fall apart at the turn of a page. The whole thing is fitting of its rustic design.*
*I slide my thumb across the edges and flip through the pages. Thick forewords and passages commenting on the journal’s historical significance skip past my eyes, most long enough to be their own book.*
*Finally, a photocopied picture of one of the original entries appears, preserving the faded cursive lettering, the entire thing printed on the next page over.*
*Crusting brown stains and wrinkles engrave the photo. Some of the writing has been scratched out too, like someone made mistakes and couldn’t erase them.*
*I hate to admit it, but for something that’s probably a sham, the thing looks pretty authentic.*
*Yeah, I’m not reading all this crap. Where should I actually start?*
*Let’s see. If he left California, lived in Missouri, and supposedly hid something in New Mexico like Marina said, then what we’re looking for is likely a later entry.*
Amber: "Ah, here we are."
Marina: "What does it say?"
Amber: "The gist of it is that it’s near a big rock and a bunch of little rocks."
Marina: "That should narrow it down, right?"
Amber: "Yeah—to the entire state of New Mexico."

Marina: "There’s a ship out in the desert? Whoa…"
*I peer over the journal. Marina’s imagination is going bonkers, her eyes alight as she tries to wrap her head around such a novelty even existing.*
Amber: "How long have you lived in New Mexico?"
*Marina swells up with pride, only to deflate like a balloon before speaking, instead choosing to blow a misplaced strand of hair out of the way.*
Marina: "All my life."

Amber: "It’s a small mountain near the border of New Mexico and Utah that’s vaguely shaped like a ship."
Marina: "Ohhhhhh, okay. That makes way more sense."

Marina: "Yeah…"
Marina: "But hey! At least I know now!"

Amber: "Dunno, but it’s worth a shot. We don’t have anything else to go on."

Marina: "No… I think I want to do this."
Amber: "Alright then."
*I put the car in drive and look ahead.*
*The sun has set, the blue shade of dusk rising up to take its place. I press down on the gas. Marina turns to me and smiles.*
Amber: "Let’s hit the road."
*The sun has long since set and given way to darkness. Only my headlights remain, illuminating the road and silhouetting the small rock formations around us.*

*With that, I step into the back room and slam the sliding door, before collapsing onto the oversized bed.*
*Even in the darkness I can make out the pictures hanging on the wall. Once upon a time, they were generic scenic pieces. Now they’re photos. Photos of me and Gramps.*
*My favorite is the one of us in front of a museum for music. Rock and roll, to be specific.*
*I was just a little pipsqueak back then, holding up an autographed vinyl sleeve that was about the size of my head.*
*He was by my side, squatting down and ruffling my hair as I gave the biggest smile I could—even with my missing teeth.*
*I let out a sigh and fixate on the ceiling's skylight, a small sliver of the moonlight shining through.*
*It feels like an eternity since I’ve last laid down in this bed. I remember it felt like being hugged by a cloud. Now it feels like concrete.*

*I look down. Through the crack under the door I can see her shadow turning around to leave.*
*The words slip out of my mouth before she can even take her first step.*
Amber: "‘Night, kiddo."
*Most people imagine Indian reservations as tucked away slices of heaven. Maybe some pocketed oasis of vibrant greens and flowing water in what is otherwise a wasteland.*
*Those people are wrong.*
*The Navajo reservation is as sandy and barren as the rest of the desert, and at 22,475 miles, it’s neither green nor funsized.*
*Not that I hate it.*
*Like there’s a rhythm to it all.*

*Soon we’re rolling into the town, its jagged namesake peeking over the horizon—Shiprock.*

Joseph: "Oh god, thank you so much."
Mariah: "Yeah, I guess…"
*About as fed up with this as I am, Joseph shoots her a glare and smacks her on the back of the head.*
Mariah: "Ow! Alright, I’m sorry and stuff."
Amber: "Marina, are you sure? They destroyed your car."
Marina: "Yeah, but it was pretty rundown anyway."
Amber: "You’re unreal, you know that?"
Amber: "Anyway, we should head out now. We have a few things to take care of."
Marina: "Thanks for showing us the gold! Now I’m sure the treasure is real."
Joseph: "No problem, ladies."
Joseph: "Again, really sorry for all the trouble. If there’s anything we can do for you, just give us a shout."
Marina: "Sure thing!"
*We head for the door, but Marina turns around before stepping outside.*
Marina: "By the way, was there a phone in the car?"
Joseph: "The pink Heisei?"
Marina: "Yeah! That’s the one!"
Joseph: "Ah…"
Joseph: "We kind of sold that too."
*It feels like the page is staring back at me.*
*In fact, at this point, it probably is.*
*My gaze drifts from the journal to the window by the entrance. Marina is outside, twirling the payphone's cord as she pops in another quarter.*
*She bites her lips and looks through the window. Our eyes meet. She sends me a small, anxious wave.*
*Closing the book, I let out a yawn and remove my head from my fist. I'm sure there's a red imprint on my cheek.*
*Figuring out we had to go to Shiprock was the easy part. Everything else has been a gigantic pain the ass.*
*There's a rush of precious cold air getting sucked from the building. Marina enters, carefully shutting the door, and taking the seat opposite me in the booth.*
Amber: "So how'd it go?"
Marina: "It went fine, I guess."
Amber: "Your parents are okay with all of this?"
Marina: "Maybe not "okay..."
*A smile creeps onto her face.*
Marina: "But they understand."
*At least someone gets it, because I still don't.*
Marina: "They weren't happy about the car, though."
Amber: "I think you were the only person who was."
Marina: "How couldn’t I be! Mariah showed us that the treasure is real!"
Amber: "You're going to believe the people who stole your car?"
Marina: "Besides, they're nice."
*Before I can argue further, our waitress emerges from the kitchen, carrying our order: a hot black coffee for me, a milkshake for Marina and a large order of fries for us both.*
*Marina's eyes brighten at the sight of the shake. Like with the slushie, her sweet tooth takes control of her and she practically starts drooling over the glass.*
*With a nod, the waitress disappears back into the kitchen.*
Marina: "Thanks for the food, by the way."
Amber: "Well, it's not like I really had a choice. I could hear your stomach from the driver's seat. I wasn't about to let you starve to death on my watch."
Amber: "Plus I was getting pretty hungry, too."
*Amazingly, Marina didn't freak out at the sight of the diner, like she has at everything else so far.*
*I was genuinely shocked when I didn't hear a gasp of awe escape her mouth as we pulled up.*
Marina: "Hey Amber, why don't you get a shake, too? Coffee and fries don't really go together, don't you think?"
Amber: "I'm fine. I don't really like sweet things."
Marina: "But it's super good. Especially when you dip your fries in it."
*She takes several of the fries and does just that, dipping them in the shake like it was ketchup or mustard.*
Amber: "That’s silly. I can do the same thing with my coffee."
*Taking a fry, I dunk it into my coffee and take a bite out of it.*
*...*
*That was a bad idea.*
*Grimacing, I place the rest of the fry on my napkin as I try to cope with the cold chill and sulfury aftertaste.*
Marina: "Maybe it would work better if you put creamer in it?"
Amber: "Somehow, I doubt that. Besides, it would just ruin the coffee."
Marina: "I don’t know how you can even drink it without cream, or at least a little sugar. It’s so bitter."
Amber: "It’s an acquired taste. You find flavor in the bitterness."
Marina: "That’s why I think it’s weird that you drink something like that."
Amber: "How do you mean?"
Marina: "I dunno…"
*Her brow furrows in deep thought.*
Marina: "You always seem so… yes or no about stuff."
Marina: "If you like something, then you do. If you don’t like something, then you don’t."
Amber: "You’re losing me, Mare."
Marina: "What did Mom use to tell us when we didn’t listen to her…?"
Marina: "Oh yeah!"
Marina: "Basically, you have a really thick head, Amber!"
*I nearly spit the coffee out of my mouth.*
Amber: "Gee, thanks."
Marina: "Or was it a thick skull?"
Marina: "Whatever, you know what I mean, right?"
Amber: "I think so. Maybe."
Amber: "It wasn’t really an acquired taste for me, now that I think about it. Maybe I’ve always liked the bitterness."
*Or maybe I just convinced myself that I did. I don’t know.*
Amber: "Is that a bad thing?"
*Just like in Mariah’s RV, Marina shakes her head and gives me a sweet, genuine smile.*
Marina: "I don’t think so. I actually really like it. It reminds me of Shiprock."
*A warm tickling sensation bubbles up in my chest. It forces me to smile, but I play it off all cool and breezy like.*
Amber: "Heh, I remind you of a rock? You shouldn't say that kinda thing. People might think they’re insults."
*Satisfied with how I played that off, I reach for a fry to reward myself, but instead of hot, crispy goodness, something smooth brushes against my fingertips.*
*I gaze down at the plate of fries. Marina’s hand, for a split second, rests below mine.*
*We instinctively snap back. Marina’s face is a bright shade of pink, and mine is probably similar.*
Amber: "S-Sorry."
*The room feels ten degrees hotter. We try to re-establish eye contact, but our glances shoot off in other directions as soon as we meet.*
Marina: "Hey, Amber…"
*I manage to compose myself long enough to look back.*
Marina: "You said something about bills and expenses last night, right?"
Marina: "I don’t know what you exactly meant by that or what it is you have to pay off, but since we know the treasure is real now, I was wondering…"
Marina: "Maybe you might think about sticking around to look with me? We could even split it… or something."
Amber: "W-Well, first off, we don’t even know if the treasure is actually real. Mariah could be pulling our legs."
*My coffee is mostly gone. All that’s left is a puddle and a few stray grounds.*
Amber: "I’ll think about it, though."
Marina: "Really?"
Amber: "I only said I would think about it! Don’t get too excited just yet!"
Marina: "I won’t, I won’t. I just think it would be super cool."
*Marina flops her chin down and rests on the table.*
Marina: "How’s it going anyway? Have you figured out where the first part of the treasure might be?"
Amber: "No luck."
Amber: "It’s not like the guy drew a map or anything. He just jotted down details about the places he went to."
*That, and rambling about how awesome he was.*
Marina: "Do you think Mariah was right and he hid it on top of ol’ Shippy?"
Amber: "Don’t listen to Mariah, okay? She doesn’t know what she’s talking about."
Amber: "Think about it—how would he even scale the mountain? He was traveling solo on horseback. I doubt he would have the tools to do it."
Amber: "And even if he did, I’m pretty sure he would’ve gone on and on about it in his journal."
*With a puppy-dog whine, Marina frowns.*
Marina: "Are you sure he didn’t mention anything else? Maybe something we didn’t catch."
*I slide the book across the table and over the leftover crumbs, stretching out my stiff joints as I rest against the booth.*
Amber: "Take a look, if you want. My eyes are gonna start bleeding if I read it again."
Amber: "The only things he really mentioned are the rocks and the dikes around it."
Marina: "O-Oh."
*Marina forces herself to stare down at the plate, the blush from earlier reappearing.*
*‘Oh?’*
*Ohhhhhhhhh.*
Amber: "No, not like that."
*I stifle a giggle. Teasing her is gonna be a blast.*
Amber: "I mean the rocks that branch out from the mountain. That’s what they’re call—"
Amber: "Huh."
*It’s so simple. But it makes sense.*
*I shoot up and throw down some money. Marina jumps at the sound of the table rattling.*
Marina: "What’s the matter? Did you figure out where it is?"
Amber: "Maybe. At least a general area."
*I march towards the door.*
*The table rattles again as Marina leaps up. She stays behind to straighten it out before scrambling to catch up with me.*
Marina: "We’re going now?"
Amber: "Hell no. We don’t have any equipment."
Amber: "But we know someone who does."
*I shove the door open, the last bastion between refreshing air and burning, dry heat.*
Amber: "C’mon, let’s go cash in a favor."
Marina: "It sure was nice for Mariah and her friends to let us use some of their equipment."
Amber: "Only after that Joseph guy begged her to."
*I pause to look around. We decided to wait until nightfall to go look around some more.*
*It’s like an entirely different world the further you get from the insanity. Quiet and calm, the only lights are the stars and the faint glow of the campground, the campers and motorhomes flickering in the distance.*
*Marina fiddles with the metal detector’s headphones, removing them halfway.*
Marina: "Sorry, the beeping's loud. What was that?"
Amber: "Forget it. I’m not playing that game. Just keep listening."
Marina: "Oh!"
Amber: "Did you find something?"
Marina: "I think so! It’s over here!"
*I set aside my shovel and throw myself to the ground, clawing at the sand.*
*A shiny speck sticks out of the ground. Frantically, I dig it up.*
Marina: "Is it the gold?"
Amber: "It’s—!"
*I scrape harder.*
Amber: "It’s—!"
*Harder.*
Amber: "It’s—"
*Finally, I unearth it and hold it up to the starlight.*
Amber: "—a soda tab."
Amber: "Dammit! This is the fifth one!"
Marina: "If we find anymore, we could start making a curtain out of them!"
*I send the tab flying, speeding off over the dikes.*
Marina: "Maybe we should call it a night. I'm sure we can find it once we're fresh in the morning."
Amber: "Nah, that’s no good. We can only do it now because Parks and Recreation isn’t paying as much attention."
Amber: "We’ll keep looking for a little while. If we can’t find anything, we might want to assume the worst."
*Her face wrenches with hopelessness.*
Marina: "That somebody else found it?"
Amber: "That it doesn’t exist!"
Amber: "Geez, it’s like you’ve never even considered that it might not be real."
Marina: "But Mariah—"
Amber: "Yeah, yeah. I know."
*I grab my shovel and swing it back over my shoulder. As we walk, we stay as close to the mountain range as possible.*
Amber: "Why are you so gung-ho about this thing, anyway?"
Amber: "If you’re just in it for the cash, there are better ways to make money than dragging yourself around the desert."
Marina: "Oh no, it’s nothing like that!"
Marina: "It’s just…"
*Her voice trails off. It hangs over us, never fully evaporating into the cool night air.*
Amber: "It’s okay. Don’t feel like you have to tell me."
*We both have things we don’t want to talk about. That’s becoming very clear.*
*At least Marina doesn’t swerve off the road so she can go pout.*
*I just don’t want to think about it. All I’ve been doing for the last month is thinking about it. I almost wish my brain would quit working so I wouldn’t have to.*
*So naturally, I take a long, lonely trip, with nothing to do except think.*
Marina: "Amber, it’s happening again!"
*Marina stops, hands quivering as she tightens her grip.*
Amber: "I swear, if this is another soda tab…"
Marina: "I don’t think it is. The beeping is really loud, and it’s vibrating a lot."
Amber: "Wait. It’s vibrating?"
Marina: "Uh-huh. It feels weird."
*The metal detector hovers over the ground between the rock and the sand. I thrust my shovel into the spot, the metal scraping against the edge of the dike.*
*Planting my foot on the shovel’s head, I drive it further into the ground, using every ounce of my strength to dig.*
*The first load of dirt and sand unclenches from the ground, loosening the earth around it.*
*I throw the load aside and repeat the process. Each time, it’s a little easier, a little lighter.*
*Finally, the shovel strikes something hard, sending a shocking vibration through my hand and up my entire body.*
Marina: "Bedrock?"
Amber: "I haven’t dug that deep."
*Dropping to my knees, I reach my hands into the hole and brush off the remaining dirt.*
*Marina leans in. The sound of her anxiously swallowing is barely audible over my heavy breathing.*
*My fingers brush against something. I gulp and pull out the hole’s contents—a small, rotting wood box.*
*My heart stops.*
*Trembling, I open the box, the leftover dirt falling into my lap.*
*Gold.*
Amber: "It exists?!"
Marina: "IT EXISTS!"
*Marina yells at the top of her lungs, a yell so loud that it bounces off the mountain, echoing off to the horizon and then back again.*
*Marina has been unleashed.*
Amber: "Not so loud, they might hear us."
*She lowers her voice to an excited whisper, channeling her energy into bouncing up and down.*
Marina: "But it exists! I told you it did!"
*She’s right, and despite being proven wrong, I can’t help but smile.*
*Here I thought it was gonna be some plastic treasure chest with a coupon for chocolate milk inside it or something equally shameless and corporate.*
Marina: "Ambeeeeer!"
*My only warning is a quick gust of wind.*
*Before I even have a chance to brace for it, I’m engulfed by Marina and her hug.*
Marina: "It’s real! I told you it was! I told you!"
*I stop for a second, then smile.*
Amber: "Yeah. You were right. It’s real."
Marina: "Should we tell someone? What do we even do now?"
Amber: "Honestly?"
Amber: "I’m not sure."
Amber: "It’s getting pretty late, though. Let’s pack it up for the night. We can think about what to do in the morning."
*For now, we can just marvel at the fact that it’s not bogus.*
Amber: "Sound like a plan?"
Marina: "It does, but…"
*She takes a deep, long breath, inhaling all the air around and maybe even some of mine, then releases it all at once.*
Marina: "IT’S ACTUALLY REALLY REAL!"
Amber: "Marina, quiet! They’re gonna hear us!"
*A thunderous banging wakes me from a dead sleep, rattling the entire motorhome.*
*The muffled shout leaks in from outside, followed by another voice.*
*And another voice.*
*Gee, I wonder who this could be?*
*I roll out of bed and open the slider connecting to the other ‘rooms.’*
*Marina is sitting pressed up against the back of the foldout bed with the sheets over her head.*
Marina: "Who do you think it is?"
Amber: "Take a wild guess."
*I march over and throw open the door. As expected, my three favorite people stand before me.*
Mariah: "It’s about time. Do you have any idea how long we’ve been out here?"
*A groggy Tess sways from side to side. Joseph’s gentle hands are the only thing preventing her from passing out on the ground.*
Amber: "It’s funny that you of all people accuse us of theft."
Mariah: "What else would you call what happened to my equipment?"
Amber: "I don’t know? ‘Borrowing?’ You loaned it to us, remember?"
Mariah: "Correction—Joe loaned it to you."
*Joseph gives a tired smirk and a wave.*
Mariah: "I was forced to sit idly by and watch you make off with it. That’s theft if I’ve ever seen it."
*She pushes me aside and clambers into the motorhome, followed by the other two.*
Joseph: "Looks like we’re coming in."
Tess: "Good morning."
Joseph: "Tired. For what it’s worth, we tried stopping her."
*Mariah stomps to the middle of the motorhome, analyzing everything before releasing a loud scoff.*
Mariah: "So where’s my stuff?"
Marina: "Don’t worry about it, you guys are great."
Marina: "Hey Tess, did you hang up your new windchime?"
*Tess’ eyes flicker with interest for all of a second then return to their obstinate droopy state.*
Tess: "Uh-huh. Above my bed."
Mariah: "What’s up with the foldout? You two don’t sleep together?"
*Amber & Marina" "Wh-What?!*
Joseph: "Told ya."
Mariah: "Dammit."
*Grumbling, Mariah pulls a bill from her pocket and slaps it in Joe’s hand.*
Tess: "Does that mean we get to sleep in tomorrow morning?"
Joseph: "Sure does, kiddo. Sure does."
*The ringleader of the circus returns to inspecting the motorhome, fiddling with the magnets on the fridge.*
Mariah: "Anyway, give me back my equipment and we’ll get out of your hair."
Joseph: "C’mon, just let them use it. It’s the least we can do after tearing apart her car."
*Mariah's attention turns to the counter, topped with the opened box of stunning, gold nuggets from the night before.*
Mariah: "What is this?"
Amber: "What is what?"
Mariah: "The thing on the counter. It almost looks like…"
Marina: "Oh, that?"
Marina: "That’s the piece of the treasure we found!"
Mariah: "Hah! Yeah right. You’re kidding."
*She chuckles.*
*She stops.*
*I have a devious thought, and crack an equally devious smile.*
Amber: "Nope. She’s telling the truth. One-hundred percent."
*I wrap my arm around Mariah and bring her close. Joseph and Tess shoot each other alarmed glances.*
*She stands there, stupefied.*
Amber: "Really now?"
Marina: "Sure thing!"
Marina: "Anyway, turns out she was right!"
*Mariah looks down, muttering to herself.*
Mariah: "But how…"
Mariah: "Why wouldn’t he just hide it on the mountain…?"
Mariah: "It doesn’t make sense... If he was going back to Missouri..."
*Her voice diminishes, until her words are reduced to lip flaps. Like a mad scientist trying to figure out a complex equation, she retreats into her own little world.*
Marina: "M-Mariah?"
*A smidge of sympathy buds inside me. I look to Joseph and Tess. Even they look a little shocked.*
Amber: "Is she gonna be okay?"
Joseph: "I… honestly don’t know."
*Tess approaches Mariah, who is still whispering nonsense under her breath. Nonchalantly, Tess hops in front of her, her hand reaching upwards, trying to wave it in front of Mariah and bring her back to our world.*
*No response.*
*Tess pulls Mariah's arms skyward, then allows them to flop down to her side.*
*Still no response.*
Joseph: "You know, we’ve been looking into that treasure since we were kids."
*Joseph steps up to the counter and stares into the box.*
Joseph: "Never expected someone else to find it before us."
Amber: "That’s more than I can say. I didn’t even think it existed."
Joseph: "There’s that too."
*He sighs. Then, with a shrug, cracks a smile.*
Joseph: "Well, good job anyway. You ladies seem cool, so if it couldn’t be us then at least it was you."
Marina: "You guys wanna stick around? We could tell you more about it."
*He looks at Mariah.*
*He grabs Mariah by the shoulders and begins pushing her towards the exit.*
Joseph: "C’mon Mariah, let’s go make you a nice, cold michelada."
Joseph: "You two can keep the equipment. Think of it as an apology gift."
*Because cheaply made tools I could get at any hardware store are of equal value to a car.*
*They’re almost out of the motorhome, when Mariah flips and rushes back.*
Mariah: "HOLD ON A MINUTE, THIEF!" with hpunch
*She looks me straight in the eyes, her expression returning to that of a piercing hawk.*
Mariah: "You just had beginner's luck."
Mariah: "You might’ve found the first piece, but I promise you: me and my team will find the rest."
*She extends a hand and smirks.*
*I pause for a moment, then grab it and offer a smirk of my own.*
Amber: "You’re on."
*Marina and Joseph both let out a sigh of relief, as if they’ve just witnessed the signing of a peace treaty.*
Joseph: "Well, guess that takes care of that."
Mariah: "Now let’s get out of here and go back to a proper base."
Tess: "Are you going to drink until you feel better again, sis?"
Mariah: "Sh-Shut up! Didn’t Mom ever teach you any manners?"
Tess: "You always said Mom is an airheaded ditz who wouldn’t know good advice or a good man if it bit her on the butt."
Mariah: "Oh yeah."
*Joseph pulls the door closed. Marina and I stay put, as the bickering of the trio fades into the distance.*
Marina: "Were you serious?"
Amber: "Hm? About what?"
Marina: "The treasure. You told Mariah that you were gonna find the rest."
Marina: "If you were just being nice and wanna leave now, that’s fine too. You’ve already done a lot for me."
*She casts her eyes downwards to hide her emotions, but I know better by now.*
Amber: "And let you keep all the treasure for yourself? Hell no!"
*Her eyes bounce back.*
Amber: "Besides, someone’s gotta keep you from getting yourself into trouble."
Amber: "May as well be me."
Marina: "For real? But don’t you have the festival to go to?"
Amber: "Eh, it’s still a little while away. I wanted to do some sight seeing anyway."
*Marina launches forward.*
*Before I can sidestep, she tackles me with the force of a gold-filled freight train.*
*I don’t even try to resist. It’s futile in the grace of Marina’s hugs.*
Marina: "Thank you thank you thank you thank you thank you thank you thank you thank you thank you!"
Amber: "Actually, I probably owe you an apology."
*She cocks her head and looks up at me. I look away, my bashful side taking over.*
Amber: "Well… you know. I kept saying it wasn’t real and stuff…"
Amber: "Yeah, but…"
Marina: "Relaaaaax. Just figure out where we have to go next."
*I smile.*
*I glance over the second to last entry. Key phrases like ‘split path,’ ‘detour,’ and ‘acres of identical arched stone’ throw my brain into the deep end, drowning me in dizzying puzzles before I’ve even fully read it.*
*Marina peeks over, trying to decipher the entries with me.*
*A speck of inadequacy crawls up inside me and I quickly flip to the next entry before Marina notices I’m stuck.*
*I’ll deal with that later. For now, we’ll just narrow this down.*
Marina: "You're my Lamasi, Amber!"
*Suddenly, my chest feels like a hummingbird is fluttering around in it. I shift my eyes away from her smile and hide my face.*
Amber: "Thanks…"
*When you strip away the circumstances, I guess I did save and protect her like that Lamasi guy did with the miner.*
Marina: "A gorge is like a canyon, right?"
*Marina leans in closer and points to the entry, strands of her soft hair tickling my cheek. I scoot back and power through it, taking a sharp breath to recollect myself.*
Amber: "Basically, yeah."
*Marina nearly springs out of her seat in a burst of realization, her head an inch away from smacking into the ceiling.*
Marina: "Oh my god."
Marina: "Does that mean we get to go to the Grand Canyon? Do we get to go to the Grand Canyon, Amber?"
Amber: "Not unless history decides to rewrite itself."
Amber: "There are lots of canyons with ruins. One of them is the Grand Canyon. But most of them have been cleaned out for hundreds of years."
*I choke down a groan. Indian ruins aren’t exactly rarities around here. Maybe that other entry wasn’t so bad after all.*
Amber: "So we’re looking for a place that was still being used in the 1850’s."
Marina: "Hmm."
*Marina hunkers down and thinks with me, wrinkles forming on her forehead as she tries to strain out an answer.*
*I’m pretty sure she doesn’t even know where to start, but hey, at least she’s trying.*
Amber: "Well, there is one place."
*Marina hardly gets a breath out before I crush her dreams once again.*
*She slinks back into her chair and quietly pouts.*
Amber: "It’s still in Arizona, but it’s a different place—Canyon de Chelly."
Amber: "The Navajo weren’t chased out until 1863, so the timing isn’t too screwy."
Amber: "Place is loaded with ruins, too."
Marina: "Oh, are they Puebloan ruins?"
*My flow of information stops. She knows what I’m talking about? She can actually pronounce it?*
Amber: "Uh… Yeah. I’m surprised you know that."
Marina: "Yup! I’ve been reading some of those travel guides lying around. Super interesting!"
*Those things must be about as old as I am by now. I didn’t have the heart to throw them away when I was cleaning everything out.*
Amber: "Hey, that’s how I did it. It’s the best way to learn."
Amber: "Anyway, Canyon de Chelly is pretty damn close to here."
*She gasps.*
Marina: "So it might’ve lined up with the route he took!"
Amber: "Well, I can’t be one-hundred percent positive, but…"
*I close my eyes and snap the book shut, the air between the pages puffing out at us. Then, with one eye open, shoot Marina a grin.*
Amber: "I’m pretty sure."
*Arizona’s highways are infamously boring. Nothing but hills; up, down, up, down. There’s nothing interesting to look at. Both sides of the road are smothered in nondescript shrubs.*
*It’s the part of the country where you just zone out and drive on autopilot.*
*When we finally see a sign for the town of Chinle, right next to the canyon, it feels like we’re approaching the Promised Land.*
*Marina looks up from the book she’s reading just in time to miss the sign.*
Marina: "Was that it? Are we there yet?"
Amber: "Nah. Forty miles to go still. So about half an hour."
*She makes a sound like a popped balloon and goes back to reading.*
*We take an exit in a tiny excuse for a town; one of those places that has a gas station and a few old houses.*
*It’s like in Monopoly, where you buy up all the property you can, even though it’s not that great.*
Marina: "This is it?"
*Marina repeats her earlier question as I slow down to turn left.*
Amber: "Nope. Almost. I don’t even know what this place is called."
*She makes the popped balloon noise again, but leaves the book closed.*
*When we finally reach Chinle, it’s pretty obvious. This is the first real town we’ve seen in hours.*
*The outskirts are industrial, dominated by what I assume are factories.*
*A pair of stray dogs lope along the side of the road, hungry and lean. Marina makes a sound of sympathy, pressing her face against the glass as we pass them.*
Marina: "Hey Amber?"
Amber: "We’re not getting a dog."
Marina: "But they’re so cute! And we could dress him up in a bandanna and-"
Amber: "No way. I’ve never gotten why people wanna dress their pets up like people."
Marina: "It’s cute."
*Marina pouts. I figured she was just joking, but she didn’t actually think I would let her get a dog, right?*
*It seems that all is forgiven by the time I pull into a gas station. She’s bouncing in her seat with her usual enthusiasm. Or maybe it’s her bladder.*
*My second guess must have been correct, because as soon as I shut off the engine, she darts out of the RV and into the store.*
*While she does that, I fuel up. No telling when we'll see the next gas station. Could be a hundred miles. And this thing drinks gasoline like a thirsty dog drinks water.*
*By the time I finish, Marina still isn't back, so I lock up and head inside.*
*The store is devoid of other customers. It’s unremarkable, with the usual selection of candy, chips, and overpriced beef jerky.*
*I’m not hungry, just passing time.*
*Soon, Marina trots out of the bathroom, glancing around. She spots me and heads over.*
Amber: "Want anything?"
Marina: "Duh. Let’s see…"
*I leave her to agonize over the candy bars while I flip through some of the travel brochures beneath the window. There are a couple about Canyon de Chelly, which is our destination just up the road.*
*There are flyers for other Arizona attractions, too, including the non-local ones. Of course there are a few brochures for the Grand Canyon, plus a couple “must-see” sights in Phoenix, the capital.*
*There’s even an ad for a novelty just called “The Thing” in Tucson, but that’s far out of our way.*
Marina: "Okay, I’m ready."
*I turn around. Marina has a couple king-sized candy bars in her hands, and a large bottle of Coke tucked under her arm.*
Amber: "You seriously need all that?"
Marina: "I thought we could share."
*She gives me her best “offended puppy” impression.*
Amber: "Fine, fine."
*I still don’t want anything, but I can’t say no to that.*
Amber: "Here, I’m gonna use the bathroom too. Meet you at the car."
*I place my wallet atop the pallet of candy she’s carrying and send her towards the attendant.*
*When I come back out of the bathroom, Marina’s still talking to the clerk. There’s a bag in her hands, so she must have already finished paying and gotten distracted talking.*
*I join the two of them.*
*Marina looks like she’s seen a ghost.*
Amber: "What’s up?"
*I take my wallet from where it’s just sitting forgotten on the counter.*
Marina: "Um, I was just asking about the canyon and the tours and stuff."
*The clerk nods.*
*Damn. I’m not surprised, but that’s frustrating.*
Amber: "What isn’t closed off?"
*I just smile at the attendant-*
Amber: "Got it. Thank you!"
*-and lead a dumbstruck Marina out of the store.*
*As I unlock the RV, Marina prances around me.*
Marina: "What are we gonna do?"
Amber: "Well, I’m gonna take a nap."
*She stops.*
Marina: "Huh?"
Amber: "I’m tired, so I’m going to nap for a little bit. I checked one of the pamphlets and there’s a tour at 6:30, so we’ll just join that and then sneak off."
Marina: "Like, on our own?"
Amber: "Uh, yeah."
*Her eyes go wide again.*
Marina: "Amber, you’re a genius!"
*I twist a lock of my hair around my finger and glance away. Her constant compliments are a little embarrassing. Especially for stuff that seems obvious to me.*
Amber: "Thanks. It’s not much of a plan though."
Marina: "Yeah, but at least it’s a plan! I thought we were gonna have to just give up or something."
Amber: "Nah. "Rules are made to be broken" and all that."
*I feel a bit guilty that we’ll be breaking rules specifically put in place because of people like us. But I can safely say that we’ll be more careful than the average treasure hunter.*
*Just picturing Mariah, I can easily see her smashing down ancient ruins in her search.*
Amber: "But yeah, wake me up at 5:30, unless you’re going to nap too?"
Marina: "Nope, I’m not tired anymore, so I’ll just read some more."
Amber: "Alright. Good night."
Marina: "Night night!"
*I leave her up in the front as I head back to my bed and fall into it.*
*After bunching up my blankets around me, it doesn’t take long to fall asleep.*
*I wake up slowly, fighting to keep my eyes open. In the back of my head, I feel like something is wrong.*
*It takes a minute before I realize I’ve overslept.*
*It’s a quarter to six. Marina was supposed to wake me up 15 minutes ago.*
Amber: "Marina?"
*I call her name, but get no response.*
*Rolling out of bed, I massage my eyes and stumble up towards the front.*
*Of course she’s asleep. Sprawled out on the dash, with her head buried in her arms. It looks like she’s been out for a while.*
*Jerking her by the shoulder, I shake her awake.*
*Her speech is slurred by sleepiness.*
*I ignore the temptation to make the "not you" joke.*
Amber: "How’s that book you’re reading?"
Marina: "It’s great! I just put it down to-"
*A look of shock spreads across her face.*
*Mounting horror takes over as she realizes the time.*
Marina: "Oh God, I’m so, so sorry. I was just gonna nap but then I forgot. To set an alarm I mean. To wake me up."
Amber: "Yeah, that’s what alarms are for."
*Freaking out, she hurriedly tries to buckle her seatbelt, failing several times in her frenzy.*
*I pat her on the shoulder as I sit down. It's not even a big deal, but she's so worked up.*
Amber: "It’s cool. We’re not late yet. Ready to go?"
*Marina just nods, staring at the floor.*
*I buckle up and turn the key. The engine roars to life, and I throw it in reverse. I have to crane my neck to make sure I don’t run over anyone.*
*Road-signs direct us out of town and towards the canyon. After spending so long on the highway, going down these streets with lower speed limits feels restrictive.*
*From the corner of my eye, I watch Marina. She’s being abnormally quiet.*
*She fixes her hair, pops her knuckles, and rubs her shoulders, but she doesn’t say anything.*
*Finally, I cave.*
Amber: "What’s wrong with you?"
Marina: "Huh? What do you mean?"
*Her tone of voice is stressed. Maybe I’m more intimidating than I think.*
Amber: "You’re being weird. Super quiet."
Marina: "Oh. Um."
Amber: "Um?"
Marina: "I didn’t know if you were mad at me or not."
Amber: "What, for falling asleep?"
*She nods.*
Amber: "Pfft."
Amber: "Nah, I’m not mad. Trust me, you’d know for sure if I was."
Amber: "I told you it wasn't a big deal, didn't I?"
Marina: "You promise?"
Amber: "Yeah, I promise."
*Marina bounces in her seat, immediately cheerier.*
Amber: "Sign of what?"
Marina: "That we wouldn’t be good partners! You know, treasure hunting buddies?"
Amber: "Oh, we’re partners now, are we? You’re the sidekick, right?"
Marina: "No way! I’m totally the main character. We’re here because of me."
*I laugh, causing her to turn away smiling. She’s a lot cuter when she smiles.*
*I’ve been smiling more, too, I realize. As I think that, it starts to make me sad again.*
*Do I really deserve to be smiling right now?*
Marina: "Hey Amber?"
Amber: "Huh?"
*Like she read my mind, Marina snaps me out of it.*
Marina: "Can we listen to Super Crush again?"
Amber: "You seriously liked that one?"
*Super Crush was one of those groups that Gramps loved to show off to other people, because they were so weird. All of the members played a different instrument on each album, so it was like a whole other band every time.*
Amber: "That’s definitely true."
*With one hand on the steering wheel, I dig around in the bin of tapes by my feet. I flick my gaze between that and the road. Eventually I find the one I’m looking for - it has a Halloween-orange case with text that looks like slime.*
*When it starts to play, Marina hums along. She’s almost on-key. Guess she liked it enough to remember how the songs go.*
*I crank the volume up, earning a grin from Marina. The sound is tinny and distorted at this level. More distorted than usual, I mean. But it’s also more fun.*
*Our smiles last all the way until we roll into a nearly-empty parking lot.*
*As I turn the volume back down, I can tell she's thinking the same thing I am.*
Marina: "Amber?"
Amber: "Yeah?"
Amber: "There probably should. What time is it?"
*Marina twists around to check.*
Marina: "Uh, 6:20."
Amber: "What the hell, did they start early?"
*There are other cars around, but they’re all unoccupied. My parking job is hasty and I take up two spaces, but don’t bother to correct it.*
*We hurry out of the RV. I almost forget the key in the ignition and have to double back to grab it. Marina’s already over at an information desk, probably talking to a ranger.*
*But then she turns back to me, the despair on her face visible even though it’s getting dark.*
Marina: "There’s no one here!"
*There aren’t any lights on inside, either. Probably closed at six. I vaguely recall reading that in a brochure, but I thought there would still be someone around for the tours.*
Amber: "Don’t panic!"
*I jog back to the RV and unlock it again. Rummaging through the glovebox, I pull out the little blue flashlight that Gramps gave me a few years ago.*
*I click it on and off a couple times to make sure that it works.*
*After I lock everything back up again, I join Marina by the unhelpful help desk. She’s bouncing on her heels, hands clasped together.*
Amber: "Hey, it’s alright. Worse comes to worst we’ll just do it tomorrow."
Marina: "But what if someone else finds the treasure tonight?"
*I don’t have an answer for that one.*
Amber: "Guess we’ll just have to catch up then, huh?"
*Turning on the flashlight, I swing the beam around. While there’s still some daylight left, it isn’t much. Maybe half an hour at the most.*
Marina: "Catch up to what?"
Amber: "The tour group, silly. Even if we’re late, they can’t have left that long ago. And they’ll be stopping a bunch so we should be able to find them easily."
Marina: "By ourselves?"
*Her voice is almost a squawk, like a surprised parrot.*
Amber: "Well, yeah. I don’t see anyone else around, do you?"
*I swivel around, illuminating nothing but rocks and bushes.*
Amber: "C’mon, it’ll be fine. It’s not like there are gonna be bears or mountain lions this close to a parking lot."
Marina: "Those kinds of things live here?!"
*If she didn’t know that, then what is she afraid of?*
Amber: "Probably. I don’t know. But come on, we’re wasting time."
*Marina whimpers.*
*Then, surprising me, she grabs my hand. Hers is sweaty and warm.*
*I turn and look at her, and meet her wide eyes. It looks like she might cry, and makes me regret being so pushy.*
Amber: "We don’t have to do it if you don’t want to."
*She shakes her head, but clings to me like a kid, her free hand buried in my clothes. If it were any brighter out, she’d see my face turn red.*
Marina: "No, like I said, if we don’t go now someone else will beat us to it."
Marina: "Just… Stay close, okay?"
Amber: "Yeah, of course."
*She lets me lead, but doesn’t let go of my hand. A large, wooden sign marks the start of the trail.*
Amber: "So uh, are you scared of the dark?"
*No answer.*
Amber: "But you were fine at Shiprock?"
Marina: "That was different!"
*Marina’s arm jerks, but she doesn’t let go.*
Marina: "That was in a town."
Marina: "With people."
Marina: "And roads."
Marina: "And no mountain lions."
*The temptation to tease her more is strong, but I don’t. Every time a bird caws or something makes the brush shake, I feel her flinch a little.*
*Somehow, even this side of her is cute, too. A little childish, sure. But it's nice to have her rely on me to keep her safe.*
*To take her mind off things, I make small talk.*
Amber: "So, what are you gonna do once you’re filthy rich from the treasure?"
Marina: "Sorry, what?"
Amber: "I asked what you’ll do with your share of the treasure."
Marina: "Oh, um… I’m not sure yet. I haven’t thought about it."
Amber: "We’re out here looking and you don’t even have a plan for it?"
Marina: "It was a spur of the moment thing! What about you, huh?"
Amber: "I don’t know either."
Marina: "Ha! See I’m not-"
Amber: "Yeah, yeah, but I wasn’t planning on looking for it, you know? I haven’t spent as much time thinking about it."
*I glance behind me to see Marina stick out her tongue. I laugh.*
Amber: "But uh, let’s see. I definitely need to pay off Gramps' hospital and funeral expenses."
Amber: "After that, I guess I should probably get the motorhome fixed up. It’s not gonna break down on us or anything, but it’s kind of old."
Marina: "Why not just buy a new one?"
Amber: "Hell no!"
*My response comes out snappier than I intended.*
Amber: "It’s just that it has sentimental value, you know? I’d rather keep that than buy a newer, better car."
Amber: "I grew up in that thing. I could never just replace it."
Amber: "After that, I’d probably just keep doing what I was doing before I met you."
Marina: "And what's that?"
Amber: "Wandering."
Marina: "Don’t you wanna stop wandering sometime?"
*My steps slow a bit. It’s an innocent question, but it hits me hard.*
Amber: "Not yet, no."
*I whisper it.*
*Marina catches up next to me, dropping my hand as she does so.*
Marina: "Pfft, at least I’m not the only one without a goal."
*I poke her in the side and she squirms away a couple feet.*
Marina: "Ow!"
Amber: "Dork."
*It’s hard to stay sad around her.*
*As we turn round a curve in the road, the tour group comes into view.*
*Just ahead is a cluster of people, most of them armed with flashlights. It’s hard to know for sure, but I can see at least a dozen different figures.*
*The tour guide is shining his light up at a rock face, illuminating some designs on it. Everyone has their backs to us.*
Amber: "Try to be as sneaky as possible."
Amber: "We wanna join up without them noticing, like we've been there the whole time."
Amber: "If anyone notices us show up, they might notice when we leave."
Marina: "Why do we have to join up in the first place?"
Amber: "I don’t know how to get to the ruins we’re looking for. We’ll stay with the group until the tour gets there, and then we’ll sneak off."
*Marina nods.*
Amber: "Okay, follow my lead. And remember, be as quiet as you can."
*We creep forward, slowly. If someone were to turn around and see us, we’d look pretty suspicious. But nobody does. The lecture on petroglyphs gets their full attention.*
*Marina kicks a rock and I tense up, expecting that to break the spell. But we’re close enough now that if it did, it might not matter.*
*And we reach the back of the tour group without anyone noticing. I give Marina a thumbs-up, which she returns.*
Guide: ". . . Anasazi ruins. Some of the Navajo people still call the area home. There’s a living community in the canyon, who…"
*When she catches me staring, she blinks and then smiles. I smile back, though I must look like an idiot just watching her instead of the lecture.*
*After a minute, the guide’s speech concludes and we start to walk down the trail. Much to my dismay, before I can say anything to Marina, the woman in front of us turns around.*
Woman: "Isn’t that just crazy?"
Amber: "Huh?"
*It takes a second for me to understand that she’s talking to me. She fits just about every stereotype of ‘redneck’, from the huge hat on her head to the baby strapped to her chest.*
*She'd probably be attractive, if not for the layers of sunscreen coating her face. Even though it's nearly nighttime. They make her look like a ghost.*
Woman: "I said, isn’t that just crazy? What he was saying about those Indians and how they used to communicate with each other."
Amber: "Oh, yeah."
*Even if I had wanted to talk to her, I still have no idea what the guide had said.*
Woman: "They were just so darn clever. Really makes you wonder how they all ended up dying out, huh?"
*Uhh…*
Amber: "I’m pretty sure a lot of them are still alive. And like, some of them live here. In this canyon."
*I had heard that much, at least. But apparently not everyone did.*
*For a second, the woman looks at me like I’m crazy. But then she busts up laughing.*
Woman: "Sorry, sorry, you’re absolutely right! I was thinking of them Aztecs, down in Mexico!"
*Her laugh is more like a bray. She sounds like a farm animal. In my head, I keep hoping she’ll turn back around and leave us alone.*
*But we’re not so lucky. She keeps on chatting us up as we follow the trail. I’m self-conscious about how noisy she’s being, compared to the low noise level of the others.*
Woman: "Say, is that where the name ‘Texas’ comes from? From ‘Aztec?’"
*Before I can answer, she’s started shouting at someone else.*
Woman: "Howard! Hey, Howard, come over here!"
*By now, nearly everyone is looking at us.*
Guide: "M’am, can you please keep your voice down?"
Woman: "Oops, sorry!"
*She apologizes loudly.*
*So much for staying inconspicuous.*
*Howard makes his way over. He looks rather defeated, face drooping and shoulders sagging. I figure he’s the woman’s husband.*
Howard: "Yes, dear?"
Woman: "I was just talking to this girl here, about history. And I was wonderin’, does the name ‘Texas’ have anything to do with the Aztecs? Since the words sound kinda similar?"
Howard: "I don’t think so."
Woman: "Huh! Go figure!"
*She cackles again. Beside her, Howard sighs. For his sake, I hope her family is loaded or something.*
*Every time she laughs, the baby on her chest bounces. But the lucky kid sleeps right through it all. Maybe he’s used to this.*
Woman: "Anyway! I must have left my manners back in Wisconsin! My name’s Linda, and this’s my husband, Howard."
Linda: "This little champ right here…"
*She thrusts herself and thus the baby towards me.*
Linda: "Is Dino! Ain’t that just the cutest name?"
*I just grunt in response. I’m trying to see around her, up to where the guide is alternating between shooting Linda death glares and trying to narrate the walk.*
*If he’s said anything useful about the canyon in the past couple minutes, I’ve missed it entirely.*
*Glancing over at Marina, who has pulled away a couple feet, it looks like she’s enjoying my torture in struggling silence. Tears have formed at the corners of her eyes, glittering in the dying light.*
*They’re the good kind of tears; the kind from laughing too much. Her hand is smooshed over her mouth and she’s shaking with the effort of staying silent.*
*There’s no way I can let her get away with that, especially since I need to get closer to the front for now.*
*I grab her arm and tug her over into Linda’s line of sight.*
Amber: "I’m Jane."
*The enthusiasm in my voice is so forced.*
*I use fake names just to be safe.*
*Marina limply shakes Linda’s hand, which has grabbed hers. The mirth has vanished from Marina's face.*
*Good.*
Lacey: "Nice to meet you."
*Linda pumps her arm like a guillotine, grinning broadly and yet still managing to walk.*
Linda: "It’s so good to see young people takin’ an interest in nature and history and all that. Are y’all from around here? We’re originally from Tennessee, see, but moved to Wisconsin and…"
*Having been properly introduced, I leave Marina to suffer through the family history. I shift away and walk ahead, passing by most of the tour group, so that I can better hear the guide.*
*For a while, he talks about the wildlife and history of the park. After a couple minutes though, the lessons become more relevant.*
Guide: "We’ll be coming up on the Mummy Cave Ruins here in just a little bit. That’s one of the better known sites in the Canyon. Seven hundred years ago, it was a bustling Puebloan village."
Guide: "We can’t get too close to the ruins, so unfortunately it might be a little too late to really see them clearly tonight."
Guide: "While the Mummy Cave itself hasn’t ever been open to the public…"
*Linda notices me approaching.*
Linda: "Oh, Jane, there you are. I was just telling Lacey about one of the cows I helped birth when I was younger!"
*I’m not sure how the conversation went from introductions to cow birth in such a short span of time, but I’m not curious enough to ask.*
*When I rejoin Marina, she mouths two words.*
*I don’t know how to get out of this one. Annoyance aside, it’ll be impossible to slip away with Linda’s attention on us.*
*But it seems we’re in luck. As we go around another bend in the road, something more interesting than Marina and me comes into view.*
Linda: "Oh, Howard, look! This is it! This is those Mummy Ruins I was telling you about."
*Linda elbows forward, moving ahead of us.*
Linda: "You girls make sure you get a good spot, you’re gonna wanna see this, even if it’s dark out."
*She forges ahead, her husband trailing dutifully. As he passes, I think I hear him say ‘sorry,’ though I’m not certain.*
*Once they’re out of earshot, Marina makes a sound like a train letting out steam.*
Amber: "Saw you laughing and figured it wasn’t fair if I was the only one who suffered."
Marina: "Ugh, I’d take it all back if I could."
Marina: "Seriously though, first she was talking about herself, then about her kid, then about her childhood, and then she started talking about growing up on a farm."
Marina: "Somehow she ended up going on about cows having babies and how messy it all is? I don’t even know."
Marina: "Ew, no! Gross, gross, gross!"
*She wrinkles up her face as she says it.*
*I giggle, but then turn serious real fast.*
Amber: "Okay, but listen."
Amber: "Because it’s dark, the group probably isn’t gonna stop to look at the Mummy Cave Ruins for very long. So we’ll have to wait until we can sneak away again, and then go over there ourselves."
Marina: "How far away are they?"
Amber: "No idea. I can’t see from here. Just follow my lead again."
Marina: "Got it."
*We’ve been at the very back of the group the entire time. The people right in front of us are much less interested in us than Linda was, so I don’t think anyone will notice if we go missing.*
*When we reach a railing, the guide stops to point out the ruins. They’re across a small valley, a couple hundred yards away. In the darkness, it’s almost impossible to see them.*
*They’re just a dark smudge against the even darker backdrop of a mountain.*
*Marina and I are a few yards away from the group, too.*
*I pretend to inspect something on a rock, though there’s nothing there.*
*As the short speech ends and the guide starts to lead everyone on again, I dart behind a rock and crouch down. Marina quickly follows.*
*I can see her start to say something, but I cup my hand over her mouth before she can get a word out. Silently, I shake my head.*
*Marina nods twice, so I uncover her face.*
*In my head, I count to 60. Once a minute has passed, I peel myself away from the rock and stand back up.*
Amber: "We should be safe now."
*Marina’s face lights up and she claps her hands. I notice that she’s taken out the journal page with the hiding spot again.*
Marina: "That was so cool! It was like something out of a video game, where you just sneak away right in front of everyone!"
*This close up, I can see her face fall at that.*
Amber: "Don’t worry, I’ll be right beside you."
Marina: "Are we just going to hop the fence and walk over there?"
Amber: "Unless you have any better ideas."
Marina: "What if they catch us?"
Amber: "Then they’ll throw us out."
Marina: "We’re not gonna get arrested for trespassing or anything, are we?"
Amber: "Nah, that’d be too much trouble for them."
*I say that, although I don’t actually know.*
Marina: "Okay…"
*The uncertainty in her voice is obvious.*
*I grab the paper from her hands and stuff it into my pocket.*
Amber: "Let’s go. The longer we wait, the more likely it is that they'll notice us missing."
*I stroll over to the metal rail, hoping that I sound more confident than I am. Honestly, this is pretty risky, and more than a little stupid.*
*But it’s also exciting.*
