Amber: "Um, maybe when I was a little kid? I don’t know."
*She slides her giant cup towards me, some sloshing over the sides in her haste.*
Marina: "Try it."
*Dredges of milk surround the rim of the cup from Marina’s sipping. Across from me, she waits, looking eager. I’m surprised she’s not bouncing like normal.*
*After sighing exaggeratedly, I pick up the cup and down a large gulp, trying not to think silly, cliche thoughts about "indirect kisses."*
*To my surprise, the drink is… Really good.*
*It’s got a deeper, rich flavor than the stuff you get in jugs at the store. I can tell that they made it themselves in the kitchen.*
*It’s not overwhelmingly sweet. In fact, it’s perfect.*
*Damn. I don’t know if I’m disappointed or not. Giving her the satisfaction of being right...*
*My pride aches.*
*As I push the cup back towards Marina, she receives it with a sly smirk.*
Marina: "Soooo?"
*She draws out the word, already knowing my answer.*
Amber: "It wasn’t bad."
*That’s all I’m willing to concede.*
*She laughs and takes another huge sip. After setting the cup down, she has a thick milk mustache on her upper lip. I watch with inappropriate fascination as her tongue comes out and cleans it away.*
*Marina grabs the sheet she was coloring and then pushes that over to me as well.*
Marina: "Wanna color something?"
Marina: "While you’re acting like a kid and all."
*I roll my eyes but snatch away the crayons that she offers.*
*Most of the pictures - all of them breakfast foods with faces and dated outfits - have already been colored in.*
*There’s a stylish looking piece of bread with a dress and a wig that’s still blank, though. I color the dress with the green crayon as lightly as I can, and use the lighter shade of red for the hair.*
*When I’m done, I hold up the page to show Marina.*
Amber: "It’s you."
*She leans in.*
Marina: "Huh?"
Amber: "The colors. Look, you’re toast."
*She collapses backwards as if my pun physically wounded her. She shakes her head and looks down while I crack up.*
Marina: "That was even worse than ‘Sam-I-Amber.’"
Amber: "Uh huh. You butter step your game up some."
Marina: "Please stop."
*I don’t plan to. The only thing that saves her is the arrival of our waitress right then.*
*That’s pretty fast service, considering how busy this place is.*
*Keeping in style with our oversized beverages, the portions are enormous.*
*The plate that Marina’s pancakes are served on is so large, it looks like it could’ve been used as a shield back in medieval times. And the pancakes form a tower that’s probably half a foot tall.*
Waitress: "Can I get you girls anything else?"
*Marina shakes her head, but I look down at the table as I mumble:*
Amber: "Can I get a glass of chocolate milk, please?"
*As the waitress departs yet again, I glance over, prepared for the inevitable teasing. But instead, Marina just stares at her food, dumbstruck.*
Marina: "...Maybe I should've gotten the kids' size."
*I finish about half of my meal before the dismay sets in. There’s no way I can possibly finish this. Most people I know couldn’t eat this much food in one sitting.*
*Every bite is a struggle.*
*Marina’s faring similarly. Her plate of eggs is nearly untouched, just because there’s so much pancake to work through.*
*And I can tell that pancake fatigue - that feeling where you get halfway through them and then realize you’re sick of pancakes - has long since set in for her.*
*She cuts off a bite, looks at it for a while, then sets her fork down.*
Marina: "I think I’m done."
Amber: "Yeah. You and me both."
*The largest plate I have consists of a mess of potatoes, cheese and bacon, with the eggs laid over it.*
*And then of course, there was the toast, fruit and sausage, which each got their own smaller plate. I’d have ordered from the kids’ menu too if I’d known this was how it would be.*
Marina: "I don’t think I’ll ever want pancakes again."
*For once, I don’t have any snarky remarks. Anyone would be sick of them after what she just went through.*
Amber: "We’ll get some boxes to go. At least we have food for a week now if we ever get stranded."
*After our waitress brings us our bill and styrofoam containers, we head back out to the RV.*
*It looks like traffic hasn’t gotten much better in the forty-five minutes that we’ve been out of it.*
*Cars are still pressed bumper-to-bumper. The sidewalks are also full, with swarms of pedestrians making better time than the traffic. I wonder if it’s always this busy, or if people are just deciding not to drive.*
*Once we’re ready to go, I take us over to the edge of the parking lot. As expected, the constant stream of cars makes it impossible for us to get out. I watch impatiently for an opening in traffic, but can’t see one for miles.*
Amber: "Oh, come on!"
*I press my forehead to the steering wheel. We’ve been sitting here for minutes now.*
Marina: "Is there another exit we could use? Onto a different street?"
*I look and check the mirrors, but don’t see anything. The edges of the diner’s property are surrounded by a cream-colored fence.*
Amber: "Nope."
*Marina begins to sigh, but that quickly turns into a sound of surprise.*
Marina: "Amber, look!"
*She points past my face and out the window. An older lady in a little hatchback sedan has stopped to let us out.*
*The two of us wave at her as we finally lumber onto the road itself.*
Marina: "Yay! I’m glad somebody was nice, at least."
Amber: "Yeah. Still a long way to go, though."
*The long line of cars extends as far as I can see, before it finally goes out of sight around a bend in the road.*
Marina: "Why do you think it’s so much busier here than the other places we’ve been?"
Amber: "Arches is one of the most popular national parks. Like, when you think of parks in the US, you think of Arches and the Grand Canyon and Yosemite. That kind of thing."
Amber: "So I think a lot of people will be at those big spots."
Marina: "Think it’ll be like Shiprock again?"
Amber: "What, with the megaphone and law enforcement?"
Marina: "Mhm."
Amber: "Maybe. Probably not. These parks are a lot bigger and more spread out. Way harder to have people watching. Their only option would be to straight up close."
Marina: "And then you have horrible people who sneak in or out of bounds anyway."
Amber: "Yeah, exactly. Who would ever do that?"
Marina: "Nobody we know, that’s for sure."
Amber: "It’s okay, once we’re filthy rich we can make a donation to the Canyon or something."
Marina: "Let’s just buy the canyon and then pay to have everything reconstructed."
Amber: "Perfect."
*It takes a while, but we finally make it past all of the stoplights. After that, it’s still slow going, but at least it’s steady. Maybe fifteen minutes pass crawling through town before things start to pick up.*
*Still a couple miles away, I can finally see the exit for the park itself. Some people continue past, staying on the highway, but most are in line for Arches. At least there’s a whole lane for people like us.*
*Traffic is congested like my nose in winter, but the end is in sight.*
Amber: "May as well get comfy. We’re gonna be here a while."
*Taking my advice, Marina leans back and props her feet up on the dash. Her dress bunches up by her knees, leaving most of her legs exposed.*
*I sneak a few guilty glances from the corner of my eye. If I didn’t know better, I’d think that all the skin she shows was intentional. But it’s Marina, which means she’s not even conscious about this stuff.*
*Or me, for that matter. I wonder if she knows that I’m… into her.*
*To distract myself, I grab the box of tapes.*
*I kept the music off earlier so that Marina could sleep.*
Amber: "Wanna pick this time?"
Marina: "Sure."
*Setting it in her lap, she begins to rummage through the bin.*
*I’m pretty sure that she knows all of the music I have by now. We’ve listened to every tape at least once. And I have an okay feel for what she likes and what she doesn’t.*
*Sometimes she taps her fingers to the beat or hums along when she likes what we’re listening to. If she doesn’t, she flat out ignores it.*
*The one she settles on is an album of instrumental folk songs. Very stereotypical western-sounding.*
*It's a little high-energy for just sitting still in traffic, but that's alright. It's better than nothing.*
*We just listen to the music, which I’ve cranked up loud, while we wait. The album is nearly half over by the time we finally make it through the park gates.*
*Beyond them, traffic mercifully lets up some. Not because there are fewer cars - there are more cars than rocks to be seen.*
*But a lot of those are pulled off on the shoulder, or cramped into parking lots. Nearly every inch of pavement that doesn’t have a car on it has a person instead.*
*Also, there are different trails and forks in the road, so not everyone is going in the same direction now. Being able to drive without having to tap the brakes every couple seconds is a relief.*
*A pickup truck towing a trailer pulls off the shoulder ahead and back onto the road, so I take his spot.*
*Putting it in park, I take a swig of stone-cold coffee.*
Amber: "Wanna grab the pamphlets?"
*We snagged a couple of brochures back at the diner. They don’t have a whole lot of info, but they’re a starting point.*
*Marina retrieves them and spreads them out on the table like puzzle pieces. Alongside them is the journal. Its spine has lost all strength and the pages just flop around.*
Amber: "So the journal says that it’s buried between two things."
Marina: "Right. But something out of the way."
*Peering out the windshield and to the expanses beyond… There’s a lot that could be considered "out of the way."*
Amber: "It’d have to be something memorable, or else he’d come back and have no idea where to go."
Marina: "And anything memorable is probably gonna be something popular, right?"
Amber: "Exactly, yeah."
*She flips through some of the pamphlets. They all have the same information on the same landmarks, just with slightly different pictures.*
Amber: "Wait."
*She was about to toss one of them, but it catches my eye.*
Amber: "Hand me that."
*Marina passes over the brochure, and I turn to one of the inner pages. It has only one picture, and a longer paragraph of text. I read aloud.*
Amber: "One of the most famous landmarks in all of the park, Double Arch is a fan-favorite. Recognizable for its two elegant curves, Double Arch can be accessed via a short hike and is open to exploration!"
*I hold up the flyer so she can see the picture. It’s of two huge arches of stone, connected at one side but otherwise perpendicular from each other.*
*She snatches it out of my hands for a closer look, nearly cutting my fingers.*
Marina: "Oh my God, you think it’s between the two of these?"
Amber: "That’d make sense, right? You’d definitely remember how it looks. And it’s kind of out of the way."
*The papers whiz past my face as Marina hurls them backwards and she points forward.*
Marina: "Full steam ahead!"
*I throw the RV back into drive and pull out onto the road again. There are signs everywhere for Double Arch, so we don’t need the map that we got at the gate.*
*We pass signs for other landmarks too, some with pretty weird names. There’s the Parade of Elephants, for one thing, which is in the same direction as Ham Rock.*
*There’s even a phallic-sounding rock simply called The Organ.*
*All of those are down exits we don't take. Instead, we soon end up in the back of yet another traffic jam.*
*Marina stares out the window as we creep along. Where we are right now is nearly indistinguishable from the highways we’ve spent so much time on.*
*There are a few actual arches around, but none distinct enough to get any real attention.*
*Inch by inch, we advance down the road until we finally come to the parking lot for Double Arch.*
*Only problem is, it’s totally full.*
*Not just busy, but packed. Every parking spot is taken, with a few cars jammed into empty space that’s clearly not meant for that.*
*Like lice, people swarm up and down the arch itself, visible even from the road. At least this time it isn’t off-limits.*
Marina: "Looks like everyone else had the same idea."
Amber: "Don’t sound so glum. We can still go take a look."
Marina: "If we can ever find somewhere to park."
Amber: "Yeah, that is a pretty big 'if.'"
*The entire caravan passes by the parking lot. There’s at least a dozen cars behind me, too.*
*Maybe a half mile up the road, we come across another lot.*
Amber: "Up for a bit of walking?"
Marina: "Yeah!"
*I quickly take one of the remaining spaces. There are a few openings here. Looks like most people weren’t as willing to walk as Marina is.*
*Some of the cars behind us pull in, too.*
*We opt to leave the metal detector and other equipment in the RV for now. If we see anything that looks promising, we can come back and grab it.*
*The walk back up to Double Arch doesn’t take long. And it’s nice to be outdoors again.*
*We have to stick to the very edge of the road to avoid the stream of cars flowing towards us. Seems there was more than a dozen behind us after all.*
*There’s a short hike from the Double Arch parking lot to the arch itself. It’s an easy one, and we don’t even break a sweat.*
*Up close, it’s obvious that there aren’t many people here just to see the sights. Almost everyone is carrying a shovel or other tool.*
*There’s a pair of park rangers watching, but they don’t seem to be intervening at all. They just slouch against a wooden fence, looking defeated.*
Amber: "Can you imagine how big of celebrities we’d be if anyone here knew we found the Shiprock treasure?"
*I whisper as quietly as I can to Marina.*
Marina: "Right? I was thinking about that earlier. I kinda wanted to show off, but that’s probably not a good idea."
Amber: "Like anyone would believe us, anyway. But you don’t want to have your face all over the New York Times as one of the girls who found the treasure?"
Marina: "Hmm… Being in the local newspaper is on my bucket list."
*Dream big, Marina.*
Marina: "And my friends from school would totally have a heart attack when they found out."
Marina: "But don’t they always say to keep it a secret if you win the lottery?"
Amber: "Yeah, otherwise people will never leave you alone. You’ll get all sorts of messages from people you used to know or long lost relatives."
Marina: "Ugh, I have enough not-lost relatives to deal with."
Marina: "Plus, I don’t wanna get kidnapped and held for ransom or something like that."
Marina: "What about you?"
Marina: "Um…"
Amber: "Yeah, this is a bit of a mess."
Marina: "Maybe we should come back later?"
*I don’t know if it’ll be any better later, but maybe.*
Amber: "I guess. Though if the treasure is here, I feel like someone would’ve found it by now."
*Just like back at Canyon de Chelly.*
Amber: "You know this must have been going on for a few days now."
Marina: "Yeah…"
*All the enthusiasm she had just a minute ago when we were joking around has evaporated in the desert sun.*
*A rock skips past my foot as she kicks at the dirt.*
*I poke her, and she yelps in surprise.*
Amber: "Hey now. I’m not saying we give up. I’m just saying we regroup and strategize. We can go see the rest of the park in the meantime."
Amber: "Didn’t you say you’ve wanted to see this place for a while?"
Marina: "Yeah, I guess."
*It’s obvious that she’s still bummed, but there's nothing I can do about that.*
*Once we’re back in the motorhome, we leave the busy main road ASAP and spend a while seeing the sights from our air conditioned comfort.*
*Marina’s mood improves steadily throughout the day, counteracted only by our growing hunger.*
*As we pull back into Moab, we're both ravenous.*
Amber: "Wanna just heat up our leftovers?"
Marina: "Hm…"
Amber: "Guessing that’s a no?"
Marina: "I’m not ready for pancakes again yet. I need a day. Or maybe a week."
Amber: "Lemme know if you see somewhere you want me to stop."
*Now that it’s approaching evening, there aren’t any more massive traffic jams going through Moab. We can cruise through at a steady pace, just like when we first got into Arches.*
*Until I slam on the brakes." with vpunch
Amber: "You’re kidding me."
*Marina had been slouching, but bolts upright at the sudden stop.*
Marina: "What happened?"
*Just a few feet ahead, in the parking lot of the diner we ate at this morning, is Mariah’s motorhome.*
*I point, and Marina leans forward. When she recognizes their RV, she grins.*
Marina: "Should we go see them?"
Marina: "Aw, but Amber. Maybe we could see about teaming up for the treasure here or something."
Amber: "That’d never happen. Mariah would rather lose every time than have our help."
Amber: "Besides, it’s not like-"
Marina: "Pleeeeease?"
*Already giving up in my head, I give Marina one last desperate look.*
Amber: "Seriously?"
Marina: "Yeah! Even if we don’t team up, it’d be nice to see them again."
Amber: "Not sure if 'nice' is the word I’d use…"
*Despite my muttering, I flick on my blinker and change lanes, pulling into the parking lot yet again.*
*Like this morning, it seems pretty busy. I have to park next to Mariah’s RV because it’s the only open spot large enough.*
*Marina skips out and towards the entrance while I trudge behind her.*
*It doesn’t take long to find the Trio. Even amongst all the eccentric people that the hunt has drawn, their questionable fashion sense stands out.*
*They have a large table near the back; bigger than the one Marina and I ate at this morning. Joseph and Tess share a bench, while Mariah’s spread across the opposite side.*
*It’s probably supposed to look sultry, but it comes across more like the bench is devouring her.*
*When she sees them, Marina scurries to the back, hand raised in greeting. I bring up the rear, still slow.*
Marina: "Hey guys!"
*Tess and Joseph both wave, the latter smiling when he sees us. Mariah looks surprised for a second, but then scowls and turns towards the wall.*
*The feeling is mutual.*
Joseph: "Hey, imagine running into you two here. Just get into town?"
Marina: "Nope, we’ve been here all day."
Marina: "We actually ate breakfast at this place this morning!"
Amber: "Yeah, you’re always a couple steps behind us, I guess."
*Mariah’s glare is stonier than Double Arch itself. But of course she keeps looking at the wall, instead of us.*
*Joseph laughs, though.*
Joseph: "Here, let's break bread."
*He and Tess scoot down the booth, making some room which Marina promptly fills.*
*I’m left next to the smoldering fury that is Mariah.*
*She moves her leg, just a little, but enough for me to sit down.*
Amber: "Thanks. Sorry to barge in on you guys like this."
Joseph: "Ah, it’s no problem. More the merrier."
Mariah: "I hope you don’t expect us to cover your bill."
*Groaning, Joe puts his hands over Tess' ears.*
Joseph: "Could you at least try not to be a pain in the ass between the hours of 5pm and 6 in the morning? It would make my life a lot easier."
*He uncovers Tess' ears and gives us a blank look.*
Joseph: "She's right, though. There's no way we can afford that."
Tess: "We had to dine and dash for breakfast."
Joseph: "We got in around lunchtime. Spent forever and a half stuck in traffic though."
*He sounds worn out. I wonder if Mariah ever lets him drive. Or makes him.*
Amber: "Us too. We were gonna look around Double Arch but it was too busy."
Mariah: "You weren’t supposed to hear that!"
Joseph: "I told you, this girl hears everything."
Marina: "We were gonna do that too! I was telling Amber that we should see if maybe you guys wanted to team up and we could look together."
*The two of us look at each other. Seems all that we ever agree on is not sticking together.*
Amber: "Uh, no offense to you, Joe."
Amber: "Just didn’t wanna… You know…"
*I trail off, trying to think of an excuse that isn’t 'didn’t wanna deal with Mariah.' Which is the truth.*
*But Joseph just laughs and waves it off.*
Joseph: "Nah, I get what you mean. Don’t think Mariah would be too fond of splitting the goods either."
*Marina frowns, but perks back up after just a second.*
*Soon, a waitress comes to take our order. Marina and I are careful to order meals on the smaller side: just sandwiches with fries.*
*On the other hand, Mariah’s order comes with a bunch of requirements as to what can and can’t be on it.*
*By the time she’s made so many substitutions and extras, it hardly resembles the item described in the menu.*
Joseph: "So, I can tell you ladies that it’s not at the Parade of Elephants, and Delicate Arch was a no-go too. Think we might try Skyline next."
Amber: "Good luck, I guess. Wonder how many of those the miner would’ve known about though. Pretty sure this park wasn’t founded 'til after the Gold Rush."
*Joseph shrugs.*
Marina: "Amber knows a lot about these places! She’s like an encyclopedia."
*I can feel my face flush like always when she praises me.*
Amber: "Not really. I just know bits and pieces."
Joseph: "Still something to be proud of. Aren't too many girls your age who care about anything that happened more than five years ago."
Amber: "It’s really nothing special. I just spent a lot of time on the road when I was younger."
Joseph: "That so?"
*I appreciate his leaving it as an open-ended question, rather than prying. Marina looks at me, eyes wide, probably to see if I’m about to cry or something.*
*I try to be nonchalant.*
Amber: "Yeah, until I was in middle school. My grandpa used to drive me all over the country."
Joseph: "Sounds like a fun time."
Amber: "It was."
Amber: "He uh…"
*Guess there’s no point in hiding it. Plus, Joseph has this kind of disarming air to him. Like he exudes calmness.*
Amber: "Passed away a little while ago. Still a little…"
*I tap my throat, hoping that gets my point across.*
*It does. Joseph nods.*
Joseph: "I’m sorry for your loss. I know how hard that can be..."
Amber: "Thanks."
*Marina’s eyes flick between me and Joseph. She must be pretty worried. So I force a smile, even though I can feel that growing glumness that shows up anytime I talk about Gramps.*
Amber: "Meeting this dork has helped a lot, though."
*I jerk my chin towards Marina, who looks shocked. As if I could’ve been talking about anyone else.*
Amber: "Spent a while moping around before that."
Tess: "Family is good."
*I blink. It’s so easy to forget she’s there.*
Amber: "Yeah, definitely."
*I hadn’t really thought of it before, but Tess seems to be in a similar situation I was. Growing up on the go and all.*
*I’m curious how it happened, but I won’t ask. Not my place.*
*A far-off look seeps onto Joe's face. He stares out the window, like he's gazing at something one hundred yards away.*
*Mariah glances up at him. For a split second — if that — she even looks a little concerned. But then it's right back to business as usual.*
*She leers over at me and shoves herself back into the conversation.*
Mariah: "So what are you going to do?"
Amber: "Huh?"
Mariah: "When all this is over."
*She gestures around.*
Amber: "Dinner?"
Mariah: "No, dumbass! I meant the treasure hunt."
Mariah: "Forget it. I don’t actually care."
Amber: "Dunno yet. Mare and I have been kind of talking about that."
*Three of us chuckle, but then the conversation trails off. Tess and Marina are working on coloring Tess's menu. From what I can see, it has different characters than the one this morning.*
*They look like they make a good team. Each of them work on separate pictures, but switch from time to time.*
*So Marina will color the hair on one character, then Tess colors the clothes. Despite not talking at all, they don’t ever get in each other’s way.*
*Joseph also observes from above, smiling slightly. He’s usually the one to play with Tess, so it must be nice for her to have some company.*
*On the other hand, like a jug of milk, Mariah’s mood seems to be getting more and more sour.*
*I don’t even know what she’s mad about this time. Maybe something happened before we showed up. Or maybe it’s because we showed up.*
*Either way, she ought to have memorized every single detail of the wall she’s been staring at this entire time.*
*She doesn’t look up until our food arrives a little bit later.*
*It’s a good thing we have a large table, because there’s still hardly any room left once all the plates are set down.*
*My sandwich is so big, it takes two hands to hold. And I have enough fries to sustain Ireland for the winter.*
*When Marina picks up her sandwich, all the ingredients pour out the back. She quickly gives up trying to reconstruct it and just uses a knife and fork instead.*
*Watching the other three eat is amusing, too. Joseph chews with slow, measured bites whereas Mariah attacks her meal like it owes her money.*
*Across from her, Tess is cutting up chicken tenders with surgical precision.*
*Marina catches me staring and smiles at me covertly. Seems she noticed too.*
*Mariah's the first to finish eating.*
*I'm just a couple bites behind. That's embarrassing.*
*She wipes her face with a napkin then finally looks at me.*
Mariah: "Where are you two staying?"
*It could be an innocent question, or it could be a trap.*
Amber: "Around. We’ll find a parking lot somewhere."
Mariah: "You thieves are gonna have a late start, then."
Amber: "I guess. Don’t tell me you planned ahead and got a campsite."
Mariah: "Don't be stupid. Of course not."
Mariah: "Wait, that’s not what I meant."
*I scoff as she backtracks to keep from insulting herself.*
Mariah: "What I mean is, there’s no way they’re gonna bother checking every single permit. There’s too much going on right now."
Mariah: "Just pick a nice spot out of the way, don’t make trouble, and it’ll be fine."
*Joe flatly stares at Mariah.*
Joseph: "Easier said than done."
Amber: "Good luck with that. I don’t wanna take the risk."
*On foot, if you’re trespassing and get caught, most of the time you’ll just get thrown out.*
*But I don’t want our license plate getting written down or something if we were to do what she suggests.*
*Mariah shrugs.*
Mariah: "Suit yourself. Don’t say I didn’t never try to help you."
Joseph: "You ever camp here before, Amber? Sounded like you know the place."
Amber: "Long time ago. I don’t remember a whole lot. Just good at remembering names, I guess."
Joseph: "Useful skill to have."
Amber: "Yeah. Used to be, before we’d go anywhere, Gramps and I would research the place we were going. History and stuff like that."
Joseph: "You sure learned a lot of useful stuff from him, huh?"
Amber: "Yep, always wanted to make him proud. He used to say I was a real chip off the…"
*My words catch in my throat. Not because I’m emotional, but because I just realized something. Remembered it, rather.*
*Mariah and Joe know it too. Mine and Mariah's eyes lock, and she bolts up at my words. The other two look confused.*
*Mariah sprints for the door without paying and I nearly yank Marina’s arm out of its socket as we run to the exit. Other diners turn to watch us, but I barely notice them.*
Marina: "Amber! What the heck?"
*Her voice is shaky from being jolted around.*
Amber: "I know where the treasure is."
*I yell behind me as I turn to look, and see the Trio exit the restaurant close behind us. Joe’s carrying Tess on his back.*
Amber: "I know where it is."
*I say it again to assure myself.*
*We reach the motorhome seconds after Mariah and her crew reach theirs. I practically hurl Marina up and into her spot before I leap into my own.*
*Not even bothering to buckle up, I hurry towards the road.*
*Less than a minute, and we’re already falling behind. I watch Mariah’s taillights as she speeds up the road, horn blaring to scare away the cars unfortunate enough to be in her lane.*
*I’m going as fast as I dare to, worried about cops that could be lurking around one of the bends in the road.*
*But I can’t let her out of my sights. There’s no room to pass yet, but I have to watch for an opportunity.*
*With one hand, Marina clutches the headrest bar of her seat. Then she leans over and past my face, nearly causing me to swerve from shock.*
Amber: "What are you-"
*She grabs my seatbelt and buckles me in. Then she does the same for herself.*
Marina: "Okay, now that we’re at least not gonna die if you hit something…"
Marina: "Can you tell me what exactly is happening?"
*She sounds a little frantic, and a little curious.*
Amber: "Okay, so, do you remember Balanced Rock?"
*It wasn’t one of the places that we visited earlier today, but it’s one of the spots that’s on posters and in lots of the ads.*
Marina: "I think so. Or at least hearing about it."
Amber: "There used to be another really famous rock right next to it. Smaller, but a similar shape and everything. They called it 'Chip Off the Old Block.'"
Marina: "What do you mean ‘used to be?’"
Amber: "I was getting to that. A long time ago, like 50 years or something, it fell. Now you can’t even tell it apart from the rest of the rocks on the ground."
Marina: "Fifty years… So that was…"
*Her eyes go wide in understanding, a fact which I look away from Mariah’s rear to take in.*
Marina: "So it was still standing when the miner came through!"
Amber: "Yeah, exactly. I’ll bet you anything he hid the treasure between Balanced Rock and what used to be Chip."
*I look over again to smile at her, only to see genuine admiration and awe in her face. Her eyes are wide, and her mouth slightly open.*
*Like always, I can’t look at her for too long, or it starts to make me uncomfortable. Nothing I’ve said or done is that impressive. Even Mariah knew about Chip.*
*But instead of singing my praises again, she just faces ahead with a little smile.*
*Then her face crumples up.*
Marina: "Amber!"
Amber: "I see it."
*A semi has begun pulling out into the road ahead of us. There’s no passing lane, so I can’t get around him that way.*
Amber: "Dammit!"
*I tap the brakes, since I’ll have to stop and wait for him to get onto the highway.*
*Ahead, I can still see Mariah, but just barely. A few of the cars between us have exited, but her lead has grown.*
*And so I make a quick, reckless decision.*
Amber: "Hold onto something."
Marina: "What are you-"
*I don’t wait for her to finish her question, since she grabs onto her seat as she asks it.*
*To answer, I yank the wheel to the left and into the other lane, even though it’s a no-pass zone." with vpunch
*Headlights far ahead are speeding toward us, maybe a quarter mile away.*
*Getting closer.*
*A concrete barrier prevents me from going any further to the left. I smash the gas pedal to the floor, putting all my weight on it.*
*Marina begins to moan with dread at the oncoming car. It’s a wordless sound, or maybe the start of my name.*
*My heart is in my throat and I can feel beads of sweat on my forehead. One slides down into my eye but I can’t wipe it away.*
*We’re even with the semi now, who seems to be stopping. He probably noticed me and is waiting for me to complete my illegal pass.*
*The car ahead of us is close enough that I can see it in our headlight beams; it’s a maroon SUV. They’re braking as they approach.*
*But I’m still accelerating, going so fast that I couldn’t stop in time even if I wanted to.*
*Like a missile, we shoot forward. I’m at least double the speed limit right now.*
*We come even with the front of the semi, which is hardly rolling.*
*The SUV has come to a complete stop, but there’s barely any room between it and the semi now.*
*And then suddenly we’re past the cab of the truck.*
*I wrench the wheel to the right; hard right, throwing us back into our own lane.*
*We zoom past the stopped SUV, who gives me a well-deserved horn blaring. The bassy sound of the semi’s horn also booms behind us.*
*But it doesn’t matter, because we’re okay. We didn’t die.*
*A need to breathe hits me, and I realize that I’ve been holding it in.*
*I sigh, adrenaline still coursing through me.*
Marina: "Oh my god, Amber!"
*Her voice cracks, and she starts to laugh. Hysterically.*
*I do too, laughing away the anxiety of the last half-minute. It felt like much longer.*
*And it’s not entirely over yet.*
Amber: "Don’t get too comfy."
*Her hands shoot back to where she was hanging on.*
Marina: "Uh oh."
*Just ahead, Mariah still has a lead but it’s shrinking quickly. I wonder if she saw how I caught up.*
*Jamming the gas pedal down again, I start to narrow the distance even more." with vpunch
*She takes the exit for Arches just a few seconds ahead of me. We blaze through the entry point, flashing our passes at rangers who barely have time to check them.*
*Even in the darkness, there are lots of people on the sides of the road.*
*I wonder if anyone else has had the same realization we did, about Chip.*
*Mariah’s starting to gain ground again, so I mash the accelerator farther down. But her motorhome is newer than mine, and it’s hard for me to keep up.*
*If I’m going to beat her there, I’ll have to do it soon.*
*I check to make sure that Marina’s still holding on tight.*
*She is.*
*Just a few feet behind Mariah’s RV, I illegally swing into the other lane yet again.*
*This is even more risky because of the way the road curves all over the canyon. An oncoming car could round a bend and completely blindside us.*
*Hope that doesn’t happen.*
*By pushing the RV to its absolute limits, I’m able to break even with Mariah. Looking over, I can see a wild flair in her eyes, hair disheveled. Her expression changes to one of shock when she notices us beside her.*
*I relish in the look of disbelief as I nudge the wheel to the right.*
*There’s a godawful scraping as the motorhome brushes against Mariah’s. Her eyes, as well as Marina’s, widen.*
*Seconds after we pull apart, Mariah’s vehicle lurches towards us, and again there’s a sound that makes me want to rip my ears off. I fight to keep control of the motorhome and stay on the road.*
*Awestruck bystanders watch as we pass by, but I hardly register them.*
*Still neck and neck, we continue toward Balanced Rock.*
*Through insane luck or divine intervention or something, the road ahead is clear.*
*I won't try to shove them off the road again, because there are now steep dropoffs that could actually be fatal.*
*But I do have to do something to gain an edge. Just arriving seconds before her isn't enough.*
*A sign, visible for just a split second, informs us that there are five miles to Balanced Rock.*
*What might have been an arrow for an exit has been scuffed to illegibility.*
*Maybe that's our chance.*
*Up ahead, barely illuminated by a flickering lamp, the road splits into two directions. A sign in the middle is smashed and folded in on itself, probably the result of someone's reckless driving.*
*One of these paths leads to Balanced Rock, but I don't know which. I'm willing to bet Mariah doesn't either, though.*
*From Marina's window, I can see Mariah shoot me one final death glare, before they rocket off down the exit.*
*We stay on the other branch, though I'm back in the proper lane now.*
*Just gotta hope it's the right branch.*
*As I start to calm down yet again, I realize my legs are shaking.*
*Marina cranes around to watch Mariah’s vehicle, which is quickly disappearing into the murk. Once it’s fully out of sight, she turns back to me, her face as white as her knuckles.*
Amber: "You uh… You okay?"
*Guilt stabs me - I may have seriously traumatized her. With two incidents. Within minutes of each other.*
*After taking a deep breath, she unclenches her fists.*
Marina: "Can we never, ever do that again?"
Marina: "That was insane. Super insane. Like, oh my god. I thought I was going to die."
Marina: "Wait."
Marina: "Did we... Did we die?"
Marina: "Am I just imagining this now?"
Amber: "I don’t think you could imagine stuff if you were dead, kid."
Marina: "That’s the kind of thing a dead person would say!"
Marina: "Like in that one movie, where it was all about the guy who could talk to dead people, except-"
Amber: "Fine, I’m ninety percent sure we’re not dead, then."
Marina: "Okay."
Marina: "Okay. Ninety percent."
Marina: "That’s not bad."
*Our voices are shaky, even though our conversation isn’t serious. I keep checking the rearview, expecting to see the headlights of Mariah’s approaching vengeance, but so far we’re in the clear.*
*Each passing minute that we don't see the Rock itself, though, makes me worry that we took the wrong turn.*
*Right on the verge of turning around and tailing Mariah, another sign suddenly looms up ahead of us.*
*We’ve reached Balanced Rock.*
*I slow down as we approach the parking area.*
*While not empty, there are far fewer people here than there were at Double Arch earlier today.*
*A good number of them look like they might just be sightseers, since they’re standing around talking or looking at the stars instead of searching.*
*When I stand up, my legs wobble. I stumble, grabbing Marina’s shoulder for support. She smiles at me, but doesn’t say anything.*
*No more jokes to crack.*
*It’s time to get serious.*
*We grab our equipment and hurry out into the night air.*
*Solid ground feels foreign after the rocketing motorhome. This must be what it feels like to return from a voyage at sea.*
*Marina, the landlubber, doesn’t seem to be having any trouble now that we’re not barrelling along the highway.*
*The spring in her step has returned, and I worry that she’s going to bang the metal detector against the ground as she half-walks, half-skips.*
*Some of the other people watch us go, but no one follows or says anything. They probably assume we’re just next in the line of failures.*
*A line which must be pretty long, given how much trash and misplaced earth there is around here.*
*Pangs of guilt remind me yet again of the broken brick at Canyon de Chelly, and I make my umpteenth resolution to be more careful.*
*Just ahead, Marina’s come to a stop. Head tilted back, I can tell she’s admiring Balanced Rock.*
*It’s certainly an impressive view.*
*Someday, I’d like to take her back to all these places we’ve gone, but without the pressure of the treasure hunt rushing us. Just taking in the sights.*
*I slow my steps a little, so I can keep watching her from behind.*
*Maybe sensing me, she spins around and waves.*
Marina: "Hurry up!"
*Yelling, even though I’m close enough to hear her normal volume level.*
*I pick up the pace once more.*
Marina: "So, where was Chip?"
Amber: "Over there, to the right."
*There’s a large stone wall besides Balanced Rock itself, with a short slope between them.*
Amber: "It was right at the edge of that little hill."
Marina: "So where that pile of rocks is now?"
Amber: "Yep, that’s the spot."
*You can’t tell that there used to be anything there. But I’ve seen pictures in the atlases Gramps kept around, or in the history books he liked to read.*
*Compared to Balanced Rock, Chip Off the Old Block looked tiny. But it was actually a decent size.*
*There’s a pile of stones at the base that are probably Chip’s remains. They’re indistinguishable from the rest of the red rocks, though.*
*Some are as big as my head, or even bigger.*
*It’s hard to tell if anyone else has picked through here. But since there aren’t any shovel marks or holes, I’m guessing not.*
Marina: "Where do we start?"
*I think for a moment.*
Amber: "First, let’s get the easy stuff out of the way. You go check that side with the metal detector, and I’ll check this one."
*We sweep over the ground between the hill where Chip once stood and the massive stone wall beside it.*
*Nothing.*
Amber: "Okay, that’s pretty much what I expected."
*I lean my metal detector against the wall and go back to the pile of rocks.*
Amber: "Just check over the places that I uncover, okay?"
*Marina nods, holding her detector at the ready. She reminds me of a surgeon, about to perform an operation.*
*I roll aside some of the rocks with my feet, but most are so big that I have to stoop down and use my hands.*
*There are more of them than I thought, and I’m soon sweaty and sore.*
Marina: "Do you want any help?"
Amber: "Nah."
*I’m panting.*
Amber: "Just keep scanning."
*She does so, dutifully retracing my steps. Still, nothing.*
*After a couple minutes, I stand up and take a swig from a water bottle. I’m glad I remembered to bring them this time.*
*Marina sets down her metal detector to do the same.*
Amber: "Why wouldn’t they be?"
Marina: "Don’t you think Mariah’s gonna flip out when she realizes she took the wrong turn?"
Amber: "Probably. That’s her fault, though."
Marina: "Not like you would’ve let her go down this side of the road!"
Amber: "Hey, I did what I had to do."
Amber: "If we get the next treasure, too, I won’t have to."
Amber: "Right. When."
*Only one more to go after this. One more frantic search and then…*
*I drain my water bottle and crumple it up before shoving it into my pocket.*
Amber: "Break’s over. Back to work."
*I pop my back and resume moving rocks out of the way. I’ve been working from the outer edge of the rock pile inward, since they’re more concentrated in the center.*
*Like a shadow, Marina continues to stick to me, going where I go.*
*Nearly ten minutes of uninterrupted shuffling pass. By now, I’m expecting Mariah to have caught up, or to get here soon.*
*But she never arrives. Maybe she just gave up.*
*Although, knowing Mariah, that’s just wishful thinking.*
*I’ve just finished moving a torso-sized rock when it happens.*
*The metal detector begins to chirp, bright and earnest.*
*Marina and I exchange a look, and I nod.*
Marina: "I’ll get the shovel!"
*It’s leaning up against the wall by the second metal detector. Marina grabs it, starts back towards me, then doubles back to set her detector aside as well.*
*I move to take the shovel from her, but she holds it away, shaking her head.*
Amber: "Yeah, but I-"
*She frowns at me, and I can tell this is an argument I won’t win.*
Amber: "Okay, okay. Be careful though. Don’t throw out your shoulder."
Marina: "I know what I’m doing!"
*And she stabs at the ground like she’s wielding a salad fork.*
*It bounces off with a jolt.*
Amber: "You need to go at it with more of an angle. Like you’re trying to slide it into the ground."
*I move behind her and adjust her arms a little. Her shoulders are damp with sweat, though I’m drenched with it.*
*She tenses up when I first touch her, but relaxes almost immediately.*
*It’s enough to make me self-conscious though, and I take a quick step back.*
Amber: "Sorry."
*I’m mumbling.*
Marina: "Oh, no, you’re fine! You just surprised me."
*Her voice is higher than usual.*
Amber: "It’s okay, you should have it now."
*Nodding, Marina jabs at the ground again. She scoops out a little bit of dirt and dumps it aside.*
*Watching her dig is painful. Either the ground is hard, or she’s not strong enough to make fast progress.*
*Whatever the reason, it’s slow going. But she refuses my repeated offer to take over, so with my hands on my hips, I watch as the hole very slowly gets deeper.*
*She’s reached a depth of maybe a foot or so when the shovel thumps against something that isn’t dirt.*
*Our eyes instantly meet.*
*I stoop down and brush a little bit of dirt away from the shovel blade. Sure enough, I can feel rough wood just under my fingertips.*
Marina: "Is it…?"
Amber: "Yeah, it is."
*Now she forces the shovel towards me.*
Marina: "I don’t wanna mess it up."
Amber: "Okay, look out."
*She steps back a couple feet, but leans forward to watch.*
*I scrape at the dirt around the spot that we hit, trying to find where the edges are.*
*Once I reveal one corner, I start to dig off thin slices of dirt next to it.*
*Soon the outline of the whole box is visible.*
*I hear Marina suck in a sharp breath. My heart is pounding with the same cautious excitement I felt at Shiprock.*
*It hardly feels real and yet…*
*Well, there’s only a ten percent chance that I’m dead and it isn’t.*
*I lift the box out of the ground with the blade of the shovel, and Marina plucks it off.*
*It makes a familiar rattling sound as she moves it.*
Marina: "Amber…"
*But I hold up a dirty finger to my lips.*
Amber: "Not yet."
*Looking back towards the parking lot, I can’t tell if anyone has seen us.*
*Seen us dig, and find the treasure.*
Amber: "Don’t say anything until we get back to the car."
*She’s a quivering leaf of excitement, but she manages to keep her mouth mostly shut for now.*
*But not before hissing out an excited "Amberrrrrr."*
*I just smile, hoping that I look calm.*
*Marina cradles the box in the crook of her arm like an infant, doing her best to keep it hidden from onlookers as we head back to the motorhome.*
*She carries the shovel in her free hand, while I have both metal detectors.*
*I don’t even notice the soreness in my arms.*
*Though it’s not the first time, the excitement of sitting down in front of the sealed box of gold feels fresh and new.*
*This one feels heavier than the last one, and it rattles less. Like there’s not as much empty space.*
*Without being asked, Marina retrieves the screwdriver and hammer that I’ve been using to smash the locks. She sets them down in front of me, but I offer them to her.*
Amber: "Care to do the honors?"
Marina: "You sure?"
*But she takes them.*
Amber: "Yeah, why not?"
*Her face lights up with a look of happiness that’s worth more to me than the gold.*
*If I’d known it was that big a deal to her, I’d have let her do this back at Shiprock.*
*It takes her a couple tries, but she bashes the lock off without even damaging the rest of the box.*
*She looks to me to open it, but I shake my head.*
Amber: "Nope, that’s all you."
*But she shakes her head too.*
Marina: "We worked together to find it, so we’ll open it together."
*And she places her hand over mine, guiding it to the lid.*
Marina: "Ready?"
Amber: "Yeah."
*She maneuvers my hand to flip the box open.*
*It’s full of dull-colored nuggets, like a moldy fast food meal.*
*I take one out, bouncing it in my palm and feeling the heavy softness that’s almost familiar by now.*
*Then I pass it to Marina, who treats it with her usual reverence.*
*She gently sets it back into the box and then smiles.*
Marina: "We’re pretty good at this whole treasure hunting thing, huh?"
*I shut the box and nod.*
Amber: "Yeah, we’re not bad."
*Even when the box is closed it radiates a heavy aura, one that’s almost intimidating whenever I come near it.*
*I place it in the drawer and seal it away, a sigh of relief following.*
Amber: "Two down, one to go."
Marina: "Whoa, there’s only one more piece left?"
Amber: "I think so. There's only some experts writing about "historical relevance" after the next entry."
*Flopping down on the couch next to Marina, I take a sip of my coffee and flip to the final entry of the journal.*
*Marina glosses over the page, a hazy look forming in her eyes as she soaks it in.*
Marina: "I can’t believe it’s almost over."
Amber: "Yeah, it’s weird that it hasn’t even been that long."
*The motorhome goes quiet, the buzz of the generator humming in the background.*
Amber: "Well it’s not over yet! We still gotta figure out where it is, and then there’s still the search!"
*I think Marina’s enthusiasm is becoming infectious.*
*She simply nods, now just gazing into her lap.*
Amber: "Well, let’s see what it says."
*I was never certain about the other locations, and considering how many giant rocks, canyons, and arches are in this country, most of them were probably just a fluke.*
*But this is unmistakable.*
*The vivid and breathtaking imagery, the constant allusions to flames, and most importantly—the heat.*
Amber: "He hid it in the Valley of Fire."
*Marina snaps back to attention, my conviction pulling her back from Marina-Land.*
Marina: "Are you sure?"
Amber: "Positive. There’s no other place it could be."
Marina: "Oh."
*She might as well have shot down my momentum with an elephant gun.*
Amber: "‘Oh?’ That’s all?"
Marina: "Yep."
*She narrows her eyes to an unimpressed squint. If they could shoot lasers, they would be right now.*
Marina: "Amber, how dumb do you think I am?"
Marina: "Always on fire? Really?"
Amber: "Now I feel dumb for saying it."
Marina: "You probably should."
*Ouch.*
*If her enthusiasm is infectious then so is my unrelenting realism. It’s corrupting her innocence.*
Marina: "By the way, what’s the Valley of Fire?"
*Well, maybe not too infectious.*
*Massaging my scalp, I shake my hair, hopefully creating enough new wrinkles in my brain that will help with explaining this.*
Amber: "Alright, so take a whole bunch of rocks, sand, and dirt. Okay?"
Marina: "Okay."
Amber: "Now make most of it red."
*My explanation is met with a vacant stare.*
Amber: "I swear, it’s a lot cooler than I’m making it out to be. It’s just kind of hard to explain without pictures."
Marina: "Don’t worry, I believe! Sounds awesome!"
Marina: "Where is it anyway?"
Amber: "A little ways into Nevada."
Marina: "Nevada!"
*Her knees bounce, jolting the table.*
Marina: "Are we even old enough to gamble?"
Amber: "There’s more in Nevada than Vegas, dummy."
Marina: "Still, isn’t that pretty far?"
Amber: "That might work to our benefit."
Amber: "I’ll need to check the map again, but I think we’ll be passing by a place I wanna check out."
*Her voice brightens.*
Marina: "You actually want to go sightseeing?"
Amber: "Don’t act so surprised. It’s not like I was gonna make a straight shot for California before I picked you up."
Amber: "Besides, I think you might like it."
*Though she does like virtually everything.*
Amber: "It’s a place called Zion."
Amber: "It’s a National Park here in Utah. I hear it's a hell of a lot greener than the places we’ve been through."
Amber: "I know we’re in a rush, so we won’t stay very long."
*She’s going to burst from excitement, and I still can’t resist messing with her. It’s cute.*
Amber: "But I guess if you don't think we can afford the time..."
*I expect her to throw herself at me. Hug me. Squeeze me with all the energy and happiness she usually does it with.*
*But she doesn’t.*
*Sitting perfectly still, Marina takes a single breath and gazes at me with a smile.*
Marina: "I’ve been surrounded by desert all my life. Seeing green for once would be nice."
Marina: "I’d love to go with you, Amber."
*An unsettling hollowness expands in my chest, as I realize I don’t know how to deal with a serious Marina.*
Amber: "Oh. Alright then."
*She doesn’t answer. Eyes now closed, arms folded in her lap, the empty, cooperative smile more forced than ever.*
Amber: "Well, there’s still a lotta hours left in the day. I’m not sure if we’ll make it in time to check into the campground, but we’ll be close enough that we can do it in the morning if we leave now."
*Marina nods.*
*We scooch out of the booth and walk the ten awkward steps to our seats. Strapping ourselves in, I switch on the ignition, the RV snorting into being.*
*I reach for the cassette player, then look to Marina, already lost in her thoughts as she stares out the window.*
*I don’t have the heart—*
*No.*
*I don’t have the courage to snap her out of it this time.*
*Sighing, I bring my hand back without pressing play and pull the motorhome into drive.*
*It’s too bad, too.*
*I think I’m really starting to like Marina’s hugs.*
*Hours, and several cups of coffee later, we’ve arrived at our destination. Or, at least close enough to easily check-in in the morning, yet hidden enough to not get towed.*
*Marina looks to the window, but her eyes drift back over to me, then mine to hers. For one, drawn-out second, our eyes are interlocked, petrified by the other’s stare.*
*Then, time continues.*
*Our heads swing in the other direction, so fast it takes my mind a few moments to catch up.*
*The side of her cheek I can still see is flushed.*
*Glancing in the rear view mirror, I find mine are, too.*
*I grip the steering wheel. Then cautiously, without turning my head, look back to Marina. She notices. Our eyes connect again.*
*Blush. Rinse. Repeat.*
*I wanted to give Marina the space she needed to say what she has to.*
*Or give myself the space to blurt out what I need to say.*
*It was just…*
*Again, Marina looks. I jump. We blush.*
*Never the right time.*
*Deciding I’ve had enough, I hop out of my seat and stretch. Marina follows.*
*I grab my mug from the holder and drag myself across the scratchy carpet to the sink. A small jolt shocks my finger as I reach for the faucet. The mug slips from my hand and crashes onto the counter, splashing coffee everywhere.*
Amber: "Crap."
Marina: "Let me help!"
*Marina springs over and reaches for a kitchen rag, but I swat down her hand and grab it just as she does.*
Amber: "Don’t worry."
*Now we’re forced to look directly at each other, but there’s no blushing this time.*
*I nod and pull away the rag as her fingers slide down the fabric.*
Amber: "I got this."
*She stares down at the counter, the spilled coffee trickling onto the floor with a steady drip.*
Marina: "Oh."
*Without another word, she slouches back to the couch.*
*The bed’s metal frame slams down with a loud clank that vibrates underneath my feet, followed by the couch cushions swishing past me, landing on the other side of the motorhome." with vpunch
Amber: "...for ya."
*Dammit.*
*Whatever. I’ll deal with this in the morning.*
*I toss the rag into the sink and head to my room, hesitating in front of the slider.*
*Please don’t let it end like this.*
Marina: "Amber!"
*There’s a pause. Several times I hear small gasps, like she’s about to speak, but they all just melt into sighs.*
Marina: "Goodnight."
Amber: "‘Night."
*My hand balls into a fist, my fingernails clawing deep into my skin. I step into the room and shut the slider behind me, immediately throwing my face onto the pillow.*
*I can feel my heart pounding as I roll onto my back. Lying there, twisted up in my sheet, it just keeps beating and beating, never settling down.*
*Managing to lift myself up on my elbows, I look at the picture next to the slider.*
*The one with the vinyl sleeve.*
*I still remember how his hand felt against my head, from the bony joints of his fingers, to how their calluses massaged my scalp.*
*For such a breezy old man, it’s amazing to think just how strong and confident his love was.*
*I let my head fall back against the bed and rest my forearm over my face.*
Amber: "Ah man, what am I gonna do, Gramps?"
*A brisk wind curls around me as I step out onto a patch of dirt that can barely be called a parking spot, in a place that can barely be called a campground.*
*Misty curtains have settled over the nearby hills and mountains and small specks of leftover morning dew occasionally drip on me from above.*
*Bending down, I drop my backpack and pull on the tongue of my ‘sorta-new’ hiking boots. A little big, but they’ll work.*
*Considering these came from the stash of supplies we borrowed from Mariah in New Mexico... her feet must be freaking huge.*
Marina: "All set?"
*Marina is standing behind me, frowning at her own velcro-strapped boots.*
Amber: "Just about. These boots are pretty big, though."
Marina: "You think so? They feel a bit snug to me."
*Sheesh, never mind about Mariah.*
Amber: "Hey, forget about the treasure. Let’s just dress you up as Bigfoot and turn you in for a cash reward."
*The joke is awful and I know it. Still, Marina plays along, sticking her tongue out in a half-playful manner before she forces a tired smile. The whole time, she doesn't make eye contact.*
*So much for lightening the mood.*
*Last night's sudden tension is still present. Even though we try to ignore it, or pretend it isn't there, we both know.*
*We both can't look at each other directly, and our conversation feels awkward and stilted.*
*But we pretend everything's normal as we try to put on our best performances and reenact our usual routine.*
Marina: "So how long is the hike?"
Amber: "The brochure said about ninety minutes each way. Might take even longer in this weather."
Marina: "Oh…"
*I can feel the gulf between us. It's like yet another canyon, except I don't know how to cross this one.*
Marina: "The picture in the brochure was super pretty. Angel’s Landing, right?"
*Now it's her turn to try and lighten the mood.*
*I hate how we're both too afraid to just address the issue.*
*But I'm just as guilty as she is here.*
*I sigh and play along as best I can.*
Amber: "Yeah, it's nice."
Amber: "It’s supposed to be the most scenic view in Zion."
*That’s what Gramps told me anyway.*
*This hike was one of his favorites. Said the view is so lush; that there's so many trees and plants, that he discovered several new shades of green up there.*
*It was one of the spots that he was most looking forward to visiting with me.*
*I glance back up at the mountains. The mist doesn’t look like it will clear anytime soon. In fact, it seems to be swallowing up more of the landscape.*
*It's got Marina's attention too. She stares up as if she's picking out specific details in the fog.*
*While she's distracted, I let my gaze rove over her again. Guiltily, like I'm sneaking glances, but I don't care.*
*Admiring the sweep of her shoulders down into her collarbone, and the rest of the curves of her body.*
*I let myself examine her like I did when we first met, and like I've been consciously abstaining from. Looking her over like it doesn't matter if she catches me anymore.*
*And I want her to pay attention to me.*
*She’s wearing her usual boutique brand clothes. Their cute frilliness is just as perfect as ever for Marina, but they clash with the clunkiness of the ugly brown boots.*
Amber: "You sure you wanna wear that? They might just get in the way."
*She gasps, almost offended by the suggestion that she wear anything else.*
Marina: "But my skirt is so comfy! Aren’t you supposed to wear comfy clothes on hikes?"
Marina: "Besides! With a name like Angel’s Landing it has to be pretty relaxing."
*She gives me a big, honest smile, tipping her head to the hills.*
*It's the first real smile I've seen from her all day.*
Marina: "So what’re we waiting for? Let’s go!"
*I melt, letting her skewed reasoning fall to the wayside just this once. Warm fuzzies and a goofy grin take over my dour mood. Just for a moment.*
Amber: "Alright, but no whining if they end up being a problem!"
*Slinging the backpack over my shoulder, we head for the shuttle that leads to the trail.*
*Logs and a stone fire pit adorn the side of each lot that we pass.*
*Maybe when we get back we’ll go to the camp store and pick up some supplies to roast marshmallows.*
*I think she'd like that.*
*Marina’s heavy breathing guides me as we slog our way uphill, the previous light mist having now morphed into a thick and dreary fog.*
*I cling to the chained railing as I stumble my way up the trail of rocks. It's the only protection from the sheer drop-off beside us.*
*All it would take is one slip for me to take a nosedive into the valley below, and I wouldn’t even see it coming.*
*Turns out, the fluffy and enticing name ‘Angel’s Landing’ doesn’t come with the ‘relaxing stroll’ Marina was hoping for.*
*This feels more like a ghastly death march. My sweat-drenched legs feel like pudding under the anchor that is a measly five pound backpack.*
*No one else on the shuttle even bothered to attempt the hike alongside us.*
*I attempt to form words between pained breaths, feeling like a rock is being smashed into my lungs with each step.*
Amber: "We should turn back."
Amber: "Can’t see jack."
Amber: "It’s not safe up here."
Marina: "What?"
*She sounds like she’s doing about as well as me, echoing the same lung-puncturing gasps.*
Marina: "But we’re so close!"
Marina: "There’s no way we’re not almost there!"
*We stop, collapsing against our knees as we try to catch our breath.*
Amber: "Not gonna mean a whole lot if we end up falling off a cliff."
Marina: "I thought-"
Marina: "You were-"
Marina: "The one who wanted-"
Marina: "To do this so bad."
*Every couple words are punctuated by her gasping for breath.*
Amber: "Yeah, but…"
Marina: "Amber… please."
Marina: "Let's keep going."
*Her voice is a whimper, fragile and meek. I fold.*
Amber: "Okay, okay."
*Marina has a way of making me shut up when she wants to. Whether she knows it or not.*
*Today, it doesn't make a difference.*
*I’m not going to be the one who makes things worse.*
*She’s wrong about one thing, though—I wasn’t the one who wanted to hike up here.*
*Gramps was.*
*It gets easier after our break.*
*The trail begins to plateau and the steepness evens out, fading behind us until the return trip.*
Marina: "Ooooh! I think we’re already here!"
Marina: "See! What did I tell you?"
*Marina darts ahead, disappearing into the fog so she can look at the mythical view that the brochure and I have preached to her, and Gramps to me.*
*But if my suspicions are right, this weather and the view aren’t going to be very compatible.*
*Following behind, I step out of the cloudy murk and into an area that offers only slightly more visibility.*
*Marina stands still on the center of the cliff, her hand clutched to her heart.*
*What should've been a bird's eye view of Zion is nothing but a sea of fog.*
*The lush greenery and full valleys are invisible. Only a few overgrown bushes and some rocky points peek out above the clouds.*
*I was expecting it, but the sight itself really is numbing.*
*The hollowness in my stomach, the one I’ve gotten so used to feeling, rears its head once again, only intensified by Marina’s silence, any expression shrouded by her bangs as she tilts her head down.*
Amber: "Sorry."
*Gently, she shakes her head.*
Marina: "Don’t worry about it. It’s not your fault."
Marina: "You tried to warn me."
Amber: "Marina..."
*But I don't know what to say.*
*I'm disappointed too.*
*There were a lot of places Gramps always talked about visiting.*
*"After we settle down and kick back for a few years."*
*"After I finish repairing the RV."*
*"After I get better."*
*This place came up more than the others, though.*
*Not just Zion, but the Landing. Kept saying we were going to hike up here, have a picnic, maybe watch the sun come up.*
*A slight smile comes to my face as I think about him trying to make that hike. I'd have liked to see the geezer try.*
*While I'm not a big enough sap to try and make all of my and Gramps' plans happen now, it would've been nice to see the view.*
*With Gramps, Marina and everything that’s happened over the past month, it just seemed fitting to make the trip.*
*Angel’s Landing.*
*I glance back to Marina, the hand that was clutched to her chest now stretched outwards, grasping at the empty air. A slight breeze blows through, ruffling her hair.*
*She looks beautiful.*
*She looks...*
*Angelic.*
*I didn't go to church much as a kid, but she looks like someone I’d see on a stained glass window. Right next to a rendition of Calvary or Skull Hill.*
*It’s frustrating to have come this far for nothing.*
*But is it because of the fog?*
*Because of Gramps?*
*Or because of Marina?*
*My teeth grit, grinding against each other under what feels like an unimaginable weight.*
Amber: "C’mon, let's go."
*I turn around and begin down the slope. There’s nothing else worth seeing up here.*
*Marina doesn’t follow. No spirited footsteps trailing behind me. No speeding bullet blazing ahead.*
Amber: "Marina, we’re going!"
*It comes out with more bite than I intended, but I don’t care. I just want to get out of here now.*
*Marina is hesitant to answer, but eventually manages to spit it out, sounding more like a question than an actual statement.*
Marina: "I don’t want to."
Amber: "What?"
Marina: "I don’t want to leave."
Marina: "Not yet, anyway."
*Annoyed, I turn back to her for an explanation, only to be greeted by a pleading look instead.*
Marina: "I’m used to it by now, I know I am. But do you think we could wait a little longer, just to see if the fog clears?"
*I don't know what 'it' is.*
*I respond with a blank stare.*
*It’s like she’s not even talking to me.*
Amber: "Don’t be stupid."
*I start back down the hill, not bothering to wait for Marina this time.*
Amber: "It’s past noon. If it hasn’t cleared by now then it’s not going to anytime soon."
*So much for not making things worse.*
*I nudge the fire with a stick. It doesn’t do any good though, as the logs just tumble on top of each other, smothering the already pitiful flame." with dissolve
*For a moment, our lot is only lit by the few remaining embers in the pit. Sighing, I grab the bottle of lighter fluid and squirt it onto the ashes.*
*An artificial flame roars into existence then peters to the size of a pathetic candle flicker just as quickly.*
*I plunge the stick back into the fire, and scrape cinders from the glowing wood. Eventually, a flame catches on a charred log and grows until the pit is blazing once again.*
Amber: "Aha! ‘Bout damn time."
*The joy is exclusive to me however, as Marina stays silent, staring into the fire.*
Amber: "Want another s’more?"
*She bounces back to life.*
Marina: "Oh my god, yes plea—"
*She catches herself and switches back to broodiness.*
*I roll my eyes, but can’t help smiling on the inside.*
*No matter how hard she tries, she’s still the same Marina she’s always been.*
*I grab a marshmallow and stick it on the end of a bent coat hanger before handing it to her.*
*Without another word, she plops it into the fire.*
*Meanwhile, my eyes are drawn to the neglected bag of hot dogs lying behind the chocolate and graham crackers. Unopened and ignored.*
*I bought them because I thought Marina would like the variety, but I should have known that she would only go after the marshmallows.*
*She’s been devouring them. She opened the bag when we left the store, and it was half-empty by the time we got back to the campsite.*
*She clung to the bag like a security blanket the whole way. It’s a miracle there are even any left.*
*Shrugging, I take one of the hot dogs out of the bag and impale it on my coat hanger, then put it in the fire next to Marina’s.*
*Even when we're this close to each other, there's still an air of tension.*
*The one that's pervaded since we left Arches.*
*Neither of us have addressed it, but the inevitability of the treasure, the thick uneasiness that hangs over us—it’s suppressed, but it’s definitely there.*
*But it's gotten better since we left Angel's Landing.*
*The snacks were a peace offering of sorts, I guess.*
*Although I was pleasantly surprised that she sat so close to me, even with three other logs around the fire pit.*
*I fix my gaze on the flames. The dog is slimy, oozing with condensation that drips and sizzles in the ashes.*
Marina: "OH GOD IT’S ON FIRE IT’S ON FIRE FIRE FIRE!"
*I’m tempted to squeeze one more joke out, but decide against it as I try to hold back the snicker under my breath.*
Marina: "Hmph. I think I like you better when you’re serious."
*She crosses her arms, turning away as she closes both eyes to block me out.*
*Then, with a grin, one eye open.*
*We both burst out laughing.*
*Since the hike, the fog has climbed down the hill, enveloping the campground. Even here it’s carved out a small pocket, the breezy chill surrounding us as we take in the warm glow of the fire.*
*It feels nice.*
Amber: "But for real, I still can't believe this is your first time leaving home."
*She settles down a bit and places her hands in her lap, teetering side to side as she looks to the sky, even though there’s nothing to look at but mist.*
Marina: "Yeah, sometimes I have trouble believing it, too."
Marina: "Since there’s so many of us, I guess mom and dad could never afford to take us anywhere."
*That’s what I thought. If her family is as big as she says, it’s not like they could have just packed up and traveled like me and Gramps.*
Amber: "You said you have nine brothers and sisters, right?"
Marina: "Yup! There’s nine of us, plus mom and dad. So eleven all together."
Amber: "Jesus."
Marina: "Right? Everyone says our house is pretty big, but it’s always been so cramped that I’ve never been able to tell."
Amber: "Is that the same brother whose car got torn apart by Mariah?"
*She laughs guiltily.*
Marina: "Yeah… He always called it his ‘Love Wagon.’"
Amber: "If that thing was a Love Wagon, then my motorhome is a Love Palace."
Marina: "Oooh. That’s a good name for it!"
Amber: "Don’t even start."
Marina: "Anyway, he said he wanted to get a new one when he got to Salt Lake City, so I don’t think he’ll be too angry."
Amber: "Hold on. That’s in Utah, too. Why didn’t you tell me? The detour would’ve been a pain in the ass, but if you wanted to visit him I wouldn’t have minded the extra miles. Not if it was for you."
*Marina does a doubletake, and I realize I've given too much away.*
Amber: "You’d have to pay for gas, though!"
*Marina blinks, then smiles. Crisis averted.*
Marina: "Nah, it’s okay. I wanna do this without my family’s help. Besides, it’s only a community college."
Amber: "Your brother moved from New Mexico to Utah so he could attend a community college?"
Marina: "Uh-huh! He sends me a gift every month. Last month it was a top hat! I put it right on top of the desk in my room!"
*She pauses for a moment.*
Marina: "Er, mine and my sisters’ room, I mean."
Amber: "Your family sounds about as weird as you."
Marina: "Really? Huh."
Marina: "Yeah, I guess we are!"
*She beams, wearing the title of ‘weird’ like a badge of honor.*
Amber: "That’s not a good th—actually, maybe it is."
Marina: "I like it! Besides, dad keeps us all in line. He's a defense lawyer—super straight laced."
Marina: "You remind me of him, actually. He likes everything nice and orderly, which is bad for him because he’s surrounded by us, but…"
*She talks for a long time, chattering and laughing about her family, the smile never leaving her face.*
*I almost get lost in it, I almost want to.*
*For the precious minutes that she speaks, I forget the tension that's shadowed us since Arches, and could believe it was never there.*
*I love that about Marina. Even when we're wet with rain, or humoring truck drivers, or racing a lunatic down the highway, she never hesitates to smile.*
*For her, it's always poised on the brim, waiting to shine through.*
*I could listen all night, but she trails off only a short time later.*
Marina: "Ah, I’m sorry. This must be boring, right?"
Amber: "Nah. I can tell you love ‘em a lot."
Amber: "It was always just me and Gramps, so it’s sorta interesting to see how other families work."
*The lot falls silent, save for the crackling flames. I brace myself for what I know is coming.*
Marina: "Hey Amber, what happened to your mom and dad?"
*I stare into the fire. It pops, casting up delicate embers that fall and vanish in the dirt.*
Marina: "I’m sorry. That was too personal."
Amber: "No. It’s fine."
Amber: "They’re both around, I think. Only met Dad once a long time ago."
Amber: "Me and Ma’ don’t get along very well. Haven’t spoken to her since I was sixteen. Last I heard, she was living in some trailer park in Ohio."
Marina: "Is that why you lived with your grandpa?"
Amber: "Sorta."
Amber: "Ma’ always had her… issues. So the court said Gramps could take care of me when I was a baby."
*I stifle a laugh.*
Amber: "Not that it did any good. The geezer had the bright idea to haul me around the Midwest and East Coast for half my life."
Marina: "He took care of you all by himself? He must’ve been amazing."
Amber: "Hah! Try again. He was an old hippie who was stuck in the seventies and spent most of his time listening to weird music."
Marina: "Sounds just like you!"
Amber: "Hey, watch it. If anything, he was more like you. I was always cleaning up his messes. Even as a little girl, even near the—"
*I choke down a growing lump in my throat.*
Amber: "Even near the end."
Marina: "Amber, I—"
Amber: "Even when he decided to settle down in Colorado it was just us. People would come and go, but it was always us."
Amber: "Ma’ didn’t even bother to come to that, but I was there."
Amber: "And he was always there, too."
Marina: "You really miss him, huh?"
*My voice lowers to a whisper, raspy and dry.*
Amber: "Yeah."
*Something slumps against my shoulder. Glancing down, I discover Marina’s head resting against me." with dissolve
Amber: "What’re you doing?"
Marina: "Nothin’."
Amber: "Ah."
*She looks up at me." with dissolve
Marina: "Want me to stop?"
Amber: "Nah, keep doing it."
Marina: "Mmm. Okay."
Amber: "We should turn in soon. We have a big day ahead of us." with dissolve
Marina: "Yeah, maybe."
*We sit in silence for a little while, or maybe it’s a long time. I can’t tell. It’s a nice silence, though, a comfortable one.*
*Finally.*
*The engine dies. I can still hear the power buzzing before it surges down, leaving us with the ambiance of the settling motorhome.*
Amber: "We’re here."
Marina: "Not a lot of cars here, huh?"
Amber: "Guess the rest of the world just hasn’t caught up yet."
*That won’t last for long. Any day now, people will figure out it’s here and an army of would-be treasure hunters will be swarming this place, drooling at the chance to find gold.*
*The last of the gold.*
*There's a fluttering in my chest, and I tighten my grip on the wheel.*
*It’s the end of our adventure.*
Marina: "I bet our names have spread across the land and struck fear into the hearts of our rivals, giving them no other choice but to call it quits!"
Amber: "Yeah. Or maybe someone already came through and found the treasure."
Marina: "Wh-What? Don’t say something scary like that."
*Still wrapped around the wheel, my clammy hands start shaking.*
*Marina takes notice, looking over with sympathetic eyes.*
Marina: "Hey."
*She takes her hand and gently places it on top of mine, giving it a reassuring squeeze. I let both fall into my lap.*
*She’s warm.*
Marina: "We’re doing this together, okay?"
*The flutters tickle the inside of my chest until I can’t help smiling.*
Amber: "Right. Let’s do it."
*Regretfully, I force out a nod and pull my hand out from Marina’s.*
*It feels wrong. Like throwing away a precious gift.*
*Reaching into the console, I retrieve the journal, for what may be the last time.*
*It’s well loved. A bent spine, wrinkled pages, and folded corners on the flimsy paper cover.*
*Looking back, it’s been getting roughed up since the moment we got it. Ever since that weird station attendant let Marina have it for free way back at the traveler’s store.*
*Hard to believe this cheap thing was the road map to something so valuable.*
*Taking a deep breath, I skim over the final entry.*
Amber: "Windstone Arch."
*It’s probably the vaguest entry so far, but I can feel it. It has to be there.*
*I slap the journal shut and lay it to rest in the center console, right next to all the cassette tapes.*
*Marina and I look at each other, now sharing the same piercing determination.*
Amber: "Alright, let’s get this show on the road."
*Because of the lack of crowds, getting around is a lot easier than usual, allowing us to take in the scenery for once instead of slogging through zombified hordes.*
*Even though there’s not many people around, it somehow feels fuller here.*
*Everywhere we walk, we’re surrounded by layered dunes and sandstones, towering over us, striped with faded reds and calico browns.*
*Somehow, we do eventually manage to find the Windstone Arch.*
*Unlike the arches back in Utah, it isn’t big and majestic. It’s not a giant upside down horseshoe that lords over us as we look on in awe.*
*It’s not even an arch, as much as it is a cave, really. Just a jagged, overgrown rock with a few crevices big enough for a person to enter.*
*Still, despite this—despite the fact that it doesn’t compare to the size or grandeur of everything else we’ve seen together so far, and despite that it hardly took us any time at all to find it—it’s still imposing.*
*The longer I stare at it the bigger it gets, like it’s judging us. It isn’t calling into question our abilities or even if we can find it.*
*It’s gauging us to see if we’re ready.*
*Are we?*
*I look over to Marina. A small bulge forms in her throat before she swallows it back down.*
*Is she?*
*I return my gaze to the Arch, asking the same questions over and over in my head.*
*Am I?*
*Finally, we look to each other. Marina readies the metal detector strapped to her back and gives me a nod, which I return.*
*Even if we aren’t ready, even if this is the worst mistake of my life, this is something both of us have to do.*
*Our steps in sync, we march over the rocks and desert shrubs before crouching into the cavern.*
*Sunlight squeezes into the cave, dyeing the rock columns neon-red.*
*Marina begins scanning with the metal detector, listening intently for a beep.*
*The narrow space forces us to huddle together as we heave our way through the arch, repeatedly brushing against each other.*
*At first, it’s accidental, but I find myself craving it more.*
*To get closer.*
*To feel her.*
*To watch as her hair absorbs the brilliant glow of the cave.*
*I’m deprived of all of that though, as her metal detector vibrates in her hand, making a low rumble.*
*She takes off the headphones and points to the wall.*
Marina: "There."
*I avert my gaze and move past her, taking a knee as I slip the bag off my shoulder and pull out a mandrill pickaxe.*
*Careful not to hit her, I start chiseling away at the rock.*
*I reposition myself and block Marina from getting hit by any of it.*
*The small portion of the wall crumbles away and after a few more strikes, reveals a hollow space, just big enough to squeeze your hand into.*
*This is gonna suck if some creepy thing’s gone and made its home in there.*
*Shuddering, I reach into the space and feel something square and wooden. With some budging, it comes free and easily slides out.*
*A box.*
*Its familiarity is expected, but it doesn’t stop my heart from leaping any less, nor Marina’s, who squeaks out a gasp.*
Amber: "Ready?"
*This time, she barely nods, unsure of what to say, but like ripping off a bandage, I swing the lid open before she can give any real answer.*
*Nuggets. A pile of them. Filled from top to bottom, all misshapen and morphed.*
*The sight isn’t anything new, but this time it feels different. It’s like for the first time, the reality has finally sunk in and everything up until now has been a haze.*
*We both just stare at it. An entire universe of thoughts race through my mind, yet it all comes up blank.*
Amber: "What now?"
*It’s directed more towards myself than Marina, but it’s the first thing I manage to spit out, and ultimately the most important.*
*Marina doesn’t answer at first, instead just tilting her head, trying to process the question. But finally, she gives a simple smile.*
Marina: "I saw a sign for a trail on the way over here. Why don’t we go hiking?"
*‘Hiking?’*
*I freeze for a second, then laugh under my breath.*
*The more I think about the simplicity behind her words, the more I laugh, until I border on sounding like a hysterical madman.*
Amber: "Hiking… For real?"
*I keep laughing, struggling to keep it together, even after I start having trouble breathing.*
*Poor Marina isn’t in on the joke though, and within a matter of seconds her face goes from confusion, to concern, then pouting.*
Marina: "I dunno why you’re laughing. I was being serious, y’know…"
*Settling down, I wipe a fake tear from my eye, an occasional chuckle still managing to break through.*
Amber: "No, no, I know you were."
Marina: "Then what’s so funny?"
Amber: "Nothing. Just…"
*I finally regain complete composure and smile at her.*
Amber: "Yeah, sure. Let’s go hiking. That sounds nice."
Amber: "Can you please keep to the signs?"
Marina: "I will, stop being such a worrywart."
*Marina skips through the sand, gleefully oblivious that her "trail" is a badly marked patch of dirt over the plains of the Mojave.*
Amber: "That’s what you said last time. If you’re not careful, we’re gonna get lost—again."
Marina: "Aww, you didn’t like those old cabins we found?"
Amber: "It gets old after the second time, Mare."
*It kills daylight, too. The sun has faded to orange, and will soon sink out of view.*
*There’s no way we’re getting back before dark.*
*But hey, maybe that’s not such a bad thing.*
*We’ve already found the treasure, so spending some time with Marina is a nice reward.*
Marina: "So about this Fire Wave thing…"
Marina: "And yes! I know it’s not an ocean of lava, smartypants."
*Damn. I must be running out of material.*
