Marina: "I mean, it's so cool - well, hot, I guess - but what is it?"
Amber: "What's the fire wave? It’s, a uh… windy... thing. That winds around rocks and stuff. And looks like fire."
Amber: "At least, I think that’s what it was."
Marina: "Ohhhhhhhhhhh?"
*A devilish grin slinks onto Marina’s face and she attaches herself to me like velcro, nudging my shoulders.*
Marina: "You don’t know what it is either, do you?"
Amber: "Wh-What? Do so!"
*Her grin grows until I force myself to look away.*
*She’s not gonna get the satisfaction.*
Amber: "I’ve seen pictures."
Marina: "Ohhhhh, you’ve seen pictures? What sorta pictures?"
Amber: "Y’know. The picture-y kind."
*I can just feel her revelling in this. For the first time, she’s got me playing right into her hands.*
Amber: "Oh shut up."
*I feel a slap against my wrist, then a tug. Looking down, I see Marina’s bright dimples shining through her round cheeks.*
Marina: "Why don’t we find out together?"
*I’m stunned.*
*It’s a position we’ve been in before, but it’s supposed to be the other way around. I’m always pulling her. That’s my job.*
*But now, it’s her smooth fingers locked around my wrist. Her fingernails grazing above my skin.*
*The realization doesn’t even have time to sink in before she propels herself forward, with me in tow.*
Amber: "Hey wait! Hold on!"
*I kick up sand and grit as I'm practically dragged along by Marina, who meets my yelps with silence.*
*Then she halts as quickly as she took off, leaving me to shoot forward and stumble to a stop.*
Amber: "Ya could have at least warned me before ya—"
*I go silent.*
*Marina does, too.*
*The Fire Wave.*
*Swirling ribbons of red wrap around the hillside and stretch out to the horizon. The setting sun casts light and shadow across the ground and sky, shading it all with intense golds, pinks, and purples.*
*As the sky dims, a shred of stray light passes over the shade and shines directly on us.*
*Directly on Marina.*
*She stares on, silent.*
*I can feel every glint of emotion coming from her.*
*Every breath taken away.*
*Every raw feeling that builds up inside her, only to be choked back down.*
*All of it.*
*I glance between her and the scene stretched out before us. The pockets of sky with their deep, vivid colors are beautiful, but the choice is easy.*
*I fixate on Marina.*
*Her clear, sweet blue eyes.*
*Her cute, round cheeks.*
*Her lips, as they quiver ever so slightly.*
*Laying a hand on her shoulder, I turn her towards me and softly touch her cheek.*
*Her mouth slips open with a faint gasp, but all it takes is a gentle stroke of her face for that uncertainty to be wiped away.*
*Marina holds my gaze. My eyes relax and I step closer, so I can feel the warmth of her body against mine.*
*Then, I lean in and press my lips to hers.*
*So soft.*
*I keep myself pressed against her, letting my lips move with the natural flow.*
*But she doesn’t press back.*
*Afraid, I pull away, but before I can step back, she pulls me closer and lets her lips melt into mine.*
*I let the pattern take control. Our mouths move inside each other, full of tender caution as we ease in.*
*We repeat the process infinitely. Bathed in the light of sunset, the two of us exchange ‘something’.*
*I eventually pull back, unsure of what to say, and scared of what I could.*
*We turn back to find the last rays of sunlight slipping behind the sand.*
*Something brushes against my hand, tickling for a moment before intertwining between my fingers.*
*Her grip is loose, but I wrap my fingers around so that I'm holding her firmly.*
*We watch the last of the light drift into a peaceful evening.*
*Together.*
Marina: "It’s pretty."
Amber: "Yeah."
*Oh god why? What have I done?*
*Her back is turned to me like mine is to hers. I claw my nails deeper into the scratchy couch fabric and stick my gaze on the floor.*
*Please end me now.*
*It was silent after we did that. Nice silence, a blissful release of silence. But as we walked back, terror started to sink in. I think she felt it, too.*
*I listen to the buzz of the generator, and stare at a patch of artifical light on the wall.*
*Stupid little box is making fun of me, I swear.*
Marina: "So what now?"
*Funny. That’s the same thing I asked when we found the treasure except she sounds even more hopeless and confused than I did.*
*Bringing my legs to my chest, I bundle my arms around them and put on a nervous smirk.*
Amber: "We could always go hiking again."
*She throws a mock-slap at my shoulder, feigning a huffy voice.*
Marina: "You would like that, wouldn’t you?"
*The heavy embarrassment in the air melts away into something a little more comfortable as we both manage to laugh at my perviness.*
Marina: "Waaaahhhhhhhhhhhhhhhh."
*Marina lets out a whiny cry and slides against the couch.*
Amber: "About what?"
Marina: "About everything!"
Marina: "Us…"
*My heart tightens like she’s squeezing it.*
Amber: "Did you not…"
Marina: "No! I did—"
Marina: "I do."
Marina: "I just don’t know."
*She turns to me, her eyes pleading. Like she’s looking for answers.*
*There's no doubt I'm responsible for those eyes.*
*Feeling a fierce sense of responsibility, I shoot up and march to the center console.*
Marina: "Amber?"
Amber: "Hold on."
*I open the console, but don’t bother digging around. I know exactly where these are.*
*Two irreplaceable pieces of paper.*
*Stopping in front of the couch, my voice darkens.*
Amber: "I’ll be using my half of the treasure to pay for Gramps’ hospital and funeral expenses."
Amber: "But before I do that, I still need to go to the music festival."
*I offer her one of the tickets. Unsure, she gently tugs it from my hand and begins reading.*
Amber: "If you want to, you can go with me. Might give you a little extra time to think about things."
*My voice softens.*
Amber: "About everything."
Amber: "Up to you, babe."
*I flop back on the couch and hold the ticket over my eyes to block out the light.*
Marina: "Ohhhhhhhhhh?"
*Oh crap.*
*She peels the ticket off halfway and peeks through, revealing an amused grin.*
Marina: "So I’m ‘babe’ now, huh?"
*On her hands and knees, Marina crawls ever so closer, while I scramble to turn and hide my face.*
Amber: "Shut up. It was an accident."
*And now I’m back to where I started: mortified and embarrassed.*
Marina: "Amber."
*Her voice is like a melody.*
Amber: "Shut up."
Marina: "Aaaamber."
Amber: "I said shut up."
Marina: "Oh Amber."
Amber: "What?"
*A tank engine plows into my back, followed by soft, gentle arms wrapping around my neck as she rests her head against mine.*
Marina: "Thank you."
*My shoulders relax, embracing her. In turn, I rest my head on hers, too. A head sandwich.*
Amber: "Sure thing, kiddo."
*Once we’re quiet, the awkwardness sets in again. Where I don’t know if I should be saying something to fill the silence or if I should leave it be.*
*Is she thinking the same thing? Is she also worried?*
*I sneak glance after worried glance down, but I can’t see her face.*
*Her hands wander around like she doesn’t know what to do. She touches her face. Then scratches her leg. Picks at a fold in her shirt. Pops her knuckles.*
*So she is nervous.*
Amber: "Hey."
*The top of her head bumps my chin as she flinches.*
Marina: "Hey."
Amber: "You good?"
Marina: "Yeah."
Amber: "You don’t seem good."
Marina: "No I am! Sorry. I’m just…"
*Rather than hurry her along like I sometimes do, I wait for her to find the words.*
Marina: "Just worried I guess."
Marina: "I’ve um… I know you have, but I’ve never had a girlfriend before."
*I start to say something, but she hurriedly cuts me off.*
Marina: "I don’t mean it like that! Well I do, but I’ve also never had a boyfriend before. Not a real one, anyway."
Amber: "You had a fake boyfriend though?"
Marina: "Barely. Like in middle school, there was this guy who asked if I’d be his girlfriend, and I said okay. But then the next day he said his mom told him he wasn’t allowed to have a girlfriend, so we broke up."
Amber: "Sounds brutal."
Marina: "Right?"
Marina: "And then junior year of high school, I kind of dated this guy for like, a month. But I didn’t really like him. My friends just wanted me to."
Amber: "That’s screwed up."
Marina: "I dunno, not really. They weren’t trying to be mean or anything. And he wasn’t a bad person. He was just… boring."
Marina: "Like for our first ‘date’ we went to dinner and a movie. But dinner was just fast food and he spilled ketchup all over himself."
Marina: "And I don’t even remember what movie we saw. He put his arm on the back of my chair and I thought he was trying to put his arm around me so I kind of got closer but nope, he just kept it up there."
Marina: "Like he was just stretching."
*She giggles at the memory.*
Marina: "Honestly I’d forgotten all about that until now. That’s how not-a-real-boyfriend he was."
Marina: "What about you?"
*I’m glad that right now, things seem normal. Maybe if she’s talking, she forgets about being worried.*
Amber: "Well, I’ve never had a boyfriend."
*Marina punches me in the shoulder.*
Marina: "You know what I meant! I could’ve guessed that much."
Amber: "Hey now, I’m just being clear. But yeah, I’ve had other girlfriends."
*Isn’t it bad manners to talk about this? Especially early in a relationship?*
*She asked, though.*
Amber: "They counted, though it wasn’t really anything serious either. By the time I was old enough for a serious relationship, I was too busy with taking care of Gramps to focus on one."
Amber: "But at least no one’s mom ever told them they had to break up with me."
Marina: "Listen, it was hard when I was 12."
Marina: "For like a day or two."
Amber: "I’ll bet."
Amber: "Anyway, yeah, there was this girl I was with through a chunk of high school. We broke up in… Junior year, I think? She wanted to focus on school."
Amber: "She ended up getting a full ride to a nice college, so I guess she made the right call."
*Marina doesn’t miss a beat.*
Marina: "Nah, it was her loss."
*I shift to free my arm that’s pressed against Marina’s side, and instead wrap it around her.*
*At first I can feel her tense up, but then she gradually relaxes, until most of her weight rests on me.*
Marina: "So Amber."
Amber: "Hm?"
*She doesn’t answer.*
Amber: "Mare?"
Marina: "Sorry, never mind."
*She looks up and smiles at me, and I smile back.*
*Maybe it’s better not to think too much right now, or ask too many questions.*
*It’s just the two of us, being happy with each other. Not minding that we’re both sweaty and kind of gross right now, or that we haven’t eaten in hours.*
*Or so I think.*
*It isn’t long before she yawns, and looks up at me apologetically.*
Marina: "Whoops."
Amber: "It’s okay, I’m kind of tired too."
*I’m not. I’m too excited still. But I don’t want her to feel bad about going to bed.*
*Plus, we did a lot of hiking today.*
*I unwind my arm from around her and switch which hand holds hers, so that we can stand up.*
*She’s fidgety again, shifting her weight from foot to foot.*
*Well, I guess it’ll take some getting used to. For both of us.*
*I let go of her hand. It falls limply back to her side, like she lost control of it.*
*Looking me in the eye, I can tell she’s waiting for me to do or say something.*
*I take a step towards her, closing the short distance between us.*
*And then I lean in and kiss her again.*
*Briefly. Enough that she doesn’t even really have time to react before I pull away.*
*She blinks twice, then rubs her lips and smiles.*
*She walks backwards away from me, still smiling. And I’m grinning like an idiot too, of course.*
*I’m tempted - so tempted - to invite her to sleep in my bed. Just to be close to her, still.*
*But I don’t. Since she’s still so nervous and unsure, I don’t want to make that harder for her.*
*I lick my lips as I go to turn off the lights, still tasting the sweetness of her on them.*
*As I climb into bed, listening for the sounds of her breathing, I don’t expect to get much sleep of my own tonight.*
*‘Sleeping on it’ is supposed to help you with tough decisions, or with big changes, but that’s hard when you can’t pass out in the first place.*
*Thoughts of Marina, and of Gramps, and of things changing, and of things staying the same all brewed together to keep me awake just like the coffee is right now.*
*When I got up this morning, the first thing I felt was surprise that I’d managed to fall asleep at all.*
*The three hours I got must have snuck up on me.*
*I drain another cup of coffee. Number four, and the last one from this pot. I dump another few scoops of grounds into the coffeemaker and start a new batch.*
*I got to see the sunrise, which even I usually sleep through. Out here in the middle of nowhere, with no light pollution, the sky was vivid; shards of jagged pink tearing into the blue.*
*I nearly dozed off again just watching.*
*Maybe someday, I’ll get to watch it with Marina. Someday, when I wake up early again, and if she manages to get her lazy ass out of bed.*
*Lazy but cute.*
*Yesterday’s events replay in my mind for the umpteenth time. And with them come the swirl of emotions that feel like a stomachache and a high, both at once.*
*Things finally starting to feel normal between us again.*
*Kissing Marina at the Valley of Fire.*
*Her leaning into it.*
*Wondering if I should stop.*
*Her not letting me.*
*Sitting close on the couch, not asking any questions. Not answering them either.*
*Wondering if I should.*
*Ugh.*
*It’s too early for this shit.*
*The familiar ping of the coffee maker tells me that I’ve been zoned out for a few minutes now.*
*Smashing my fists into my eyes, I try to rub the sleepiness out.*
*Movement from the back of the RV means that I must have woken Marina up. I can hear the sound of her bed creaking, and of the covers being thrown back.*
*I feel a strange, nervous elation as she appears in the hallway, looking bedraggled.*
Amber: "Good morning."
*She groans like a zombie and stumbles toward me.*
Amber: "Want some coffee?"
*A stiff head nod.*
*Smiling, I prepare her a cup. It’s only half coffee. The other half is a boatload of sugar and the creamer that neither Gramps nor I ever drank.*
*I don’t even know why we had it, but it’s not expired.*
*When she reaches me, Marina leans into me. Sort of falls, really, crashing against my shoulder.*
Amber: "Hey, careful."
*A bit of coffee sloshes out of the mug and onto my hand, but I don’t mind. She takes the drink and a couple small sips.*
Marina: "Mmmm."
*Minor bliss replaces her glazed expression.*
Amber: "Good?"
Marina: "Goooood."
Amber: "There’s hardly any coffee in it, you know."
Marina: "Just the way I like it."
*I wonder if she had a hard time falling asleep like I did, but I’m hesitant to ask.*
*Afraid she’d say no.*
*Afraid she’d say yes.*
*So I just sit down, and she takes a spot next to me. We both quietly sip our coffee as she rejoins the land of the living.*
*Once she finishes her first cup, she’s alert enough for a conversation.*
Amber: "Sleep okay?"
*I figure that’s safer than flat-out asking.*
Marina: "Mmm. Yeah, kinda."
Amber: "Kinda?"
Marina: "I like…"
*She trails off. Maybe I gave her alertness too much credit.*
Marina: "I forgot what I was just saying."
*I laugh and stand up.*
Amber: "Don’t worry about it."
*Marina mumbles something incoherent.*
Amber: "What was that?"
*She clears her throat.*
Marina: "Sorry. I asked if you slept alright."
Amber: "Took me a while to fall asleep, but after that, yeah."
*It’s not strictly a lie.*
*Eyes half-closed, Marina smiles thinly.*
Marina: "Good."
*I turn around so that she doesn’t see how preoccupied I am, or guess what’s on my mind.*
*Whatever it is that I’m dealing with, it’s not her problem.*
*I don’t want her to worry about me.*
*The floor creaks as Marina stands up too, but I don’t face her.*
Marina: "So where are we going today?"
Amber: "No idea. We have a few days before the show. Anything you wanna see?"
*Whatever it is, I’ll make the trip.*
Marina: "We’re not very far from Las Vegas, right?"
Amber: "Not at all. It’s just a couple hours."
Marina: "Let’s go there!"
*Finally, I meet her eyes again. The smile on her face is pure and earnest. I can’t help but return it.*
Amber: "Sure. That sounds like fun."
*And she hugs me.*
*Her warm figure is pressed close against mine. She smells like my shampoo.*
*I wrap my arms around her and hold her close.*
*Just for a second, everything feels right. Like it’s all gonna work out.*
*And I realize how badly I want it to.*
*I squeeze tight and then pull away.*
Amber: "What was that for?"
Marina: "Just felt like it."
*Worry creases her face.*
Marina: "Should I not?"
Amber: "Nah, you definitely should."
Amber: "Go get dressed and I’ll get us on the road. You cool with waiting to eat until we get there?"
Marina: "Yeah!"
Amber: "Cool. There are a million buffets and exotic restaurants there, so we can find something good."
Marina: "I like Las Vegas already."
Amber: "Dork."
*I gently push her towards the back to go get ready. She sticks her tongue out over her shoulder as she goes.*
*Watching, I rub my temple before climbing into the front seat.*
*I’m looking forward to this, and I know Marina is too.*
*So why does it feel like I’m doing something wrong?*
*For a long moment, I just press my forehead against the steering wheel, not thinking much of anything. The sound of Marina’s approaching footsteps makes me sit up, and I put on a smile as she hops into her spot.*
Amber: "Ready?"
Marina: "Yep!"
Amber: "Here we go, then."
*And we head out towards the highway.*
*Las Vegas appears on the horizon far before we actually reach it. Clusters of generic skyscrapers are interspersed with the world-famous casinos, which come in many shapes and sizes.*
*Some are indistinguishable from the tall banks and offices around them, except for gaudy logos and signs.*
*Others are constructed to look like a pyramid, or a Roman palace, or a medieval castle.*
*There’s a distinct clash where some buildings are striking and clean looking, as if they’d just been completed yesterday.*
*But then just beside them will be a sad looking place with faded paint and signs that aren’t supposed to be flickering.*
*As we enter the city limits, we’re suddenly surrounded by the noise of traffic and the smell of exhaust. Exiting the highway was like crossing the boundary to another world.*
*Marina drinks it all in.*
Marina: "Ooh, what’s that one? The super tall one!"
*She points through the windshield. Even though it’s impossible to follow her finger, I know which one she’s talking about.*
Amber: "That’s the Stratosphere. I think it’s one of the tallest buildings in the country."
Marina: "Whoa…"
*We’re still miles out, but she already seems to be infatuated with the city. Her eyes are wide and so is her mouth.*
Amber: "It’s even cooler up close."
Marina: "I never knew cities could be this… huge."
*I keep forgetting that she’s never been out of New Mexico. Sure, there are couple decently sized cities there, but nothing this impressive.*
*I’m excited for her to experience all this for the first time.*
Amber: "Honestly, Las Vegas is kind of on the small side, at least compared to places like New York or Chicago."
Marina: "Seriously? That’s insane…"
Marina: "I can’t imagine that many people in one place."
Amber: "What about your house?"
*She giggles.*
Marina: "That’s pretty close."
*She reminds me of a puppy, straining against its leash as she whips between looking out the windshield and out the passenger-side window.*
*To me, the part of the city that we’re in looks like any other downtown area, so I’m not impressed. Once we get to The Strip, where all of the really famous casinos are located, it’ll be different.*
*But first I have another destination in mind.*
Amber: "So Mare."
Marina: "Yeah?"
Amber: "Ever heard of the World Carnival Buffet? At the Rio?"
Marina: "I don’t think so. Should I?"
Amber: "Well if you like food, it’s pretty much the greatest place on Earth."
Amber: "They have like, everything imaginable to eat. And lots of stuff that you can’t imagine either."
*Somehow, her eyes get even wider.*
Marina: "I wanna try it all."
Amber: "Pretty sure you’ll change your mind when you see some of it. But that’s the spirit!"
Marina: "Are we going there now?"
Amber: "Hell yeah we are. I’m starving."
Marina: "Me too!"
*And she grabs my free hand; the one that isn’t on the steering wheel.*
*My heart leaps to my throat.*
*Since we’re at a red light I catch her eye and she smiles, though her face is a little red.*
*I smile back but then face forward.*
*Our hands hang limply between our seats. It isn’t long before our palms are moist with sweat. I don’t know whose.*
*Her hand is so soft. Like she treats it with lotion or something, but I know that’s not the case.*
*Smooth, like a baby’s.*
*It makes me a little self-conscious of my chewed fingernails and chronically dry skin.*
*Worried that I’m gonna gross her out and she’ll pull away or something.*
Marina: "Is this okay?"
Amber: "Huh?"
*Maybe I was getting a little too distracted.*
Amber: "Is what?"
*She raises our linked hands.*
Marina: "This."
Marina: "You just seemed kinda…"
Marina: "Like, I mean, I can not, if you-"
Amber: "No! No, it’s fine. Seriously."
*I’m talking louder than I need to, flustered as I try to backpedal. She doesn’t look convinced.*
Marina: "Okay. Sorry, I’m still… You know. Not really…"
Amber: "It’s fine. You’re fine. Really."
Amber: "I’m the one who should be sorry. I’m not usually this weird in a relationship."
*Now she looks almost offended.*
*Crap.*
Amber: "Not like that! I just mean-"
*A blaring honk from behind reminds me that I’m sitting stopped at a green light.*
*The middle of traffic isn’t the best place for this conversation.*
*Breathing deeply as we pull ahead, I try to collect my thoughts.*
Amber: "Sorry. Again. I just… Have a lot on my mind. Because I like you! I really do! You know that really freaking well by now!"
*She smiles, though she’s still looking down. And our hands are still together.*
*And this is such an awkward time and place to be talking about this.*
Amber: "So it’s my bad if I’m being weird or whatever. But being with a girl I met on the side of the road is a new one, even for me, you know?"
*Finally, she laughs.*
Marina: "Yeah, I guess it’s a little weird."
*I feel like I can still hear a slight quiver in her voice, but I might be imagining it.*
*So I squeeze her hand tightly. Immediately, she squeezes back.*
*I only have a vague idea of the direction I need to be driving. Fortunately, it isn’t too long until the Rio comes into view, looming large a few miles away.*
*We pull into the parking lot near the back, where the only open spaces are left.*
*It’s pleasantly warm outside. I’ve heard stories of people’s shoes melting on the asphalt during the summer. But for now the weather’s perfect.*
*As we cross through the parking lot towards the casino, Marina takes my hand yet again.*
*I can feel my heart rate accelerate, but I squeeze her fingers all the same.*
*The first few steps inside are sensory overload. A million different sounds and a billion different lights all pour from the sea of slot machines. Even at lunchtime on a Thursday, the majority are occupied.*
*We both take a second, frozen in place, to try and process it all. This isn’t even my first time in a casino, but I’m overwhelmed just like Marina is.*
*I can see the reflections of a game in her eyes, like a cartoon character thinking of money.*
Marina: "Whoa…"
Amber: "Yeah, whoa."
Marina: "Is this one of the most popular casinos?"
Amber: "Not even close. Like, it’s big, but it’s not the biggest."
Marina: "Whoa."
*I tug her forward.*
Amber: "C’mon, we’ll have tons of time to gawk with food in our stomachs."
*The mention of food steadies her steps.*
*Giant, overhead signs direct the way to the Buffet. They must be visible from any part of the building.*
*If this place is famous for something besides the food, I don’t know what it is.*
*We can smell it before we can see it, even despite the stench of cigarettes and B.O. that are pretty strong here.*
*It doesn’t smell like any one particular food item, but rather lots and lots of them.*
*Guided by our stomachs, we half-run towards the buffet.*
*The line to get in is small, since we got here so early. Marina hops up and down, trying to peek over the head of the bald guy in front of us.*
Amber: "Can you see anything?"
Marina: "Barely. It looks like it goes on forever."
Amber: "Good."
*I practically throw my card at the attendant. It reminds me of paying to enter an amusement park, and I’m just as excited.*
*We’re let through a small gate and into Heaven.*
*Rows upon rows of stations form a small maze in the massive room. Deep bins full of food - fried or grilled or baked or roasted - fill nearly every inch of space in each station.*
*Marina and I grab plates from the tall stacks nearby.*
Amber: "Meet you in the dining room?"
*She nods, already distracted, and heads straight for the desserts.*
*I load my plate with a slice of prime rib, two rolls, and a scoop of mashed potatoes. I pour gravy over them, letting it run down and soak into everything else.*
*For good measure, I also throw on a pair of chicken wings from a nearby basket. They’re so fresh that I can see the steam coming off of them.*
*. . . I’m gonna need a second plate.*
*I grab one off another pile and toss a small piece of salmon, some potstickers from the Asian section, and a large spoonful of some sort of casserole.*
*I can’t even guess what it is, but it looks good. Everything looks good.*
*Solely because I can’t fit more food on my plates, I head to the dining area and pick a seat where Marina will be able to find me easily.*
*Sure enough, she emerges just a minute later, carrying two plates like I did.*
Amber: "Wow, you weren’t kidding about trying everything."
*Instead of big portions of a few things like I got, Marina has one or two bites of a couple dozen dishes.*
*Roughly half of them are cakes or other sweets.*
Marina: "When in Rome, you know?"
*I slice off a bite of steak and close my eyes in ecstasy while I chew. It’s divine. Holy cow.*
*Barely balancing her plates as she slides into the seat beside me, Marina bumps against my shoulder as she gets situated, and I almost send a forkful of potatoes into her lap.*
Marina: "Whoops, my bad."
*In retaliation, I spear a bite of chicken from her plate, swimming in some Asian sauce.*
Marina: "No fair!"
Amber: "It’s double fair."
Marina: "That doesn’t even make sense."
*After that, she finally gets to take her first bite. And she melts. Just like me, her eyes roll back into her head while she chews.*
Marina: "I don’t even remember what this is but it’s amazing."
*She scoops up another forkful of what looks like some kind of barbecue brisket and holds it towards me.*
Marina: "Try it."
*I open my mouth and she guides the fork in. I pull back to chew. It’s got a nice, smoky flavor.*
Amber: "That is good."
Marina: "Right? I almost want to go get a whole plate of just that."
*Then she tries the baked chicken that she has next to it.*
Marina: "Wait, never mind, this is even better."
*There’s a distinct lack of vegetables on both of her plates, but she seems to enjoy everything that she got. Every bite results in some statement about how it’s the best thing so far.*
Amber: "Did you get any of this?"
*I gesture towards the slice of steak that I’ve almost finished.*
Amber: "Okay, open up."
*She dutifully opens her mouth while I cut a bite for her. It’s the best part; right near the edge where all the flavor is.*
*It’s a little awkward to be feeding her like this, but no one pays any attention to us.*
*When she swallows, she sits still.*
*Very slowly, she opens her eyes again.*
Marina: "That was literally the best thing I’ve ever eaten."
Amber: "You’ve said that like 20 times so far."
Marina: "I mean it this time! That was illegally good."
Marina: "It makes double sense!"
*We laugh.*
Amber: "Gramps always used to say that the best place to get a steak was at buffets like this."
Amber: "Not ‘cause they’re always better, but because you can just go up and get slice after slice."
Amber: "Although he’s the only person I’ve met who could eat more than one."
*Marina chuckles with me, but it’s nervous-sounding.*
Amber: "I used to tease him for it. No one should be able to eat that much meat!"
Amber: "He would always complain about ‘em being too done. He cooked his own steaks to be kinda medium-rare. Closer to rare."
Marina: "Gross."
Amber: "Don’t tell me you eat yours well done."
Marina: "Um…"
Amber: "Marina. I dunno if we can still be together."
*She looks pained.*
Amber: "I’m just kidding, you know."
Marina: "Yeah, I know."
*But it doesn’t sound like it.*
*I shut up. It’s probably not a good idea to talk about Gramps too much either.*
*Our plates are mostly empty, and I look back at the serving area dubiously.*
Amber: "Round two?"
*She thinks about it, then shakes her head.*
Marina: "I think I’d explode if I ate much more."
Amber: "Yeah, same."
*There’s a soft flutter against in my cheek as Marina leans over and kisses it.*
Marina: "Thank you for bringing me."
Amber: "Y-Yeah, no problem."
*I intently examine the hard plastic bench I’m sitting on.*
*Too long passes by before I talk again.*
Amber: "So are you ready to go?"
Marina: "Oh, sure."
*I can tell how on edge she is again.*
*Good going, Amber. Nice.*
*I launch to my feet.*
Amber: "Just leave the dishes on the table. Someone will come by and clean them up."
*I have no idea if that’s true.*
*I start to leave, expecting Marina to follow me.*
Marina: "Amber!"
*But I turn around. She’s still in the process of getting up.*
Marina: "Wait for me."
*First she has to brush her face with a napkin, and then she drains the glass of orange juice she was drinking.*
*Then she scoots out of the booth and hurries toward me.*
Marina: "Are you alright?"
Amber: "Yeah, sorry. Just felt kind of nauseous for a second. Probably ate too much."
Marina: "Oh."
*I doubt she’s buying it.*
*From the corner of my eye, I see her reach toward my hand. Then she seems to think better of it and lets hers fall back to her side.*
*I grab it myself as I lead the way out of the casino.*
*Outside, I take Marina's hand, but it feels limp inside my own.*
*I let her lead the way down the street and towards the busiest part of the city.*
*Sometimes stuff on the side catches her eye. Brightly colored sports cars that probably belong to millionaires pass by us pretty frequently.*
*There are also people in bike-drawn carriages, and double-decker tour buses.*
*Besides the people, the buildings themselves all compete to stand out. Almost every storefront has something neon, flashing to get you to look at it.*
*Toy stores, restaurants, sex shops and more all vie for real estate. Within a two-block radius, you could probably buy anything you ever wanted.*
*Maybe a half-mile ahead of us, on the other side of the road, there are a few street vendors on the sidewalk.*
*They have awnings set up to shade themselves and their merchandise, though it’s too far away to see what they’re selling.*
*Marina spots them and points.*
Marina: "Ooh, let’s go look!"
*But then her excitement vanishes.*
Marina: "Um, I mean, if you want to."
Amber: "Yeah, let’s check it out!"
*With a hopeful smile, Marina starts to run. A red light at the crosswalk makes her stop not too far ahead, though.*
*I catch up just in time for the light to change.*
*Before she can dart away, I take Marina’s hand. She doesn’t object, but searches my face. So I smile, and she smiles back.*
*I let her pull me to the vendor, who seems to be selling a little bit of everything.*
*T-shirts cover most of a wire rack that serves as a wall. There are also bins of buttons, keychains, and other small trinkets.*
*One bin is full of tiny soapstone animal carvings, and those are what Marina goes for.*
*I ignore the vendor and his weasely small talk, refusing to be conned into buying tons of junk.*
*After a minute, Marina selects a little stone coyote. I pay five bucks for it; practically robbery.*
*We start down the road again, with Marina inspecting her new treasure.*
Amber: "Why a coyote?"
Marina: "I thought she was the cutest. Look at the way the color changes here."
*She holds the little trinket up by my nose. There’s a spot where the color changes from brown to a milky white on one side of its face.*
Amber: "That is pretty."
Marina: "Mhm. Plus, wolves are pretty much my favorite animal and coyotes are close enough."
Amber: "Yeah? Have you ever seen a live wolf?"
Marina: "No, I wish. You?"
Amber: "At Yellowstone a couple years ago, yeah. I begged Gramps to take me."
*As soon as I mention Gramps’ name, I see Marina’s eyes flit towards mine.*
Marina: "What else do they have there?"
Amber: "All kinds of stuff. Wolves, bears, buffalo, elk, deer. Pretty much any kind of forest animal."
Marina: "And they just wander around, right?"
Amber: "Yeah, there are even places where you can feed some of them out of your car."
Marina: "What about the meat-eaters? Like wolves and stuff."
Amber: "There are spots where you can see them but you can’t feed ‘em. They’re still wild animals. It’s not a zoo or anything."
Marina: "I wanna go there someday."
Amber: "I’ll take you to see them sometime. It’s a cool place."
Marina: "Really?"
Amber: "Yeah, sure."
*No matter what, I’m happy that she’s happy.*
*I follow behind as she darts from stall to stall like a hummingbird.*
*They all offer more of the same: generic desert-themed trinkets or gaudy ‘I Love Las Vegas’ merchandise.*
*The sidewalks are full of these vendors, who shout and try to get the attention of all the dozens of people who pass by. Marina’s the only one who gets hooked in.*
*But she doesn’t ask for anything else. Maybe there just isn’t enough wolf merchandise.*
*Every time she dives in to examine someone’s wares, I hang back with my hands in my pockets and watch. Once she’s done, we hold hands again.*
*It’s a nice cycle.*
*But all that changes once we reach the Strip itself, with its eclectic architecture and eccentric population.*
*I point out some of the more famous places, like the Luxor, which is shaped like a pyramid, and Caesar’s Palace, which looks like it was stolen from Rome.*
*Marina ‘ooh’s’ and ‘aah’s’ in all the right places, though to my surprise she doesn’t try to lead us to them.*
*Instead, she seems dead-set on taking us further and further down the row of sidewalk salesmen, like she’s looking for something in particular.*
*Not that I have any idea what that is.*
*She leads the way, looking from side to side, but never seeming quite to find it.*
*Until suddenly, she freezes in place. A large wave of pedestrian traffic behind us crests and spills around like we’re not even there.*
*Marina points ahead at one of the street vendors a few blocks ahead.*
Marina: "Found one!"
*I’m yanked forward once again, being dragged by her newfound enthusiasm.*
*Rather than a bunch of cheaply made goods, the merchant she's noticed has a bunch of colored drawings of cartoon characters - barely recognizable due to the exaggerated style.*
*A sign beside them advertises, ‘Custom commissions - $30 each.’*
*I immediately understand what it is Marina wants.*
Amber: "No. No, no no."
*I try to drag my heels, but I’m tugged along by Marina. She’s surprisingly strong when she wants to be.*
Marina: "Amber."
*She says my name without even turning around.*
Marina: "This has been on my bucket list for years."
Marina: "It’s happening."
Amber: "What has? Getting ripped off by a street artist?"
Marina: "Having someone draw me!"
*My protests going ignored, I fall in step and accept my fate.*
*The guy manning the booth looks like he's completely out of place: he’s wearing a flashy Hawaiian shirt and a baseball cap.*
*When he notices Marina and I approaching, he sits up from his slouching position and grins.*
Artist: "Hello, ladies!"
Marina: "Hi there! We were interested in having our portrait drawn."
*"We" weren’t. You were.*
*But I don’t protest. I’ve resigned myself to going along with whatever she wanted.*
Artist: "Perfect."
*He frames us with his fingers, like he’s going to take a picture.*
Artist: "You girls stand right there and get comfy. I’ll get ready."
*He lurches off his wooden chair and grabs a sketchpad and a box of pencils.*
Artist: "We’ll do you two in color, how’s that sound?"
Marina: "Great!"
*I just grimace.*
*I’m not fond of having my picture taken, and this isn’t much better.*
*I just never feel satisfied with how I look.*
*That’s why there aren’t many pictures of Gramps and me. Just a couple.*
*I’ll deal with it, for Marina’s sake.*
*We stand under an awning, though its shade does little to help the heat. Marina shifts from foot to foot while I just wait with my hands in my pockets.*
*Every so often, she turns to beam at me, though she doesn’t say anything.*
*Geez. Not sure why she’s so hyped up, but it’s cute.*
Artist: "Go ahead and scrunch together now."
*He makes a motion with his hands.*
*Marina stands near me, and I wrap one arm around her.*
Artist: "Closer."
*Our shoulders are touching.*
Artist: "Closerrrrr."
*Now we’re cheek to cheek.*
Artist: "Perfect."
*And he stares at us with a smile for just a second longer than is comfortable. It makes his expression seem more than a little sleazy.*
*Before I can change my mind, he looks down at the paper and starts to draw.*
*We stand there, not talking or moving at all.*
*Once, Marina starts to ask something, but he waves his hand without even looking up.*
Artist: "Quiet! If you move, you’ll mess it all up."
*So she shuts up. I can feel her jaw snap shut since it’s smooshed against my own.*
*Fifteen minutes pass. My mouth is starting to get sore. I didn’t have any guesses as to how long this would take, but it’s starting to get annoying.*
*Even Marina seems to be having second thoughts. She wriggles, just barely, probably to fight the growing tension in her muscles.*
*Not enough to ruin the drawing though.*
*From the corner of my eye, I notice a couple of the glances that we get. We probably don’t look especially out of place, but more like hapless tourists who get conned into something.*
*I guess we are, to an extent.*
*I wouldn’t mind, except that we also look ridiculous being stuck together like nearly-Siamese twins.*
*Our artist draws with long, swift strokes. Though I can’t see what he’s doing, he appears confident. Hopefully the result is worth the discomfort.*
*And the thirty bucks.*
*I’m nearly at my breaking point when he finally leans back and sets his pencils aside.*
Artist: "Done!"
*Marina peels away from me instantly, leaving a damp spot of shared sweat on my face. I rub at it as I follow her, more slowly, to see the results.*
*As she cranes around the easel to look, I watch her expression go from excited to overjoyed. Her smile widens into a grin as she grasps the drawing in her hands.*
Marina: "Amber! Look! It’s us!"
*She flips it around so I can see.*
*It’s… us.*
*The first thing I notice is my boobs. They’re a lot larger than in real life. Same with Marina’s—kinda.*
*Other than that, the drawing is cartoony - we’re recognizable, but our features are exaggerated. Marina’s eyes are almost comically huge.*
*In contrast, the buildings behind us in the drawing are sharply defined and realistic.*
*Clutching the paper to her chest, Marina sighs a contented smile.*
Marina: "Thank you so much!"
*The artist wipes his sweaty palms on his shorts as he stands.*
Artist: "My pleasure, my pleasure."
*And he looks at me expectantly.*
*With a sigh, I pull out my wallet and pass him a pair of wrinkled bills.*
*I pass it to him with a nod.*
Artist: "Y’all have a lovely day now!"
*With that, he slouches down again and pulls out a cheap-looking paperback novel.*
*The drawing is still close to Marina’s chest like a priceless artifact.*
*Every few seconds, she holds it away from her and grins as she looks at it.*
*That makes it worth the cost.*
*Finally, Marina rolls up the drawing like a scroll and tucks it under her arm for safekeeping.*
*We stand in the shade of the awning while we look up and down the road.*
Marina: "We should split up for a little bit."
Amber: "What?"
*I can’t help but yelp. That was the last thing I was expecting.*
Marina: "Not for long! There’s just something I wanna do."
Marina: "And you can’t be there."
Amber: "Why not?"
*Shuffling in place, Marina looks away.*
Marina: "‘Cause, it’s a surprise."
Amber: "Mare, no. This isn’t some small town. This is Las Vegas. What if you get lost, or if some creeper tries to mess with you?"
Marina: "It’ll be fine, Amber. There’s like a million people on this street."
Amber: "Yeah, so? That doesn’t mean anything."
Marina: "I mean it, it’ll be fine."
*There’s a fire in her voice that takes me by surprise. There’s even a small spark in her eyes - like she’s angry.*
*My frown deepens.*
Amber: "But-"
Marina: "Seriously. It’s nothing bad, I promise. I just wanna do something, but it’s a surprise for you. So you can’t be there."
*Her face softens.*
Marina: "Please? It’ll only be a couple hours."
Amber: "Marina, I dunno…"
*Of all the cities to split up in, this is one of the worst. Not only do we not have a way to contact each other if needed, but the people aren’t the sort you can trust.*
*If I leave her on her own, there’s a good chance I’d never see her again.*
*But the way she stands there, hands on her hips, looking determined, makes me not wanna argue.*
*I sigh.*
Amber: "Are you sure?"
Marina: "Yeah! I mean it, just give me like… three hours."
Amber: "That’s a long time."
Marina: "You think? I tried to keep it short."
*In three hours, she could be snatched into someone’s car and well into Arizona. Or California. Or halfway to Reno.*
*It’s irresponsible of me to let her go.*
*But I can tell by looking at her that I don’t have a choice.*
Amber: "Okay, fine. But can you at least stay on this street?"
Amber: "It’s a straight shot back to the Rio where we’re parked. And so it’d be harder to get lost."
*Marina nods fast.*
Marina: "That’s fine! I already saw where I need to go. But you’re not allowed to watch, okay?"
Amber: "Yeah, yeah. I’ll probably just go back to the car anyway."
*Now she frowns.*
Marina: "Isn’t there anything you wanna see?"
Amber: "Not without you."
Marina: "Aww, Amber."
*She kicks at the ground.*
Marina: "I guess we don’t have-"
Amber: "No, go on."
Marina: "What?"
Amber: "Just be safe, okay? Don’t do anything silly."
Amber: "I guess it’ll be fine for a couple hours."
*She takes the drawing in one hand and then hugs me tight. Then, Marina leans up and kisses me on the lips.*
*She pulls away before I can get into it.*
Marina: "Okay. I’ll see you in a while then."
Amber: "Right."
*We stand there, neither of us moving.*
*I sigh again, and then trudge back down the road.*
*I glance back every few steps, but she doesn’t move. Instead, she watches me and waves.*
*Probably making sure that I keep good on my promise not to follow her.*
*As tempting as it is, I’ll let her go for now.*
*Three hours can’t be that hard.*
*Right?*
*Turns out I was wrong.*
*Two hours are agony.*
*I wander from store to store, stepping in without actually caring what’s for sale.*
*The first is a vegan restaurant. I don’t try any samples.*
*Just next door is some fetish shop, with its windows covered. All the lights inside are colored, mostly red or sometimes violet.*
*The girl behind the counter, pale-skinned and with artificially black hair, gives me a smile that I return.*
*But I don’t stick around.*
*I don’t even know what I’m doing. Going through the motions of being a tourist, I guess. Technically, I am exploring Las Vegas.*
*Tomorrow, I’m sure I won’t be able to remember a single detail of these shops.*
*Up ahead is yet another street vendor. His setup is nearly twice as long as most of the others, and includes large glass display cases.*
*Looks like it must be a pain to transport.*
*I wander over, pretending to be interested.*
*The clerk, a Hispanic man with a short goatee, watches me but doesn’t say anything. Not even a greeting.*
*It’s all cellphones and phone accessories. Chargers, cases, cords, and the like. Nothing relevant to me.*
*There’s a special discount on pre-paid phones and time cards right now, it seems. A half-dozen identical posters plaster the wall of the longest case.*
*I stare at one of the fliers, though I’m not actually reading it. Just imagining if Marina and I had cellphones.*
*It might help at times like this.*
*Times when I’m being ridiculous and clingy and an idiot.*
*Being able to reach her any time would be nice. But would they get much use?*
*That’s when I realize something, and I’m dumbfounded by a simple fact; what it is that’s been bothering me since we split up.*
*This is the first time since meeting Marina that I’ve been on my own. That she isn’t just a room away, or within shouting distance.*
*Even though it’s only been a couple hours, I suddenly feel profoundly alone.*
*It’s pathetic, I know. I’ll see her again in less than an hour now.*
*But the anxiety of separation gets the better of me. It makes me sick as all the horrible scenarios rush into my head again.*
*The ones where something bad is happening to her, and she’s not able to call for help.*
*I look at the phones in the case.*
*I’ve never had one, so I’m not very knowledgeable, but they look cheap and flimsy.*
*I try to find a brand name, but it wouldn’t mean much to me even if I could.*
*The only names I’d be familiar with are those massive companies who make everything, not just phones. The ones whose merchandise costs hundreds if not thousands of dollars.*
*I think Marina had a Heisei? I’ve heard they’re pretty good.*
*Then again, I've also heard about shady stuff with them suddenly replacing all their production workers with robots.*
*Gramps said this sort of thing would be a part of the New World Order.*
*Doesn’t matter, though.*
*It’s tempting, but there isn’t much point.*
*And after that…*
*Well, a phone gives us a way to stay in touch.*
*Once she’s…*
*Once we’ve…*
*I spin around and walk away in a hurry, feeling suddenly sick again.*
*I must have looked like a lunatic; coming up, standing still, then freaking out and leaving.*
*Maybe that’s why the guy didn’t say anything. I just look like another insane junkie.*
*I picture some tweaker stepping out an alley towards Marina, who of course wouldn’t even notice and-*
*I shove those thoughts out of my head.*
*One hour. Just one more hour. Chill, I tell myself for the thousandth time.*
*Across the street from the phone carts is a bright green sign for a candy store. One of those places that has floor-to-ceiling bins of gummies, and that sell by the pound.*
*I half-expect to see Marina in there herself.*
*Getting her a surprise bag of sugar might be a good way to try to make up for being weird.*
*Until the next time I act weird, that is.*
*I cross the street as soon as there’s a gap in the traffic, not bothering to go to a crosswalk.*
*The doors have an outline and handles that are the same lime green as the sign. When I step inside, I see that the walls are too.*
*I’m reminded of the slime from those old shows I used to watch on TV, that would be sprayed onto the audience or cast members.*
*The selection is good, though. Like I expected, there are more varieties of candy than I’ve ever seen in a single place - only pictures.*
*There are several different types of plastic bag that you can fill, ranging from ‘I’m on a diet’ to ‘garbage bag’ sizes.*
*I grab a medium-sized one.*
*Then I realize that I don’t know what to get for Marina.*
*Definitely chocolates, but with or without nuts? I can’t remember if I’ve ever heard an opinion.*
*I try to recall what she’s gotten at gas stations along our trip, but I never paid that much attention.*
*Maybe I should go for a mixture of both. But then what if she’s allergic to something?*
*It probably wouldn’t have ever come up, since we always eat out and order for ourselves.*
*I don’t want to give her a three-pound sack of anaphylactic shock.*
*Gummies, then. Those might be safer.*
*There are more types of gummy candies than I ever thought existed: different flavored rings, candies shaped like the fruits they taste like, and other things like chewy soda bottles.*
*For just a moment, I wonder about which ones I should pick.*
*I think I remember her saying she loves raspberry. Or was it strawberry?*
*But I think she’s gotten strawberry soda before. Not that that means anything.*
*This is a gift, so I want it to be perfect. Not a bag full of things she dislikes.*
*Then I remember: Marina likes pretty much everything.*
*Elated at the simplicity, I dump a small scoop of just about every sort of gummy that they sell.*
*For good measure, I get a second, smaller bag and fill it with some malted chocolate balls, caramel clusters, and plain old chocolate chunks.*
*Safe options.*
*I haul the two bags over to the counter. There’s just one attendant, and she’s currently ringing up a mom with her two sons.*
*The kids dart around their mom’s legs, rattling displays and knocking items off the shelf. No one makes them stop or fix their mess.*
*One of the kids stares up at me and I frown at him, deeply. He stops running and hides behind his mom’s legs. Good.*
*When they leave, the kid is still clinging to her like a bear cub.*
*I set my bags up on the counter and kneel down to replace some of the spilled items. As I do that, the shopkeeper comes around front, nearly bumping into me.*
Amber: "Yeah, no problem."
*I look up, directly into her knees.*
*I straighten up and we’re suddenly way too close. Stumbling backwards, I grab the counter to support myself. She laughs, eyes crinkling.*
*She’s cute: her tan skin is complemented by the bright red uniform she wears.*
*A little nametag on the counter says that her name is Cassi.*
Cassi: "You know, there’s a special going on right now."
Amber: "Oh yeah?"
*A stack of postcards sits by the credit card machine. She hands me one.*
Cassi: "If you buy ten more bags you get one for free."
*I try to imagine how much candy that would be. You’d need a wheelbarrow to haul it all out.*
*Cassi laughs, probably at my expression.*
Cassi: "Yeah, we haven’t had many people spring for that one."
Amber: "The person I’m buying this stuff for probably would, if she was here. This’ll probably be gone by tonight."
*I gesture at the bags, still sitting on the scale.*
Cassi: "Shopping for your sister, or…?"
Amber: "Nah, my girlfriend."
*I try out the word, seeing how it sounds in relation to Marina.*
*As expected, it makes my heart skip a beat.*
*During that beat, I feel like I see the shopkeeper’s face fall. Just for second.*
Cassi: "Yeah? Lucky girl."
Amber: "Thanks."
*I don’t know if she means because of the candy, or because of me.*
*After ringing up my bags, she dumps them into an even larger bag. That seems unnecessary, but I guess it makes them easier to carry.*
*She passes me the candy as well as the receipt.*
Cassi: "Have a good day."
Cassi: "Try the peach rings. They’re my favorite."
Amber: "I will. Have a nice day, too."
*The girl waves as I leave the store.*
*Outside, I check the receipt, since I wasn’t really listening to the total.*
*It looks like she didn’t charge me for the smaller bag.*
*I glance back inside, but she’s disappeared into the back room.*
*I guess that answers my earlier question.*
*Flattered, I head back to the Rio where we’re parked. It’s not long until I’m supposed to meet Marina, and if I spend too long outside, her present will melt.*
*Walking back to the casino where we parked is way faster than leaving it was. That’s probably because I’m still missing the human pinball, so I’m not held up every time we pass a shop.*
*I say that, but of course I’m eager to see her again.*
*In some parallel universe, where I hadn’t met Marina, I might’ve stuck around at the candy store to chat for a longer time.*
*But unfortunately for Cassi, I already have someone.*
*The parking lot at the Rio has filled up even more. Getting out of here is going to be a pain, since there wasn’t a whole lot of room to begin with.*
*I make my way to the RV and let myself in. It feels like an oven, and the first thing I do is turn on the AC.*
*I set the bags of candy down on my bed to inspect them.*
*As I’d feared, the chocolate is slightly melted, streaking brown against the clear plastic. It’s not too bad though, and a couple hours in the fridge should have it good as new.*
*I leave the gummies on the counter, since they didn’t suffer as much on the walk back.*
*Marina and I are supposed to meet up here in about half an hour.*
*Since it’s so soon, I may as well just wait in here where it’s more comfortable.*
*I lay down on my bed and try to read that book Marina’s been sucked into for a few days, but I can’t stay focused on the words long enough to process them.*
*It doesn’t take long before I give up and set it aside.*
*Now I just stare up at the ceiling. I used to do this most nights, when I couldn’t sleep.*
*After Gramps passed, but before I met Marina.*
*It wasn’t that it comforted me or anything. But the amount of effort it took to not think of anything usually led to me falling asleep without realizing.*
*Instead, this time, I let my thoughts wander.*
*Sleeping has been easier lately. Actually doing something during the day, and getting exercise, helps a lot.*
*But also my thoughts have been less cloudy; less muddled.*
*Sure, there are still moments where I think of Gramps, and the pain hits me again.*
*Having Marina around makes those rare.*
*Someday I might be able to think of him without it hurting. I don’t know if I want that, though.*
*Isn’t that just like forgetting? People say you move on and get over deaths, but what does that mean for the person you lost?*
*I roll onto my side and stare at the picture of me and Gramps hanging on the wall.*
*I can’t take it, and roll onto my other side.*
Amber: "Ugggggh."
*Groaning into my pillow is cathartic. Without Marina here, I can be as loud as I want.*
*To my surprise, no tears come.*
*Anyone walking by outside would probably think I’m dying or in pain, but I don’t care.*
*Alone time has its up-sides after all.*
*Grieving is complicated. And exhausting. Sometimes I wonder if I’m even doing it right.*
*There must be something wrong with me, if I don’t even want to get over Gramps.*
*I sit up, still hugging the pillow to my chest.*
*Marina will be back soon. Maybe I can talk about this with her.*
*As I think that, I feel a stab of guilt.*
*It’s not her problem. It’s something I have to deal with myself.*
*I force myself up and over to the driver’s seat. I’m not going anywhere, but I can listen to music and watch for Marina’s return.*
*I stick in a tape and kick back.*
*A few songs pass by. It’s been twenty minutes or so.*
*I start to scan the parking lot, looking for her familiar red hair.*
*The tape ends.*
*That’s okay, it was a short one. I replace it with another one, chosen at random.*
*Still no sign of Marina.*
*I get up and check the clock. She’s about ten minutes late now.*
*Maybe the clock is off somehow?*
*I hop out of the RV and ask someone for the time. It matches up exactly with what the clock said.*
*Worry begins to tighten my stomach, but I force myself to calm down. It’s Marina, and it’s only been ten-*
*-no, 15 minutes.*
*Most likely she got distracted and is on her way right now.*
*Five more minutes pass. I begin to pace back and forth.*
*Should I go look for her? I have no idea where to start, though.*
*And if she got to the RV and I wasn’t there, then she might go looking for me instead.*
*No, I have to stay and wait.*
*Another ten minutes. She’s half an hour late now.*
*I’m so buying us cellphones. As soon as she’s back here we’re going to that guy on the street and we’re getting prepaid phones.*
*From a few streets away, I can hear the sound of a siren; a fire truck or ambulance or something.*
*The knot in my throat feels larger. I cough, just to relieve some of the tension.*
*I have to go look.*
*I can’t take just sitting here.*
*I switch off the engine, throw my shoes on and climb out.*
*And then I see her.*
*Head down, walking slow, but coming towards me.*
Amber: "Marina!"
*The relief in my voice probably conveys the panic that just drained out of me.*
*She looks up at the sound of her name, and that’s when I see that she’s crying.*
Amber: "What’s wrong?"
*I rush over.*
Amber: "What’s wrong?"
*Alarm poisons my voice. Did something happen to her after all?*
*I put my hands on her shoulders and shake her, gently.*
Amber: "Marina, talk to me. What happened? Are you okay?"
*She still can’t talk, but she nods.*
*Thank God.*
*There are no sobs, but her eyes are pouring tears. Every so often she hiccups, like she’s barely restraining herself from fully bawling.*
Amber: "Come on."
*I take her hand and lead her back to the RV. No one gives us a second glance. This is probably a common occurrence here.*
*Marina silently follows me into the RV.*
*I lead her to my bed and have her sit down. I sit beside her.*
*Alternating between rubbing her back and her shoulders, I try to get her to calm down.*
*Slowly, her breathing slows to normal. When I offer her a tissue, she blows her nose, loudly. Then she takes a few more to wipe her eyes.*
*Rather than prod her more, I wait. Even though it takes all my self-control.*
*After a shuddery breath, she looks at me, eyes still watering.*
Marina: "I messed up, Amber."
Marina: "Bad."
Amber: "How?"
*Her breath catches again, and she sobs. It’s hoarse and seems to take her by surprise. I immediately rub her shoulders again.*
*And then there’s a knock at the RV door.*
Amber: "What the hell?"
*I stand up, but I can’t see who it is from here.*
Amber: "I’ll be right back."
*I unlock the front door and open it to an all-too familiar face.*
Mariah: "It’s time to pay up!"
*I shut the door.*
*Before it’s even fully latched, Mariah starts to pound on the door.*
*I lock it and head back to Marina.*
Marina: "Let them in."
Amber: "Huh?"
Marina: "Go let them in. It’s Mariah, right?"
Amber: "Yeah, but how did you…"
Amber: "Wait, does this have something to do with…"
*Marina nods.*
*With a bad feeling about it all, I go back to the door, which is still suffering Mariah’s assault. I open and step back so they can enter.*
*Mariah storms in with a huff, acting like always as if she owns the place.*
*Joseph and Tess file in after her. Joseph gives me a smile and a small shake of the head.*
Joseph: "Sorry."
*He says it so quietly that I don’t think anyone else hears. I figure I’ll find out why he’s apologizing here in a second.*
*Like a conqueror, Mariah marches over to Marina and jabs a finger in her face.*
Mariah: "Time to pay up, ditz!"
*In two large steps, I move between the two of them.*
Amber: "Okay, what the hell happened?"
Mariah: "Didn’t you hear?"
Amber: "I was about to, when you attacked my front door."
Mariah: "Well, allow me to deliver the bad news, then!"
Tess: "Sis stole Marina’s gold."
*It’s impossible to figure anything out. Marina is crying too hard to talk, and Mariah’s just yelling nonsense like always. I turn to Joe, who’s keeping a safe distance, along with Tess.*
Amber: "Can you please explain?"
Joseph: "Sure."
Joseph: "Mare ended up making Marina a bet for all the gold, which she lost. It was a pretty silly thing, and I told her to just let it go, but here we are."
*I’m equal parts amused and disgusted that Joseph uses the same nickname for Mariah that I do for Marina. Come to think of it, I’m not sure that I ever noticed how similar their names are.*
Mariah: "A bet’s a bet, and she lost fair and square."
Tess: "I don’t think it was fair."
Joseph: "The odds were kinda in your favor."
*I turn to Marina, the only person whose opinion really matters to me.*
Amber: "Was it fair?"
*Slowly, she nods.*
Amber: "What game did you play?"
Marina: "Poker."
*I glare at Mariah, who at least has the shame to look away. Anyone who’s spent more than a minute around Marina would know that there’s no way she could keep a straight face.*
*I sigh, and sit down on the bed beside Marina. I wrap an arm around her. She leans into me, eyes moist.*
Amber: "You know you can only take half the treasure, right? Marina and I split it equally, so she couldn’t bet away my half."
Mariah: "That wasn’t the deal."
Amber: "That’s the only deal she could’ve made."
Amber: "But."
Amber: "I’ll bet you my half against hers."
Marina: "Amber, no!"
*I squeeze her, affectionately, but I shake my head.*
Amber: "It’s fine."
*I stand and go to where we’ve been stashing the gold. I pull out the box of nuggets and open it, so Mariah can see.*
*Her eyes light up, and she licks her lips.*
*I snap the box shut.*
Mariah: "Fine. But we’re not playing poker."
Amber: "Oh yeah?"
Amber: "What game, then?"
*She thinks about it for a moment.*
Mariah: "Blackjack."
*Seriously? Blackjack is more about luck than it is skill.*
*I’m not that great at poker, though. So blackjack might be the most fair game we could play.*
*I wonder why Mariah suggested it, though. Maybe she expects me to win at poker. If that’s the case, then the mind games have already begun.*
Amber: "Fine. It’s a bet."
*Marina’s stopped crying. Her breath still comes in little spurts, but she’s mostly in control of herself.*
*She tugs at my shirt.*
Marina: "Amber, really. You don’t have to do this. I screwed up."
Amber: "It’s okay, I’ll fix it. That’s what I’m here for, right?"
*I can’t read the look on her face. The one that vanishes after a split second.*
Marina: "Okay."
*Facing Mariah again, I put the gold away.*
Mariah: "There’s a casino close by, down the street from this one. We can do it there."
Amber: "I’m not old enough to gamble. Not legally, at least."
*She shrugs.*
*I don’t like the sound of that. I feel like I’m being set up.*
Marina: "It’s true. It’s where we played."
Amber: "Really?"
Marina: "Yeah. They had like, party rooms that were empty. We just used one of those."
*I’ve never heard of anything like this place, but if they let Marina in, then they’ll probably let me in, too.*
*I’d also feel better about playing with cards that I know Mariah won’t have messed with.*
Amber: "Fine. Tell me how to get there, and I’ll meet you in a little bit."
*She gives me directions as well as the name of the building. Worst case, Marina can probably help find it. Maybe.*
*I count out Marina’s half of the gold and put it into one of the boxes before handing it to Mariah, who snatches it from my grip.*
Amber: "Enjoy, because this is the only time you’ll get to touch the treasure."
*With a final sneer, Mariah leads her troupe out of the RV. Joseph still looks pretty sorry about the whole thing.*
*The door slams shut and we’re left in silence.*
*I look at Marina. She looks at the floor.*
Marina: "I’m really sorry."
*I’m not even that upset. Compared to all the million and one awful scenarios I’d concocted, this is pretty tame.*
*And knowing Marina, it’s not that shocking, either.*
*So I’m being honest when I smile at her.*
Amber: "It’s okay. Really."
Amber: "I’ll go win back your gold, and then we can get outta Vegas."
*She sniffles.*
Amber: "Seriously though, what were you thinking?"
Marina: "I was just so surprised to see them here! I thought it must be fate or something. I was shopping around and I saw something that I really wanted but I didn’t have enough money."
Marina: "And then I saw the three of them at this little diner so I went in to say hello, and told them all about it."
Amber: "What was it you wanted to buy?"
Marina: "That’s a secret. I wanted to show it to you."
Marina: "But anyway, then Mariah made me that bet and I dunno, Amber. I guess I just got so excited about it that I didn’t really think."
Marina: "Like if you’d been there you woulda beat her no problem. I don’t even think she’s very good at poker."
Marina: "It’s just that… I’m kind of bad at hiding how I feel."
*I can’t help but laugh.*
Amber: "No way, you’re kidding me!"
*She sticks her tongue out at me. Guess she’s feeling a little bit better.*
Marina: "Really though, what if you lose to Mariah?"
Amber: "Then I lose. It won’t happen though."
Marina: "Are you good at blackjack?"
Amber: "Eh. A lot of it’s luck. There’s stuff you can memorize, like card combos, but I don’t know any of that."
Marina: "What if Mariah does?"
Amber: "I doubt it. She would’ve played that against you if she did. Easier to make it seem like you just got unlucky, instead of her taking advantage of you."
Marina: "She didn’t ta-"
Amber: "That’s bullshit, Mare. She might not have cheated but she still should’ve known better."
*Marina frowns and looks at the floor again.*
*I can’t tell if I’m helping or not.*
*Then I remember the candy I bought her earlier. It had totally slipped my mind over the last hour.*
Amber: "Oh yeah!"
Amber: "I got you something. To try to say sorry for being so weird lately. Even though I’ve already said that, too."
*I grab the bag of chocolates from the fridge and the gummies from above it.*
*Eyes wide, Marina holds them to her like babies.*
Marina: "Amber…"
*Her eyes start to water again.*
Marina: "Thank you so much."
Amber: "Hey, no more crying. This wasn’t supposed to make you cry."
*She giggles, but that doesn’t stop two tears from running down her face.*
Marina: "Sorry, I’m crying a lot, huh?"
Amber: "Kinda, yeah."
*Marina laughs again. She unties the gummy bag and digs around in it.*
Amber: "The chocolates got a little melted on the walk back, but they’ve been in the fridge."
Marina: "They look fine to me!"
*She pulls out a few gummy soda bottles and then offers me the bag.*
*I don’t wanna take too much, but I do find one of the peach rings the cashier recommended.*
*It really is good. Sweet, but in a mild way. It actually tastes like peaches.*
Amber: "Are you ready to go?"
*Marina spins the bag to twist it, then ties off the top.*
Marina: "Yeah."
*I vaguely know where the casino Mariah mentioned is located. We passed it earlier today, but I didn’t pay much attention to it.*
*As we load out of the RV, Marina’s dragging her feet.*
Amber: "Do you wanna wait here? I can just go take care of this and then come back."
Marina: "No way!"
Marina: "I don’t wanna split up again."
*After I lock up, I take her hand. Since I’m in the lead this time, the walk isn’t as slow.*
*With the evening approaching, the temperature’s gone down. At the same time, traffic is starting to build up.*
*In a few hours, it’ll look like an entirely different city here. Once all the lights come on.*
*The view of a big city at night, far in the distance, has always been one of my favorite sights.*
*I can remember staying up late when Gramps and I were on the road so that I could press like a sticker against the window and look.*
*They sell postcards of New York and Atlanta and other places all lit up, because it’s so pretty.*
*We’ve got a couple hours until then.*
*Neither Marina nor I say much while we walk. Even given the situation, it doesn’t feel as awkward as some of our quiet times before.*
*Soon, we reach the building Mariah described.*
Amber: "This the place?"
Marina: "Yeah, this is it."
*We step inside.*
*I can see why Mariah said this place might be closed soon. Although it’s pretty clean looking, there’s hardly anyone here.*
*A couple of people occupy slot machines, far away from each other. Aside from those two, and a bored looking host, the place is pretty empty.*
*Well, those three and the Trio. Mariah and her posse are seated on a semicircular couch. It and an identical couch form a circle around a card table.*
*No greetings are exchanged as Marina and I take our places across from them.*
*A sealed deck of cards sits on the table. It’s got gaudy Las Vegas landmarks and logos all over it, but at least it’s legit.*
Amber: "Should we ask that guy to come deal for us?"
Mariah: "Hell no. The only reason they don’t check ID is because they don’t care."
Mariah: "If you attract any attention they’ll throw you out."
Amber: "Okay, so who will?"
Mariah: "Tess can."
*Tess nods.*
Amber: "Really?"
Amber: "No offense, but does she even know how?"
Mariah: "Of course she knows how, she’s not an idiot."
*Tess nods again.*
Tess: "Just ask."
Amber: "Alright. That works for me."
*Because if two underage girls are going to play, then why not have a ten year old deal the cards?*
*Mariah slits open the plastic wrap on the deck and then hands it to Tess. The little girl takes out the instructional cards and the jokers and then shuffles.*
*I have to say, I’m impressed. She creates a bridge with the cards that’s way tidier than I could ever do.*
*Mechanical, even.*
Amber: "So there are sixteen pieces total. We’ll go piece by piece, I guess?"
Mariah: "Sure."
*That means that I’d have to win eight hands in a row, without Mariah or the dealer winning any, to earn back all of Marina’s treasure. And I won’t settle for anything less than the full amount.*
*We have a small stack of tokens near us that each represent a single piece of treasure.*
Marina: "How does this game work?"
Amber: "Basically, you try to get the cards in your hand to add up to 21, or as close as possible without going over."
Amber: "Whoever’s closest wins that round. The dealer can win too."
Amber: "We all get two cards. Then, you can stand, which is where you don’t take any more cards, or you can hit, which is where you draw another."
Marina: "That sounds really easy."
Amber: "I guess. Like I said, it’s mostly luck."
Mariah: "Shut up. Time to play."
*I flip her off but don’t argue.*
*Tess deals us the first hand. I stand while Mariah hits. I don’t expect her to beat my 18, but she lays down a ten, a three and a six.*
Amber: "Damn."
*Tess slides her two tokens.*
*On the next round, I beat her.*
*I win the next two rounds after that, but then the dealer wins and we have to start over.*
*The game drags on for a long time. Joseph has fallen asleep, head lolling backwards over the couch.*
*Even Marina, who has both her honor and money at stake here, is starting to fidget.*
*We’ve been playing for close to an hour. She doesn’t complain at all, though.*
*After a while, even Mariah and I are starting to get annoyed. She sighs and shifts irritatedly while I debate what to do.*
*And when she takes her time thinking, I bite down the urge to say something snarky and hurry her along.*
*Once Tess wins again and we’re both sitting at three pieces each, Mariah wipes her palms and clears her throat.*
Mariah: "This is taking forever."
Amber: "Yep."
Amber: "But if you’re saying we just call it here, the answer is no."
Mariah: "No."
Mariah: "But let’s just go all or nothing. One final hand."
Mariah: "Then you won’t be able to say I cheated, or took advantage of anyone, and I can finally relax with my treasure."
*I think about it. One more hand, huh? The odds are probably better than if we were to sit here all night.*
*Maybe. I’m not good enough at math to calculate.*
*It would be possible that we’re here until the festival comes around, if we kept going back and forth like this.*
*I look over at Marina, whose interest has been piqued again.*
Amber: "What do you think?"
*She gives it a long thought, her face betraying nothing.*
*Then she shrugs.*
Marina: "I have no idea."
*Right then.*
Amber: "Fine. All or nothing."
*Mariah nods, satisfied.*
Mariah: "Deal ‘em, kid."
*Tess passes out our cards.*
*I pick mine up, holding them so only Marina can see.*
*A nine and a seven. Not awful, but not great.*
*Tess is showing an eight.*
*I try to gauge some sort of reaction from Mariah, but her face is totally expressionless. No wonder she beat Marina at poker.*
Tess: "Amber?"
*I think that’s the first time she’s ever said my name. It sounds weird coming from her.*
Amber: "I’ll stand."
*If I were to take a hit and draw another card, there’s a pretty good chance it’d put me over blackjack. And that would be a loss by default.*
Tess: "Sis?"
Mariah: "Hit me."
*Tess slides her another card, then flips one over the hole card.*
*It’s a four, for a grand total of 12. That means we still beat her, at least.*
Tess: "Let’s see."
*I lay down my cards, and wait for Mariah to do the same.*
*Except she doesn’t.*
*She just looks at my cards, flat on the table. Face stony, mouth silent.*
*Then she makes a sound of disgust and stands as she flings her three cards towards the table. I flip them over to see a three, a two, and a ten.*
Amber: "We win."
*That’s all I say.*
*Marina looks at me, then at the table, then back to me. Making sure she heard me right.*
Marina: "We did?"
Amber: "Yeah. The total of our hand was closer to 21 than hers was."
*Strangely, I don’t even feel excited. Not about the victory, which is nice, or the fact that it came over Mariah, which is nicer. I just feel relieved.*
*Marina squeals and then explodes towards me, half leaping, half tackling me into a hug as I nearly fall off the couch. She plants a large kiss on my cheek, and then travels over to my mouth.*
Marina: "Oh God, Amber! I was so worried but oh my God! Thank you! You’re the best!"
*Her praises are dotted with kisses all over my face.*
*Joseph, woken up by the commotion, looks away with a blush. Tess, on the other hand, stares at us, creepily apathetic.*
*I separate myself from Marina and stand up. Mariah has already traveled halfway to the door, not saying anything in her defeat.*
*The little wooden box of gold sits abandoned where her arm was. I check and count the pieces of treasure. They’re all there.*
*I start to move towards Mariah, to say something, but Joseph clucks his tongue. I stop.*
Joseph: "Don’t worry about her. She’s always like this."
Amber: "Sorry."
*Not sure what else to say, I half-shrug. Which is hard, since Marina still clings to my arm.*
Joseph: "Don’t be. Better things work out this way."
Joseph: "C’mon, kiddo."
*He takes Tess’ hand and they start to follow Mariah, who’s left the building.*
Joseph: "You ladies take care."
Amber: "You too."
Marina: "See you later!"
*Once they leave, I look down at the mess of chips and cards on the table. Someone will come by and clean them up.*
*Marina tugs on my shirt. When I look at her, she kisses me on the lips again.*
*For the first time today, we stay like that for a long time. Not caring about how many stares we might be getting. Though it can’t be more than three.*
*I can still taste a faint saltiness from her tears.*
*I feel her lips curve into a smile even before we pull apart.*
Marina: "Thank you. Seriously."
Marina: "I know I’m always saying ‘thank you’ and ‘sorry.’ But I am sorry that I screw up so much."
Amber: "Don’t worry about it. It all worked out, right?"
Marina: "I dunno what I would’ve done if you weren’t."
Amber: "Well if I wasn’t then you wouldn’t be either, you know?"
*I mean it as a joke, but she doesn’t laugh. Just leans into me.*
Marina: "I know."
*Then we kiss again.*
*We must look like perverts, just standing in the middle of a casino making out.*
Amber: "You ready to get out of Vegas?"
Marina: "Definitely. But um, one thing first."
Amber: "What’s that?"
Marina: "Can we get something to eat?"
Amber: "I was hoping you’d say that. I saw this Chinese place earlier that smelled amazing. Sound good?"
Marina: "Very."
*Hand in hand like always, we leave the casino and make the quick walk to the restaurant. I’d come across it while waiting for Marina before.*
*All along the Strip, more and more lights are coming on.*
*It’s hard to tell that it’s getting much darker out, because of how much artificial light there is.*
*We get to the restaurant and eat outside on the patio, watching as more and more buildings are illuminated.*
*By the time we’re done and back at the RV, Las Vegas has shifted fully from daytime to artificial-daytime.*
*Before we buckle in, I pull back all of the curtains.*
Amber: "Make sure you get a good spot by the window."
Amber: "You’re gonna wanna see this."
*It starts slowly. Just a few lights in apartment windows coming to life, or lonely streetlights flickering on.*
*I drive slowly, so as to keep us here longer.*
*Beside me, Marina shifts impatiently but doesn’t say anything. By now, she knows when I’m being serious.*
*I know she won’t be disappointed.*
*As more and more lights flare up across the skyline, I can see Marina shift to get a better view. I smile to myself. This is what I was waiting for.*
*What she was waiting for, even if she didn’t know it.*
*Las Vegas lights up like a forest of Christmas trees, glimmering orange and yellow against the muddy blackness of the night sky.*
*The further away from the heart of the city we get, the more of its gorgeous sprawl we can see. Soon, it’s so immense that the entire world seems to be awash with forced color.*
*I ignore the signs that will lead us all the way out of Las Vegas and instead cruise along the streets that will let us admire for longer.*
*Marina’s head slowly turns with every bend in the road, her gaze magnetically fastened to the city.*
*Skyscrapers zoom by, each one another pillar of light.*
*The casinos, which have gaudier decorations, make an exotic contrast to the mundane office and apartment buildings.*
*Marina drinks it all in like water, out here in the desert.*
*Light from the window frames her face, and I remember how I thought of her as angelic back at Zion.*
*Now, the word is even more fitting, because of the way her open-mouthed smile enhances the effect.*
*She catches me watching and, for just a second, that smile is directed at me. Then she turns back to Vegas, which is finally starting to recede as we head out of town.*
*She’s silent until long after it’s faded from the rear view mirror. Then she clears her throat, and finally looks at me again.*
Marina: "We’re going back one day, right?"
Amber: "Sure, if you want."
Marina: "Good! Because we didn’t even get to do, like, a nickle—"
Amber: "Quarter."
Amber: "Oh yeah? Like what."
*Even if I do live like a gross hermit, I know what Vegas has to offer. But I like seeing her excited. There’s always a genuine spark of wonder that’s ignited when she is. It might have to do with her lack of experience, but I like to think she’d be the same regardless.*
Marina: "Well, there’s this pirate stunt show that looks really cool! I heard about another show with fountains and lights that shoots water waaaaaay up high into the air, and—oooh!"
*She spreads her arms far apart and scrunches her face down, giving herself a double chin.*
Marina: "I wanna try every single buffet in town!"
Marina: "But we’re rich now!"
*I thump her head and squish her down with the palm of my hand.*
Amber: "And we almost lost all of that in a single day, so you should know how easy it is to blow it."
*Guess I have some high expectations to live up to.*
*I’ve always had them, but now it feels more official. Now, more than ever, I’m obligated to keep her happy and safe.*
*Those thoughts occupy my mind for an hour or so as we traverse the desert highway. Marina’s gone back to staring out the window at the barely-visible roadside.*
*We pass a sign right off the side of our deserted lane. We’re pushing eighty so we almost miss it, but I catch a quick glimpse as we speed by.*
*Nothing special, just a dusty sheet of aluminum with a picture of poppies and three words printed on it.*
*Welcome to California.*
*It’s gone before I can even process it.*
*Huh. I guess we’re in California.*
*It doesn’t look like Marina noticed and why would she?*
*We’ve passed several state lines and it was never a big deal then. No welcoming party or even a government checkpoint, and that suits me just fine.*
*She hasn't brought up her 'surprise' anymore either, whatever it was. Probably better to just let her forget about that.*
*But that doesn’t mean the day is over yet, and the places that geezer would always yack on about may as well be stapled onto my brain.*
Amber: "Hey. You trust me?"
*Her head perks up and she turns away from the window, answering without delay.*
Marina: "Of course I do!"
Marina: "Why?"
Amber: "Just checking."
*And that’s that. No more questions. Just blind trust.*
