*I continue driving, eventually branching off onto an exit. Another thing I don’t think Marina notices.*
*Soon, budding lights start popping up in the distance. Typical yellows and whites, barely hovering above the road—with one exception.*
*Towering on the horizon, outlined in neon red with digital numbers climbing towards the sky, is a giant monstrosity of a thermometer. It caps off above the middle, reaching temperatures all the way up to 62 degrees.*
*Finally, it dawns on Marina. Her head twists towards me like an owl, her eyes just as wide as one.*
Marina: "Amber, what’s going on?"
*She feigns concern at first, but her voice curls up mid-sentence, unable to hide her smile any longer.*
Amber: "You said you trust me, right?"
Marina: "And I do."
*Marina tilts over and rubs up on my shoulder. I go dead stiff. Again, feeling the weight of that trust and responsibility.*
*We drive into town, which as far as I can tell, is just one long street. Old, beat up gas stations and fast food joints line the side of the road, separated by an occasional diner.*
*The thermometer is tucked away, hidden in a corner, but it may as well be the center of the universe, the rest of the world zooming by it on the interstate.*
*I pull into the lot by the thermometer, its only neighbor being a small shack. The sound of the ignition dwindles down to nothing, leaving the RV parked wherever.*
*I’m the first one to get out, craving the sweet, stationary concrete as I stretch my legs.*
*Wobbling in front of the thermometer, I flop my hands at my sides, dotting our arrival.*
Amber: "Here we are. The World’s Tallest Thermometer."
Amber: "I thought you might like it."
*A smug grin finds a home on my face, as I revel in the pride and satisfaction of knowing I did something to make her smile.*
*Except she’s not smiling—she’s staring. Not at the thermometer now, but me. A near lifeless gaze, cloaked by the dimming headlights of the motorhome.*
Amber: "Uh, Marina?"
*Readying her stance, she pushes her feet off the ground and charges towards me.*
Marina: "If we’re doing this, then we’re doing it right!"
*Too late.*
*Before I can even finish my plea, she swings around my neck and snuggly brings me to her side, swooshing out the camera and aiming it right at us.*
Marina: "Hey Aaaamber."
*Her finger inches closer to the button.*
Amber: "Don’t do it!"
Marina: "Saaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa-"
*She draws out the word to an excruciating length. I struggle to break free but she just squeezes me tighter.*
Amber: "I’m warning you!"
Marina: "-aaaay cheese."
*She bends in and presses her lips against my cheek.*
*Hold and flash.*
Amber: "Huh."
*I stare at the camera, mind blank, my non-reaction drawing out a cute giggle from her.*
*And then it hits me. A thought so mortifying and embarrassing that it shades my skin a pink and red gradient—she thinks this is cute.*
*At this point, I’m butter. Or clay, from Marina’s perspective.*
*And that’s exactly what she treats me as, molding me into new positions as she snaps more photos.*
*She’s so happy.*
*Before she winds for the next photo, I nudge her head with mine. She nudges back, both of us cuddling against each other as she snaps the next picture.*
*She takes several more shots, each one a little more gooberish and drenched in cheesy romantic goo than the last.*
*I’ll probably die if anyone ever sees these. But with any luck, Marina’s constant bouncing will hopefully make a few of these come out fuzzy.*
*Still. I think I might love this. Like, a lot.*
*When we first met, I didn’t think I’d ever understand the naive ball of energy just looking for some help. In a lot of ways, I still don’t. I drove by at such a perfect time, who even knows what would have happened to her. Thinking about it still drives me nuts.*
*But still, those types of things don’t seem nearly as big as they used to. Even almost losing the treasure to Mariah was just a sizzling drop in the frying pan.*
*If anything, I’m more upset that I wasn’t there to help her when it went down. After all, that’s why I’m here—to help and protect her.*
*She makes me so happy.*
*With my hand now wrapped around Marina, I clench her tighter, only for my hand to be met with an adorable gurgling rumble transmitted from her stomach.*
*We both look down at the source, her with a bashful sort of terror and me with amused disbelief.*
Amber: "Even after all that food at the buffet?"
Marina: "I can’t help it!"
Amber: "You’re a bottomless pit—the Marina Trench."
Marina: "Amber please, that one wasn’t even clever."
*I cackle with glee, tossing her the keys to the RV as I walk over to the nearby shack.*
Amber: "I’m just messin’ with ya, babe. Go lock up. We’ll grab a bite and sleep here for the night."
*The shack is sad and abandoned, the cute logo of a little chef carrying an oversized bun all but faded away.*
*I peek inside the browning windows. It’s more of the same. Rusty and decaying, outlines of tables and booths are imprinted in dust, while the ones built into the walls were just left to rot.*
*It was always just a side thing he mentioned when talking about the thermometer, but not being able to experience everything he mentioned feels wrong. Like I’m not doing right by him.*
*Damn.*
Amber: "Looks like that’s one more thing I can check off your list."
*Marina waltzes into the diner, keeping the door open for me as we scurry in from the breezy entryway.*
*Immediately, she’s drawn to the claw machine in the corner, filled to the brim with non-specific and unlicensed stuffed animals.*
*Her eyes glitter as she tries to get me to notice the glass box of wonders.*
*Coming from anyone else, this action would be subtle, maybe a few quick glances at most.*
*Coming from Marina—not so much. Instead, she repeatedly swings her head in the direction of the machine, like she’s trying to form a giant crescent moon with her neck.*
*But I know Marina well enough now. Well enough to know exactly which one she wants.*
*Snuggled tight between a fuzzy ball with googly eyes and a lion with a crown lies the holy grail.*
*A gray wolf with a polka-dot bowtie.*
*Unfortunately for her, our waitress is already waving us over to our seats. Marina stares into the glass, her foggy reflection lining up with the wolf.*
*I take her hand and lead her to the booth. Once there’s some distance between us and the waitress, I lower my voice.*
Amber: "Don’t worry. I’ll give it a whirl after we eat."
*Marina, however, can’t take a hint, and blurts out her response, alerting every weary soul in the diner.*
Marina: "Woah! You actually know how to win at those? I've never been any good at them."
*A counter lined with Jumbo clones turns around and glares at us before shaking their heads and returning to their meals.*
*Oh well.*
Amber: "Of course I do. You know how many of these diners I went to as a kid?"
Amber: "When you eat at the same place almost every day, you get good. Fast."
Marina: "Wow… you’re like a claw goddess."
Amber: "Damn right."
*Marina ogles me like the ‘claw goddess’ I am as we take our seats at the booth. I gladly accept her glory and praise.*
*The waitress flops the menus on our table and silently waits for us to order our drinks.*
Amber: "Just coffee for me."
*With her eyes glazing over, she turns to Marina.*
Marina: "Hmm. What kinda shakes do you have?"
*The waitress reaches her wrinkly hands across the table and tosses the dessert menu in front of Marina.*
Marina: "Hmmmmmmmmm."
*She flips through the menus and strains her eyes shut, focusing on what very well may be the most important decision of her life.*
Marina: "Amber, you got coffee, right?"
Amber: "Right."
Marina: "Then that’s what I’ll get!"
*Overflowing with certainty, she stares at the waitress and gives her a direct, confident command.*
Marina: "I’ll have the coffee shake."
Marina: "Extra whipped cream."
*The waitress doesn’t even bother to write this down, instead dragging her body back to her tomb in the kitchen.*
Amber: "You know that shake isn’t going to taste the same as my coffee, right? It’s just gonna be a ton of ice cream and cheap coffee flavoring."
Marina: "Yeah…"
Marina: "But I at least gotta try it! For your sake!"
Amber: "Why would you choke down a crappy shake for my sake?"
Marina: "You do everything for me. Everythiiiiing."
Amber: "‘Course I do. That’s my job."
*A stiff chuckle slips out from under her breath.*
Marina: "Right…"
Marina: "But I wanna do things for you, too!"
Amber: "So you’re ordering the coffee shake as a cosmic favor? You’re losing me, Mare."
*She whines, momentary frustration seeping onto her face.*
Marina: "No, not like that."
Marina: "So I guess it’s not just for your sake, but for both our sakes!"
*She smiles and rests her head to the side, as if expecting my shoulder to be there like always.*
*Realizing she’s only leaning on air, Marina blinks, then tries to play it off with a tiny cough.*
Amber: "I saaaw that."
*Before I can take my teasing to more exciting places, the waitress returns from the kitchen, barely managing to balance our drinks on the platter as my coffee splashes everywhere.*
*She doesn’t seem to care.*
*She places our drinks on the table, then limps out her notepad and pen to take our orders.*
Amber: "Thanks, but I’ll stick with coffee."
*Marina is too distracted by the shake to notice. Her eyes climb up the tower of ‘extra’ whipped cream as it twirls to the ceiling, rivaling the thermometer outside.*
Amber: "Snap to. What do ya want?"
Marina: "Oh!"
*She opens up the big menu and flips through the pages—multiple times, front to back, side-to-side, even upside, repeating the process every time.*
Amber: "Before we miss the festival, Mare."
Marina: "I know, I know, but there’s so much to choose from! I have to be picky!"
Amber: "You didn’t have a problem vacuuming up everything at the buffet."
*Marina drops the menu. If it were a few pounds heavier I’m pretty sure she would have dropped it on my feet.*
*Handing the menus to the waitress, she leans over the table, inches away from my face. She places her order. Her pupils burn with intensity.*
Marina: "I’ll have the pancake sandwich."
*Dead. Serious.*
*The waitress wobbles away, somehow not collapsing on her way back to the kitchen.*
*I try not to burst out laughing at Marina, but it doesn’t work. The cracks are showing. Stilted snorts and spurts of snickering keep leaking out.*
*Marina’s cheeks puff out, getting brighter as I bubble over.*
Marina: "Why do you have to tease me about everything, Amber?"
*I break. My laugh echoes across the diner. Again, we’re met with glares, but I don’t even care.*
Marina: "Fine! Maybe I won’t share the treasure with you after all!"
*I wave off her empty threat and settle down.*
Amber: "I’m sorry, I’m sorry."
Amber: "It’s just that…"
*Done laughing, I rest my chin on the table and start fumbling with the salt and pepper shakers.*
*Marina has already reverted back to her usual self, watching me pass the salt and pepper back and forth, like she wants to join in too.*
Amber: "It’s so weird. Just two huge pancakes with eggs, bacon, and ham smashed in the middle. Thing’s drenched in syrup."
Marina: "That sounds super tasty, though!"
*I chuckle and slide her the pepper.*
Amber: "I’m sure you’ll love it."
*Now it’s just the salt shaker, sliding between my hands.*
Amber: "He never eats the eggs. Always just passes them onto me."
Amber: "They’re so gross. Just scrambled and cold and sticky."
Amber: "I always tell him no, but the dork just laughs and says he’s gonna keep ‘egg’n me on.’"
Amber: "Every single time."
*Marina laughs at the stupid joke. I laugh too.*
Amber: "We would just end up bringing it home in a box. And when it went bad he would just take it out of the fridge and say ‘That’s all, yolks!’ Like it was the most clever freakin’ thing."
*She laughs even harder at that one. I think I do too.*
*I keep my eyes focused on the shaker, sliding it faster and faster.*
Amber: "When we go home, you two really need to get together and me—"
*The salt shaker trips on my pinkie and topples over, spilling a few grains of salt.*
*Marina is tense, but forces a smile anyway.*
*She sighs. We both look down and go silent.*
Amber: "I, uh—I think I’m gonna head back to the RV."
*I get up and reach into my pocket, placing a bill on the table.*
Marina: "Amber—"
Amber: "Don’t worry, I’ll be fine. Just need some time."
*Leaving Marina behind me, I walk out of the diner.*
*Blazing hot tears stream down my cheeks as I stumble through the motorhome.*
*I’m choking. I’m choking and there’s nothing I can do to stop it.*
*How could I forget, even only for a second. Am I really that heartless?*
*I want to throw up. I can barely stand, tearing down all the magnets on the fridge as I trip over my own tears.*
*The next thing I know, I’m falling onto the bed, and even though I land on its stiff, concrete springs, I still feel like I’m falling.*
*The ceiling is getting further and further away as I sink deeper.*
*I jerk myself up, faced with the photo that has comforted and haunted me for so long.*
*For a second, I smile again, too. But then I remember, and it fades just as quickly.*
*The tears start flowing again, harder than ever before. I curl up in a ball and bury my face into my knees.*
*It doesn’t help muffle my crying, cover the shame or even make me feel any better. I just cry.*
*The cracks have been forming for a while and now I’ve shattered. I’m not sure if I’ll ever be able to glue myself back together.*
*It never occurred to me just how hard it would be when he was gone.*
*Growing up, I wasn’t just his responsibility, we were each other’s responsibility.*
*How many credit cards did I max up in those last few months? Even after he told me to stop, I kept pushing.*
*Bank calls, doctor calls, middle of the night ER visits—everything.*
*He held up his end of the bargain. Through every discovery, every stupid heartbreak, and every fight with Ma’. He always took care of me and always just loved me.*
*I couldn’t hold up mine, though. I failed. Even now, I keep finding holes in everything I did.*
*I could have found a specialty hospital. I could have read up more on new forms of medicine, too.*
*I could have, but I didn’t.*
*And now he’s gone.*
*My pants are damp, they’ve been soaking up all my tears, but I don’t care. I just want my grandpa back.*
*The motorhome door opens and brushes against the outside wall, followed by soft footsteps that creak along the floor.*
*Marina.*
*I try to act quick, sneaking a final few sniffs in before wiping away my tears, but it’s no use, they just keep trickling down.*
*She appears in the doorway. I turn away and drown out the sobbing with my hand.*
*It’s so embarrassing. She shouldn’t have to see me like this, but I don’t think I can muster enough composure to ask her to leave without breaking down.*

Marina: "‘Morning."
Amber: "Sorry, did I wake you up?"
Marina: "Mnm."

*My voice is garbled, muffled by the toothbrush stuffed in the corner of my mouth.*
Amber: "Kinda. I have a few ideas."
*Spit.*

*Marina nuzzles against my cheek, bringing with her an aching burden that presses down on my entire being.*

*I rip myself away from her.*
Amber: "Anyway, get ready. We’re gonna hit the road as soon as you’re done."
*I rush to the sink. Steam rises out of the cup as my shaky hand pours the coffee, splattering most of it on the counter.*
*Steadying myself doesn’t work. My body won’t listen. The burning coffee splashes on my skin as I wrench it to my mouth. It scalds my tongue and blisters down my throat. The pain sizzles all the way to my stomach.*
*I gasp out a short scream, but quickly muffle it with my hand. With tears forming, I turn and make sure Marina didn’t hear it over the running water.*
*Once I’m sure, I wipe my eyes. After several sharp breaths, I pour myself another cup.*
*I drag myself to the driver’s seat and slam my cup in the holder, then plant my head on the steering wheel. It honks.*
*The handles from the bathroom squeak and the sound of water is reduced to a drip.*
*A few moments later, Marina trots down the motorhome and hops into her seat.*
*I jerk up my head.*
Amber: "All set?"
Marina: "Yup!"
Marina: "But…"

Amber: "Don’t worry, everything’s fine. Just a little tired."
*Marina studies me for a few seconds, then shrugs and looks out the window, happily staring at the giant thermometer outside.*

*Please don’t hurt her. Please.*

Amber: "Oh that’s great."
*Everything she’s been saying for the past few hours has been zooming right past me.*

*The drive hasn’t done much to soothe the budding anxiety inside me.*
*In fact, I’d say it’s just made it worse.*
*After four states worth of desert and endless stretches of road, it’s maddening to see it all get mauled over by overpasses and the slowest, most painful and rage-inducing traffic I’ve ever seen.*

Marina: "I’ve never seen so many…"

Amber: "Hey, uh, do you think we could actually be quiet for a little while?"
Marina: "Oh sure, that’s fine."
Amber: "Thanks."

*I don’t even want to consider it, but it adds up.*
*I was lonely, didn’t have anyone else. Saw a cute girl, picked her up and consistently used her to curb that, all while telling myself that I was just helping and protecting her.*
*And then I convinced myself of more, hoping to forget about the most important person in the world to me.*

*Tears start to swell up deep inside me, but it just morphs into fury.*
Marina: "Check it out! That mountain still has snow—"
Amber: "GODDAMMIT SHUT UP!" with hpunch
*As if the universe were conspiring against me, a deafening explosion blasts in front of us." with hpunch

Amber: "Dammit! Not now!"

Amber: "Shit!"

Amber: "How the hell would I know?"

Amber: "Do I look like a mechanic to you?"
*Marina wraps herself in the security of her arms and looks to the windmills, their wings casting thick, brief shadows over us.*

Amber: "You thought? The last time you thought, you ended up broken down on the side of the road."
Amber: "So maybe it would be better for both of us if you continued not to think."

Amber: "Yeah, alright. I understand."
Amber: "Thanks."

*Hotel sheets are surprisingly soft. A hell of a lot softer than the stiff rags I’ve been sleeping on lately.*

Amber: "I was angry."

Amber: "I’m sorry."
*The crowd moves on. I don't bother raising my head, allowing myself to be dragged along wherever they're going.*
*My hand is tucked deep within my pocket, playing around with the cassette and its string of tattered ribbon.*
*The hotel is far in the distance now, lit up against the darkening sky. Marina's probably still up there crying.*

Amber: "O-Oh."

Mariah: "Hah! I knew you numbskulls couldn’t live without me!"

*My experience with alcohol is really limited. Gramps didn’t keep a lot of booze around, and when he did it was always mead or wine.*
*I only snuck some out a few times for ‘special’ dates.*
Mariah: "Gotta say, you’re a lot less bearable without the ditz holding you on a leash."
Amber: "Shut up!"
*She barely held the flame to the fuse, but that single word is enough to make something inside of me go off.*
Amber: "Don’t you talk about her that way! She’s a kinder, more caring person than you’ll ever be!"
*Mariah scrunches her nose, like my outburst didn’t make any sense to her dumb, drunk face.*
Mariah: "That’s not a very high bar."
*She’s not wrong, but couldn’t she have at least tried defending herself?*
*I groan and let my blood simmer down, but not before spitting out one more shot of venom.*
Amber: "Whatever. It’s not like you’re any better. You’re a mess without the other two keeping you in check."
Mariah: "Hey! Don’t get any ideas! I’m the one keeping them in check."
Mariah: "They’d be dead by now if it weren’t for me."
*Sure, Mariah.*
*I pinch the bridge of my nose and shake my head with a short laugh.*
*Whether or not I choose to accept that Mariah exists in my reality, she’s at least something familiar. ‘Comforting’ probably isn’t the right word for it, but she’s someone to talk to.*
Amber: "Why are you here, anyway?"
Mariah: "Who even knows?"
Mariah: "Joe mentioned something about a music orgy thing."
Amber: "‘Music orgy?’ You mean the festival?"
Mariah: "Yeah, that."
Amber: "Huh. And you went along with it? I guess that makes sense. You guys are a thing, right?"
*Building up from a bunch of snorts, Mariah bursts out laughing.*
Mariah: "Hah! Me and that slacker wouldn’t be a thing if the planets collided, wiped out everything, formed a mega-planet and we had to repopulate."
*Why do I get the feeling this exact scenario has been played out in her journal multiple times?*
Mariah: "Besides, there’s more important things to focus on."
Mariah: "Or, at least…"
*She looks at the drawer again then glares at me.*
Mariah: "There were."
*I hide the temptation to laugh in her face and take another gulp.*
Amber: "So nothing, huh? You grow up with someone and don’t feel the tiniest thing for them?"
Mariah: "Eh."
*She takes another swig, but this time just gives the wall a dry, blank stare.*
Mariah: "If he’s still single in ten years, maybe I’ll marry him out of pity."
*I bet she’d marry the treasure in a heartbeat, though. Probably do more than just marry it.*
Mariah: "What about you and the ditz? Aren’t you two going to the music orgy together?"
*Her eyes narrow.*
Mariah: "I’ll kill you if that’s what you’re using my treasure for, by the way."
*I don’t answer, looking at my now near-empty bottle instead.*
Mariah: "What? You two have a fight?"
Amber: "Something like that."
Mariah: "I bet it was because of the gold. No honor among thieves, I tell ya."
Amber: "No. Nothing like that."
Amber: "It’s… It’s hard to explain."
Mariah: "Well, I don’t really care, but go ahead and moan, if you want. It’s not any different than what you usually do."
*I look at Mariah. She doesn’t seem particularly invested, interested in downing more beers than anything.*
*She’s a maniac; that has always been, and still is, abundantly clear. She probably would tear apart the entire Southwest if left unattended. Hell, maybe even the entire nation.*
*She’s no Joe. No way she could hold up an actual conversation. No way she could accidentally offer some good advice. No way she feels any sympathy.*
*Still, I feel like I can vent to her.*
*Not trust her. Not after all the RV chases and games of blackjack. But vent.*
*She doesn’t care, but maybe that’s what makes it so appealing.*
*I stay silent and stare at the ceiling, mulling over everything that’s happened, searching for the right words, words I couldn’t even give to Marina.*
Mariah: "I said that I don’t care, not that I’m willing to wait all night so you can steal my time too."
Amber: "Just give me a minute! I’m not really used to… this."
*Taking a deep breath, I look past Mariah, unwilling to let her see my shame.*
*The single shred of pride I have left snaps and I let all the weakness come fumbling out.*
Amber: "I met Marina a little after my grandpa died."
Amber: "He was…"
*My chest tightens.*
Amber: "He was my best friend."
Amber: "My parents didn’t want me, but he did."
Amber: "He raised me in that motorhome. He didn’t have to, but he took care of me anyway."
*Mariah sips at her beer, listening in silence. No smarmy remarks or deluded rambling.*
Amber: "So when I was a kid, I decided I was gonna take care of him too. Gratitude, y’know?"
Amber: "Gratitude for a lot of things."
*I stifle a laugh.*
Amber: "God, the music."
Amber: "The goof could spout random trivia about the weirdest music—bands you’ve never even heard of."
Amber: "It sounds dumb. A little girl feeling responsible for some full-grown geezer."
Amber: "I didn’t care, though. I wanted to prove how much he meant to me."
Amber: "And that’s how it worked for the longest time."
Amber: "But then he got sick."
*I funnel down the beer and reach for another one. Mariah shoves her hand in front of me and pops off the cap, right as the next cycle of heartbreak starts closing in.*
*I whisk back the bottle and force it down. Soapy foam rushes to the top and spills over as I pull it back. Mariah only shrugs.*
Amber: "I failed at my job, but I could at least take the trip for him and try to make up for it."
Amber: "Then I met Marina."
*A smile slips onto my face. Despite everything, even just saying her name makes me feel cheerful.*
Amber: "She was really cute and needed a ride, not to mention oblivious. Maybe it was just instinct, but I wasn’t gonna let someone so innocent fend for themselves in the desert."
Amber: "I didn’t plan on sticking around for long, but when we found the first part of the treasure, I… I don’t know. "
Amber: "I told myself I was gonna stick with her because I had to pay off Gramps’ hospital bills and funeral."
Amber: "But the truth is, I stuck with her because I felt like it was a second chance."
Amber: "I could do some good. Help and protect someone who really needed it."
Amber: "And the closer I got with Marina, the more I felt like she was my responsibility."
Amber: "But even more than that, when I was with her…"
*My voice cracks, trembling under the weight of my words.*
Amber: "I was happy."
Amber: "It was like Gramps was never even gone—then I remembered he was and I completely shut down. Marina tried comforting me, but I just took advantage of her."
Amber: "When I woke up, I realized that’s what I’d been doing this entire time."
Amber: "I was never protecting her. I was just using her to cope with Gramps."
Amber: "So I bought her a ticket home."
Amber: "She yelled and cried, but I did it anyway."
Amber: "I didn’t just fail Gramps, I made this trip about me, and I hurt Marina."
Amber: "I don’t deserve to be happy, and I don’t deserve her."
*Soaking in the silence, I blink away an annoying watery speck in my eye and fight back a few sniffles.*
Mariah: "You done?"
Amber: "Yeah."
*Mariah polishes off the rest of her drink and slams it down, rattling the other empty bottles on the table.*
*Smacking her lips, she stretches her neck. Each bone cracks one after the other, sounding off in a delayed trail.*
*Then, she turns to me and speaks.*
Mariah: "You’re a dumbass."
*I pause.*
Amber: "Excuse me?"
Mariah: "You heard me!"
*Mariah launches herself at me. I wince as her breath streaks out. The smell was bad before, but now it’s like she rinsed her mouth with whisky.*
Mariah: "God, you piss me off."
*A growl emerges from deep within her throat as she looks me over. Then, as if acting on simple instinctive desire, she delivers a stiff smack to the back of my head.*
*For a split second, everything is a fuzzy white. The infuriating image of a snarling Mariah greets me as my vision fades back, partnered with an even more infuriating throbbing.*
Amber: "OW! WHAT THE HELL?"
*Mariah stumbles off the couch, gripping the table for support. Her face has darkened by several shades of red since I’ve gotten here.*
Mariah: "Listen here, thief! Dead is dead is dead!"
Mariah: "Your dear old grandad ain’t crying up in Heaven because you decided to do some broad in the back of his RV!"
*Her words burn, right to their fiery core. I can’t believe I ever thought I could count on Mariah.*
Mariah: "I said shut up."
Mariah: "Get over your snoozefest of a sob story."
Mariah: "You got the girl and you got MY treasure!"
Mariah: "But noooooooooo. You can’t be happy because of some stupid, phony sense of obligation!"
Mariah: "AND THAT’S ANOTHER THING!"
*Her voice gets louder as her words get sloppier, slipping and sliding over each other.*
*I couldn’t get a word in even if I tried. All I can do is listen stone-faced as the lashings keep on coming.*
Mariah: "Who the hell do you think you are? What gives you the right to be some self-righteous guardian?"
Mariah: "You don’t look after people because you have to prove yourself! You do it because…"
*She stops and scratches her head. I lean in, my breath frozen by anticipation as she tries to decode her own logic.*
Mariah: "I swear, that annoying complex of yours is gonna smother you and everyone else."
*Mariah grabs another bottle and swings it straight down on the sharp corner of the table. The cap somersaults through the air. By the time it lands, she’s already chugged most of it.*
Amber: "But I used her…"
*That’s right. Obligations, second chances, complexes. None of that means anything, because it’s all said and done, I used Marina to make myself happy.*
Mariah: "I use those slackers all the time! That’s using!"
Mariah: "And ya know what? Who cares? That ditz used you to find the treasure, didn’t she?"
Mariah: "Hell, if anything, you probably don’t use her enough! I’d feel like a real jackass if some dumbass were coddling me all the time and all I could do was sit there."
Mariah: "People use people all the time. That’s what makes ‘em family."
*Mariah takes one last swig of her beer, then with a glint of sadness in her eyes, stares into the empty bottle.*
Mariah: "So if you’re gonna hate yourself, find a reason that doesn’t suck."
*Her face murks from red to a sickly green. The bottle falls from her hand and onto the floor as she rocks side-to-side, like a sailor that’s lost their sea legs.*
Amber: "Hey… You don’t look so good."
*For a quick moment, Mariah’s cheeks bloat out, then just as quickly, she swallows.*
Mariah: "I’m fine. Just need to drink it off."
*She wobbles back to the couch and collapses next to me.*
*Neither of us speak.*
*Mariah groans, holding her stomach as she feels around for the bottle opener.*
*I look at the pile of empty bottles building up on the table.*
Amber: "What happened to not caring?"
Mariah: "I don’t. I’d just prefer my treasure not being in the filthy hands of an idiot."
*Did I just get chewed out by Mariah?*
*Even worse, did some of it actually make sense, or is it just the booze?*
*Unable to spare the energy to lean forward, she stretches her arm way out for another beer, but I catch it just in time and grab ahold of her hand.*
Amber: "You need to quit. You’ve had way too much."
Mariah: "Eat me!"
*She slaps my hand away and snags two more beers, shoving one of them into my stomach.*
*Mariah rolls her eyes.*
Mariah: "Dumbass."
*Nothing she says burns anymore. Now it only stings with the acidity of truth.*
*Getting over it. Letting Marina in—really letting her in.*
*It’s just not that easy.*
*It’s never been that easy.*
*But if I don’t.*
*Putting my hand on my thigh, I squeeze the outline of the cassette.*
*Mariah raises out her beer and grunts. Sighing, I raise up mine and we clank them together.*
Amber: "Jerkass."
*Massaging the drunk away, I nurse my head as it pounds in rhythm with my heartbeat.*
*The table has become a stockpile of empty promises and broken dreams, foundationed beneath even emptier bottles.*
*Mariah is still at my side, slumped over and passed out.*
*She might be a super heavyweight, but I decided to stick around and keep an eye on her.*
*Soon, Joe and Tess will be back and they can take care of the rest.*
*I watch the slight movements of her chest as she breathes in and out.*
*Seeing her so quiet is eerie. It’s hard to believe there’s still a person underneath all that yelling and obsessiveness. But there she is.*
*‘People use other people all the time. That’s what makes them family.’*
*It sounds gross and awful, but those words have been echoing inside me since she said them.*
*I used Marina to cope. There’s no denying that. But Mariah said that Marina used me to find the treasure.*
*If that’s true, then why don’t I feel used? Why did I always feel genuinely wanted by her?*
*She relied on me. Is that using? That’s all I ever wanted her to feel like she could do. Her trust was the most important thing to me.*
*But no matter how much I wanted it, I knew I didn’t deserve it. Because I felt like I was betraying Gramps and would just drag her down with me.*
*What about her, though? Marina was furious when I left, but she wanted to understand, she wanted to help.*
*I wanted her to rely on me, but maybe she wanted me to rely on her too?*
*Reaching into my pocket, I pull out the cassette and hold it in the palm of my hand.*
*The sight of the ribbon all torn and tangled doesn’t choke me up or ignite a bitter explosion of anger like earlier.*
*All it does is serve as a numbing reminder of all my regrets.*
*Her wording was off, but Mariah was right. I never let myself rely on Marina.*
*From the very start, I always treated her like a kid and never as an equal.*
*No wonder she got upset. She wanted to do as much for me as I was doing for her, but I would never let her in long enough for that to happen.*
*Of course she wouldn’t say no if I made a move. It was the only opportunity she had.*
*If I go back as I am now, things will never get better. I’ll just keep treating Marina like a kid, never letting her in, and she’ll just smile and bear it until she explodes, like earlier.*
*I know what I have to do, and yet, it feels more like a betrayal thing than anything else I’ve done this far.*
*But if I don’t do it, then I can never truly hope to do right by Marina.*
*I stagger to my feet.*
*Turning around, I take one last look at Mariah before deciding to take off her bandana and lay her out on her side.*
*Then, I walk to the door, take a breath, and step outside.*
*The night sky is deep and clear, embedded with a rich blue draped behind the glow of the constellations.*
*Its light is all that guides me as I sludge my way through the thickness of the sand, wandering from Mariah’s RV with a destination of nowhere.*
*I’m alone. For the first time, I’m truly alone.*
*Free from the treasure, free from the festival, free from the responsibility. Free from even Marina.*
*Sitting down, I sink into the sand and look at the stars.*
Amber: "Remember that time in Wyoming, when we camped out on the Great Plains?"
Amber: "The whole ‘sleeping under the stars’ thing seemed like the coolest idea ever to little me."
Amber: "You were so excited to teach me about the constellations. Didn’t matter that you sucked at astronomy, you just kept mislabeling them."
Amber: "I made sure to read up on them later, by the way. That was the Big Dipper, not ‘Howe’s Guitar.’"
Amber: "But that’s okay, I still got a kick out of it."
Amber: "Besides, that’s not the type of thing I expected to learn from you anyway."
Amber: "What I really remember from that trip was the s’mores."
Amber: "Pretty sure you only liked them burned because you were so old. Really stuck with me, though. Can’t have s’mores any other way now."
Amber: "I think I may have converted someone else to the burned side, too. She has a huge sweet tooth though, so I shouldn’t be too surprised."
Amber: "Still, it made me really happy when she said she liked it."
*I stare down and pet the cassette, feeling my thumbs over its holes and brown blotches and stains.*
Amber: "I think you’d like her a lot."
Amber: "She’s a sweetheart, always cheery and nice, even when she really shouldn’t. Don’t know how she does it, but she can somehow deal with me and my moods."
Amber: "This is her first time ever leaving home and it shows."
Amber: "I worry about her a lot."
Amber: "Kinda like how I worried about you."
Amber: "Kinda like how I still do."
*A tear drips onto the cassette. I didn’t even notice it trail down my cheek, or the rest.*
Amber: "Maybe I got attached too easily and really did use her, but I don’t want to leave. I love her so much."
Amber: "And I want to prove I’m worth loving too."
Amber: "Would you hate me?"
Amber: "Would I be letting you down if I decided to be happy instead?"
*I gaze back up at the sky through my blurry vision. The stars don’t answer back, they just keep twinkling.*
*I lower my head. The cassette feels like lifeless dead weight in my palm.*
*My breath shakes and the next words slip out in a whimper.*
Amber: "I’m sorry."
*I dig my fingers into the ground, scraping away the sand as sharp grains nudge between my nails.*
*A shallow hole forms, a pile of dirt laying next to it.*
*I can barely stomach looking at the cassette as I lay it down in its grave. I glance away as I slide the dirt back over the hole.*
*When I look back, a corner of it still sticks out, only partially buried. It’s reaching out, grasping for me.*
*I cover my eyes and pinch away the tears. It’s still there, it will always be there, but now I don’t have to acknowledge it.*
Amber: "I’m sorry."
Amber: "I’m sorry…"
*Drenched in sweat, I gasp to life and hack the sand out of my mouth.*
*Memories from the night before start flooding back as I choke down a lump in my throat.*
*The desert, the tape, the stars—*
Amber: "Marina!"
*My head snaps to the sky. The sun is already a quarter of a way done with its cycle.*
*Soon, if it already hasn’t, the taxi will pick up Marina and I’ll never see her again.*
*All because I couldn’t let myself trust Marina.*
Amber: "Dammit!"
*I slam my fists down, kicking up a pitiful plume of sand.*
*Even if I ran, I’d never be able to make it in time.*
*If only I had the motorhome.*
*Wait…*
Amber: "Mariah!"
*There’s a chance they still might be camped out nearby.*
*Wiping the tears from my eyes, I spring to my feet and sprint in the direction of their motorhome, leaving the half-buried cassette behind me.*
*Please still be there, please still be there.*
*Sure enough, it’s right where it was last night.*
*I run up and pound on the door, knocking harder and louder until someone answers.*
*Looking up with wide eyes, Tess opens the door and stares at me.*
Amber: "Tess! Is Mariah here?"
Joseph: "Amber?"
*Joe slinks off the couch and joins Tess at the door.*
Joseph: "Sheesh, you’re a wreck. Everything okay?"
Amber: "Quick, the time!"
Joseph: "Eh?"
Amber: "The time! What time is it?"
Joseph: "Uh…"
*He looks to the invisible watch on his wrist, then switches between all the walls before Tess digs around in her shorts and hands him a chained pocket watch.*
Joseph: "Ten o'clock, apparently."
*He turns to Tess.*
Joseph: "When did you start carrying this around?"
Mariah: "WHY THE HELL’S IT SO FRIGGIN’ LOUD?" with hpunch
Joseph: "Oh yeah. She smashed the last one."
Amber: "Fifteen minutes! There’s still time!"
Joseph: "Woah, slow down a sec. Time for what?"
Amber: "I know this is short notice, but I need a ride!"
Joseph: "A ride? Now?"
Mariah: "I SAID SHUT THE HELL UP!" with hpunch
*Another bottle comes flying towards him, but this time Tess hops and snatches it out of the air.*
*He looks across the room then back to me.*
Joseph: "Now’s probably not the best time."
Tess: "Sis’ hangovers are lethal."
Amber: "No! We need to leave now!"
Mariah: "DO YOU NOT REMEMBER WHAT I SAID I’D DO WITH THE JUMPER CABLES THE NEXT TIME YOU—" with hpunch
*Nursing her forehead and lifting up another bottle, Mariah stomps over, only to freeze once she sees me.*
Amber: "Mariah! I need a ride!"
Amber: "Please! You were right! I made a big mistake and if I’m not back at our hotel in fifteen I’ll never see Marina again!"
*The motorhome falls silent, all eyes on Mariah.*
*Her face is blank, unreadable compared to all the crazy looking scowls she usually wears.*
*Finally, she speaks.*
Mariah: "Alright. Let her in."
Joseph: "I’m really sorry, but you know how she—wait."
Joseph: "For real?"
*Mariah closes her eyes and takes a deep, long breath.*
Mariah: "Before I change my mind."
*Joe and Tess shoot each other surprised glances, then shrug.*
Joseph: "Well what do you know? Miracles actually do happen."
Tess: "The desert heat has thawed her icy heart."
Mariah: "Yeah, yeah. Just shut up and get ready."
*I hop in. The other two scurry around and batten down the hatches, strapping shut all the cupboards and shelves.*
*Grabbing her bandanna, Mariah ties it around her head while I shuffle around and try to spit out a thank you.*
Amber: "So, uh, I should probab—"
Joseph: "We’re all set! I’ll take the wheel!"
*Joe rushes past us with the keys, but Mariah trips him and snags them from his hand.*
Mariah: "No way! You drive like an old man. I’ll drive."
Joseph: "No, I drive like a normal person. You just happen to drive like a maniac. Besides, you’re hungover!"
*They both dart over to the front seats and the RV roars to life.*
Mariah: "Tess! Hair of the dog!"
*Tess nods and runs to the fridge, grabbing a bottle that looks a lot like the ones from last night, except this one has a picture of the devil on it.*
*She tosses it over to her sister, who showcases the same catching talent as her.*
Mariah: "Now that’s what I’m talking about!"
*Slamming the bottle in the cup holder, Mariah sticks her head out the window and backs up the motorhome.*
*Joe shakes his head, replacing the devil’s brew with a nearby water bottle while she isn’t looking.*
Joseph: "You’re going to need to hang on tight, by the way."
Amber: "Huh? Why?"
*My question is immediately answered, as I’m flung against the couch. Tess’ small frame crashes into me.*
Tess: "It doesn’t hurt as much if you relax your muscles."
*The RV speeds forward. The license plate wind chime dings in chaotic disunion as the bottles and cans roll across the floor, clanging together whenever they meet.*
*Even the table is seesawing back and forth.*
*I look out the window. Mariah flies onto the road, cutting off an entire lane of cars.*
*The RV hits the ground and bounces. I manage to keep my head from bashing the cot above me, but Tess lets herself get tossed around, rolling across the floor with the trash." with vpunch
Mariah: "So where exactly am I going?"
Amber: "The hotel downtown! You’d have to be stupid to miss it!"
Joseph: "Remember who you’re talking to here."
Mariah: "Hotel? You didn’t spend any of my treasure on that, did you?"
*Mariah glares back at me, oblivious to everything on the other side of the windshield.*
Amber: "I might’ve used a nugget to bribe the manager…"
Mariah: "WHAT? I’ll kill you!" with hpunch
Joseph: "Save the cat fight for later! There are more important things to focus on right now—like the road!"
Mariah: "The road will always be there, Joe! My treasure won’t!"
Joseph: "The treasure or our lives?"
*I think everyone here already knows the answer to that one.*
Amber: "Watch out!"
*A big rig comes speeding towards us, blasting its deafening horn." with hpunch
*At the last second, Mariah swerves back into her lane as the truck barrels past us.*
*The motorhome hits a bump and bounces up again.*
*Unprepared, poor Joe’s head hits the ceiling, knocking the light from his eyes. If I squint hard enough I can even see his soul drifting away from his body.*
Tess: "Sis, I think Joey’s dead."
Mariah: "About damn time! He was getting annoying!"
*Tess salutes her fallen comrade.*
*It’s always the sane ones who die first.*
*We zoom past the town’s welcoming center. It’s not far at all now.*
Amber: "Tess, what time is it?"
Tess: "10:12."
*I grit my teeth and glare out the window.*
*Please still be there.*
*Downtown comes into view, the strip of familiar stores and restaurants start forming.*
*Mariah races down the street, ignoring all traffic lights and pedestrians as people leap out of the way and cars brake into other lanes.*
Amber: "We’re almost there! Get ready!"
*The hotel looms just up ahead. Through the windshield I can see Marina holding on to a small suitcase as she prepares to step into the cab.*
*I leap to the door and swing it open. Wind rushes through my hair. The motorhome is slowing down, but I don’t care.*
*I launch myself onto the street, tumbling across the burning concrete as the road tears against my skin.*
*Immediately, I spring back up and dart towards Marina.*
*Mariah screeches to a halt in the middle of the street, blocking anyone from driving through. The three of them hop out, Joe clutching his head as he stumbles out the door.*
Marina: "A-Amber?"
*Before she can get another word, I grab her by the waist and throw my lips against hers.*
*She pulls herself away and glares at me through her puffy, red eyes.*
*I plant my hands on her shoulders and steady myself as tears stream down my face.*
Amber: "I’m sorry, I’m so sorry."
Marina: "I thought you weren’t coming back…"
Amber: "I know, I know. I was such a bitch."
Amber: "I didn’t mean any of it. I love you so much, I just didn’t want to hurt you."
Marina: "Wh-What? How could you ever hurt me?"
Amber: "I was scared…"
*The tears don’t stop. The crying gets louder.*
Amber: "I was afraid I was using you because I didn’t have Gramps anymore, I lost my family."
Amber: "I always wanted to help you, but I think I might be the one who really needs help."
*Marina can’t hold it back any longer and starts bawling in my arms, hugging me as tight as she possibly can.*
Marina: "Dummy, I’m your new family! I don’t care if you use me, I love you!"
Amber: "I love you, too!"
*This time we both move in for the kiss, two blubbering idiots trying to prove just how much the other means.*
*I’m not sure if it’s her tears I’m tasting or mine, but I can still make out the soft sweetness of her lips regardless.*
*It’s funny.*
*This entire time, I thought I was the nucleus of it all.*
*That I was the one helping her—or using her.*
*But as cheesy as it sounds, I think Marina has always been the one helping me. From the very beginning.*
*Through the kissing, I catch a glimpse of the trio, Joe covering Tess’ eyes as he slips Mariah a bill.*
*We manage to compose ourselves enough to pull each other away, but we never let go of the other’s hand.*
*Rolling her eyes, Mariah unties her bandanna and tosses it to me.*
Mariah: "You both look disgusting. Here."
Amber: "Heh. Thanks."
*I wipe away as many of my tears as I can before passing it along to Marina, who blows into it like a trumpet." with hpunch
*She sniffs, her nose red and tears still leaking out, then hands the drenched cloth back to Mariah, who gives it to Joe.*
*He cringes as he dangles it from his fingers.*
Marina: "Hi, guys."
Amber: "Thanks, you three. I mean it. Especially you, Mariah."
*For a moment, Mariah lowers her guard and almost smirks, then remembers it’s just not her style.*
Mariah: "Whatever. That doesn’t make us cool or anything. I still despise you."
Tess: "It’s true."
Joseph: "Yeah, she hates your guts."
Amber: "Wouldn’t have it any other way."
*Marina giggles and I bring her in closer, then suddenly, I remember something.*
Amber: "Oh!"
*I bend over and dig through Marina’s suitcase, bringing out a single gold nugget and passing it to Mariah.*
Amber: "Figured yours was getting lonely."
*Mariah stares at her hand, the other two gathering around her.*
Joseph: "How about that? You ended up getting the treasure after all, Mariah!"
Tess: "It seems bigger than the one we have."
*Finally defeated, Mariah looks up and sighs.*
Mariah: "I guess we can probably pawn it off somewhere."
*She walks back to her motorhome, waving along the other two.*
Mariah: "C’mon. Let’s go."
Joseph: "Well, see you two ladies down the road."
Mariah: "God, I hope not."
*Tess looks back and forth, then scurries over to Marina and gives a quick hug around her waist before following the other two.*
Tess: "Bye."
*And with that, they speed off, leaving a trail of property damage behind them as they beep their loud-ass horn into the distance.*
*Marina and I are left alone, the cab long gone.*
*Between small spurts of laughter, we thumb away each other’s tears, just happy to be together again.*
Amber: "Hey, let’s go pick up the motorhome."
Marina: "Yeah."
*She sniffs one last time.*
Marina: "I’d like that."
*Fading light from the sunset wakes me up.*
*After reuniting, the first thing Marina and I did was take a nap.*
*Now, hours later, we're still in the same spot. One of Marina's hands clings to my shirt, while she's pressed up against my side.*
*Her breath, shallow and steady, is warm against my neck.*
*I feel like I could stay here all day. Or for a week. A year. Whatever. Just enjoying this closeness.*
*Marina sighs in her sleep, shifting a little but not moving too far away. I watch her, a small smile dancing on my lips.*
*No way we’re gonna be able to sleep tonight after taking naps this late. We can probably head out and get to the festival site a bit early.*
*Beat the lines to get in and maybe end up closer to the stage.*
*I’ll see how she feels when she gets up.*
*Gently, I extract myself from her grip and climb out of bed.*
*To kill some time, I wander to the back to take stock of our supplies.*
*We’re running low on bottled water, and could use some more snacks. The healthy kind, not the Marina-selected, tooth-rotting kind.*
*Also, we need to stop by a laundromat sometime, too. Hell, we should probably go to a clothing store and pick up a few more changes of clothes for Marina.*
*Since we’re gonna be sticking together and all.*
*That thought makes me smile, so happy that I couldn’t stop even if I wanted to.*
*I don’t have to be lonely anymore. I can make these plans for two.*
*When I walk back by the bed, still being careful not to disturb Marina, I notice something weird.*
*It’s gotten brighter outside.*
*No way.*
*It dawns on me, quite literally:*
*We didn’t just sleep through the day.*
*We slept all the way through the night and into the next morning.*
Amber: "Marina!"
*I shout her name as I lunge across the bed to shake her shoulder.*
*Despite my frenzy, she opens her eyes lazily.*
Marina: "Amber?"
Marina: "What’s wrong?"
Amber: "We slept way too late. We’ve gotta get going fast. The festival’s starting in just a few hours."
*Marina pats my head like I’m a naive kid.*
Marina: "Amber, it’s okay, that’s not until tomorrow."
Amber: "It is tomorrow! We slept all night."
*Now her eyes get a bit bigger, and right on time the sunlight falls across her face.*
Marina: "Huh?"
Amber: "Yeah."
*I move back, letting her sit up.*
Amber: "We’ve gotta go, or we’ll be standing in line all day."
*She smooshes her fists into her eyes and rolls out of bed.*
Marina: "I’m up, I’m up."
Marina: "Can you make some coffee, please?"
Amber: "No prob."
Marina: "Gonna get ready."
*I nod. Marina zombie-shuffles off to the bathroom to brush her teeth and fix her hair. I set about preparing.*
*Soon, the warm smell of brewing coffee fills the air, as does the sound of running water. Both Marina and the coffee finish around the same time.*
*Marina skips over to where I am and leans into my shoulder, while I wrap an arm around her.*
Marina: "Sorry, I shouldn’t hold us up, huh?"
Amber: "Nah, this is okay."
*Just for a moment.*
*Then I pull away and grab a pair of mugs, passing one to Marina. I fill mine up with steaming black coffee, then hand her the creamer and sugar.*
*But Marina shakes her head.*
Marina: "I’ll try it how you drink it."
*Eyebrows raised, I pour her a cup.*
Amber: "You sure? It’s a super acquired taste."
*Marina takes a sip and scrunches up her face in disgust.*
Marina: "It’s good!"
Amber: "Uh huh."
Marina: "No, really!"
*She chokes on her next sip.*
Amber: "You don’t have to drink it black just because I do, you know."
Marina: "You said it’s an acquired taste though, right? So I wanna acquire that taste."
Amber: "Right."
*By the time I’ve finished my first cup, she’s only had a quarter of hers.*
*She gets halfway through before she gives up and dumps in some sugar.*
Marina: "I’ll acquire it later."
Amber: "Of course."
*While she preps her coffee, I go over to the cabinet where we stash the gold. Aside from those, there’s one other small, brightly decorated box.*
*I take it out and set it on the counter. Marina instantly hops over.*
Marina: "Ooh, what’s that?"
*Inside are two wristbands made of soft fabric. They’re our entrance passes to the festival.*
*I pass one to Marina.*
*The tenderness with which she takes it reminds me of when she first held the gold. Like if it fell, it would break.*
Amber: "Make sure you don’t lose it, or you’ll have to wait in the RV while I go watch."
Marina: "You wouldn’t!"
*I think about it for a second.*
*Then I answer, truthfully.*
Amber: "Okay, you’re right."
Amber: "But still."

*There’s a lump in my throat, over how it was always supposed to be me and Gramps who went.*

Amber: "You ready?"

*Once we get past, I can finally openly accelerate. The new and improved engine pulls its weight; everything is much smoother and quieter.*
*As we get closer and closer, our anticipation builds. It’s easy to read Marina, since she’s bouncing and fidgeting in her seat.*
*I’m more composed, but I feel it too.*
*We glance at each other, at the exact same time, and grin. We’ve been doing that a lot lately.*

*I take the exit and join yet another long line of cars heading for the parking area and campground.*
*Guests carrying coolers, chairs and camping supplies crowd the edges of the unpaved roads, making us slow down.*
*Finally, we find a spot and I kill the engine.*
*Marina looks at me, waiting for me to say something.*
*But I’m all out of cheesy speeches. So I just grab her hand, and we both stand up.*
Amber: "Let’s go."
*The air seems to crackle with electric anticipation. It’s on people’s faces and in the way that they move, the energy like a wave that flows over them.*
*We all share a feeling of excitement and of unity. Every single person here has been waiting for months for this day to arrive.*
*And it’s finally here.*
*High tents and portable stages dominate the space around us. Between those and the trees, and most of all the noise, you’d never guess that there’s a city and highway just a couple miles away.*
*Drum beats and muted chords float by as musicians do a few last-minute soundchecks. They’re barely audible over the sound of thousands of chattering voices.*
*Folks of all different shapes, sizes and colors mill around us. It’s like social boundaries have been eroded. I think that’s a sentiment Gramps would like.*
*Idly, I use my thumb to trace a heart into Marina’s palm. She squeezes back, probably unaware of what the shape was but understanding all the same.*
Marina: "So where do we go now?"
Amber: "Now we go stand in line while they check wristbands and let people onto the grounds. It’s gonna be a while."
Marina: "One more traffic jam, huh?"
Amber: "That’s right. One more."
*Even if I hadn’t been mapping the grounds in my head, it’d be impossible to get lost. Bored looking staff are positioned every few yards, holding signs with arrows and directions.*
*Like molasses, the throng of people that we’ve joined oozes down the trail. We all hold each other back, the sheer numbers making for slow going.*
*Nearly a hundred thousand people attend each year.*
Marina: "What time does the show start?"
Amber: "First bands are at like, 11. But the ones we wanna see are later in the evening."
Marina: "What time is it now?"

Amber: "All of it!"

Amber: "Yeah, you get all types."

*When I look back at Marina, she’s not paying any attention to me, just grinning up at the stage.*

Amber: "How’d you like it?"

*That’s how we spend the next few hours: going from stage to stage to watch bands I’ve never heard of. Sometimes they’re groups Marina likes, and sometimes they just have a weird name.*
*Around the time the sun starts to go down, we head back to the east stage where we’ll see our last show of the day.*
*Marina’s in the lead this time. We’ve gone between the stages so many times that we have a good feel for how to get around.*

Marina: "Are you sure? Won’t it be hard to see?"

Marina: "Excited?"
Amber: "Bit of an understatement."

*With just a wave and not a word of introduction, they launch into the first song. It was the opening track on their second album, back from before I was born.*
*They’ve been playing this one longer than I’ve been alive.*
*It’s a great song, though. They’re all great songs.*
*The whole set is magical. They go from one song to another without stopping in-between. If you didn’t know the music, you’d probably think it was all just one, super long track.*
*Even though it spans a few decades’ worth of albums, everything flows seamlessly, like it was meant to be heard this way.*
*At some point I close my eyes, though I don’t notice myself doing it. I’m lost, but in the good way. With Marina beside me, gently swaying.*
*There’s not a single sad thought in my head.*
*For forty minutes, everything in the world seems right.*
*I marvel at the odds. That my life could turn around entirely in such a short amount of time.*
*All that, and one faulty fuel gauge.*
*I open my eyes and look at Marina.*
*To my surprise, she’s looking right back at me.*
*She scoots a little bit closer and leans against me, her head on my shoulder. I put my head on hers, and together we rock in time to the music.*
*Then it stops. Everyone cheers again.*
*And the frontman holds up a finger. It’s the first break there’s been in the whole set.*
*Then he plays those guitar strums I had come to know so well, the ones that I was both dreading and looking forward to the most. It’s the most familiar intro in the world to me, and of course it’s their swan song.*
*Gramps’ and my favorite.*
*I can feel Marina’s worried gaze, wondering like always if I’m about to break down and cry.*
*But this time I meet her look, and I smile as I press my lips against hers.*
*I’m with the girl I love, seeing my favorite band live, as they perform my favorite song. What’s not to smile about?*
*Somewhere, I hope that Gramps is smiling too. If there’s an afterlife, I hope they get free tickets to the festival.*
*A single, bittersweet tear leaks out of my eye, rolling down my face and onto our lips.*
*I picture Gramps here beside me, not replacing Marina but with us. I know he would’ve liked her.*
*As the song comes to an end, I mouth just one word.*
*"Goodbye."*
*And then it’s over. I feel something change; not a physical motion but an emotional one. Something new; something deep inside me feels… right.*
*The band bows. People are screaming their affections. It’s over. We saw them. They performed. It’s over.*
*Beside me, Marina shifts to look to me with a coy look on her face.*

Amber: "Yeah, sure."
Amber: "We need to give ourselves ample room for sequels."
*The doors have an outline and handles that are the same lime green as the sign. When I step inside, I see that the walls are too.*
*I’m reminded of the slime from those old shows I used to watch on TV, that would be sprayed onto the audience or cast members.*
*Man, being a 90s kid is the best.*
Amber: "Can you please explain?"
Joseph: "Sure."
Joseph: "Mare ended up making Marina a bet for all the gold, which she lost. It was a pretty silly thing, and I told her to just let it go, but here we are."
*I’m equal parts amused and disgusted that Joseph uses the same nickname for Mariah that I do for Marina. Come to think of it, I’m not sure that I ever noticed how similar their names are.*
*Honestly, if we didn’t color their names differently in the text box, I’m pretty sure we’d lose track of who was talking.*
*She spreads her arms far apart and scrunches her face down, giving herself a double chin.*
Marina: "I wanna try every single buffet in town!"
Amber: "That’s a lofty goal, Marina."
Marina: "But we’re rich now!"
*I thump her head and squish her down with the palm of my hand.*
Amber: "And we almost lost all of that in a single day, so you should know how easy it is to blow it."
Amber: "That's why you should instead put the gold in a 401(k), to help you have a guaranteed future."
Marina: "You really know how to make optimism look like a bad idea."
Marina: "Amber."
Amber: "Marina."
Marina: "Is that like… a typo or something? It says they have deep-fried candy bars."
Amber: "Nah, those are really popular at fairs and festivals."
*She gives me that "I know you’re making this up" look that she’s perfected.*
Marina: "Yeah, right."
Amber: "No, I’m actually dead serious. Candy bars, pickles, people… You can get almost anything deep fried at places like this."
Amber: "One guy I met figured out how to deep fry the concept of objective truth."
Marina: "What was that like?"
Amber: "Technically unknowable depending on who you talk to."
Marina: "Amber?"
Amber: "Yeah?"
Marina: "I think... I think I know what I want to do with the treasure now."
Amber: "Buy cell phones?"
Marina: "No, that would make too much sense."
Amber: "Yeah, let's just keep doing this instead."
